Each PR branches from `main` after its dependencies have merged. No stacked PRs.

Lessons from Phase 1 that shape this plan:
- Every PR ended at 390-440 changed lines once review fixes were in. The estimates below are
  first-commit sizes counted from each PR's listed contents (code after `gdformat`, which wraps
  every long list one name per line, plus 15-25 lines per test case and helpers). The rule:
  every PR estimated at 330 or more names a split line, and no estimate is above 400, except the
  rows whose size says the named split is the expected outcome (P32, P38w, P50a), which split
  before they open. If a first commit passes 400 lines, it is split along its named line before
  it opens, never after review. Declaration-heavy PRs (World v1) are counted after `gdformat`,
  not before.
  **The PR-size rule** (P26n docs, 2026-10-14): Michael decided in chat on 2026-10-14, verbatim
  **"ja til PR-reglen"**, and `CLAUDE.md`'s Workflow line now reads "Small PRs (under ~400
  changed lines of production code; tests and fixtures don't count), one concern each, with
  tests." From now on the 400 above and every split trigger count production lines only: a first
  commit is split along its named line when its production lines pass 400, whatever its tests
  and fixtures add. The ~Lines estimates in the tables still count tests, as written; they are
  not re-estimated here.
- The plan PR (P26) is docs and is exempt from the ~400-line code rule, as the Phase 0 and
  Phase 1 plan PRs were. It carries this plan (about 1,770 lines), ADRs 0007-0011 (about 1,290),
  the `rts-sim-conventions` edits, the status lines in ADRs 0003 and 0004, the box 1 tick and the
  architect memory files.
- All PRs code against one contract table, including a column table for the World.
- File ownership is disjoint. A cross-owner change goes in a "Requests" section, except the few
  named one-line exceptions below, which would otherwise leave `main` red.
- At most two lanes run at once. (P26q docs, 2026-10-14: this limit is superseded; how many
  lanes run at once is Michael's call through his start commands, three at a time in practice
  since P33 (lead decision, "Answers and follow-ups (P26q docs)").)
- Each PR names its phone check, or says none.
- Michael approves merges with "merge Px" (a batch such as "merge P27a-P29b" is fine, Phase 1
  answer 9).
- Tests pin every contract number literally, never derived from the same `.tres` (P1 review).
- Performance is measured from the first week (probe on the phone, harness in CI), not at the
  end.

## Goal
Every Phase 2 box in `docs/ROADMAP.md` is met on `main`:
- The sim runs at 15 Hz with World v1, Command v1, DetRandom, a state hash every second and a
  replay test that reproduces the final hash.
- Units path around rock and water with flow fields, and do not overlap (separation).
- Three unit types (light tank, rifle squad, rocket squad) fight with hitscan and projectiles.
  Damage depends on armour class, and dead units are removed.
- Fog of war (visible and explored) is drawn on the map and hides unseen enemies.
- The view interpolates units and projectiles at the display frame rate.
- `tests/perf/` runs 150 units for 900 ticks, prints ms per tick and fails above
  `tests/perf/budget.cfg` or above the deterministic cost bounds.

Then Michael runs the gate (see "Gate procedure"):
- the dev scene "Perf arena" in the release-template perf APK (P51r), with the sim on the worker
  thread, shows `GATE: PASS` on the gate phone (Michael's own phone, with the divided limits of
  answer 1 as ADR 0012 §4 restates them) three runs in a row, with every unit on screen;
- its checkpoint hashes (APK and web) match the golden hashes that CI checks for the same
  commit.

Accepting ADRs 0007-0011 also makes the lead update `rts-sim-conventions` in P26 (list under
P26), because the skill must match the code agents write.

Out of scope (each needs a plan change first): buildings, production, economy, heavy tank,
artillery, harvester, splash damage, AI (including `PlayerView`, reserved by ADR 0010), minimap,
the "under attack" edge indicator and minimap ping (Phase 3 HUD, together with the minimap), a
gesture for moving without fighting (Phase 3 plan agenda), rig pooling (Phase 3, with
production), sound, info card, save and resume, writing replays to disk, wiring
`run-sim-smoke.sh` (Phase 4 box; it stays "no sim yet", and the determinism and perf suites stand
in for it in Phase 2), an always-on enemy marker (not built, Michael 2026-10-12; Phase 5 polish
revisits the colour-blind cue if playtesters have trouble, answer 3), a second, slower test phone
(only through a new ADR if field data demands it, answer 1), C++ outside `native/sim_kernels/` or
outside the phases of ADR 0013 §1 (revision 15; later ports follow ADR 0013 §1 and §15), web on
C++ (the dlink template, ADR 0013 §7 option B: a P26w spike and a new ADR after the gate) and
iOS builds, phases of one tick run in parallel.

## Command and data changes
- **Command v1** (ADR 0007 §5):
  - adds `MOVE` (2), `ATTACK` (3, `target_id`), `STOP` (4) and `payload`;
  - reserves 10-19 for Phase 3 economy and production, and 20-29 for match-level commands;
  - validation is per type (`match command.type`); the group rules apply only to the four unit
    types, and an unknown or reserved type is dropped as unknown;
  - `ATTACK` needs a visible enemy from P46b on;
  - `encode`/`decode` for replays.

  Humans issue `ATTACK_MOVE` (ground tap) and `ATTACK` (enemy tap). `MOVE` and `STOP` have no UI
  in Phase 2: they exist for tests now and for the Phase 4 AI. Players therefore cannot
  disengage in Phase 2 (a known limitation, recorded in HOW_TO_PLAY).
- **Scenario orders** (`ScenarioDef.orders`) are turned into ordinary Commands by
  `ScenarioOrders` and stamped by `MatchLoop`, so replays and the hash see them like any order.
- **New and changed data** (every number below is the ADRs' starting value, pinned literally by
  tests):

## Interface contracts (all PRs code against these)
Every `class_name` script is PascalCase, everything else snake_case (ADR 0002 §2). "milli" means
milli-tiles, a "cell" is a tile index `(y / 1000) * map_cols + x / 1000`, and "row" is an index
into the per-unit arrays (ascending id). All sim classes `extends RefCounted` unless noted.
`local_player` in every view, input and UI contract below (`UnitLayer`, `FogLayer`,
`HealthBarLayer`, `ProjectileLayer`, `EffectsLayer`, `PickSnapshot`) means
`runner.scenario.local_player`, read the way Phase 1's `PlayerController._local_player()` reads
it.

| Item | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| Numeric and coding rules | ADR 0007 §1: no int64 overflow, shifts of non-negative values by 0-62 only and parenthesised next to `+`/`-`, `PackedInt32Array` holds only int32-range values (never a squared distance or an unsigned 32-bit word), no `hash()`. In per-row, per-candidate and per-cell loops: no allocation (lint with `# alloc-ok` exceptions), World arrays aliased into typed locals before the loop, cell, passability and visibility lookups inlined. Helper methods are for code outside loops |
| `Sim` | `Sim.step(world: World, commands: Array[Command], probe: SimProbe = null) -> void` runs the ADR 0007 §4 pipeline: `world.prepare()` → clear events and stats → commands → `FlowCache.run` (revision 15; `FlowCache.advance` before ADR 0013) → grids → `Targeting.run` → `Movement.run` (intents) → `Separation.run` → `Movement.integrate` → `Combat.advance_projectiles` → `Combat.fire` → `world.remove_dead()` → `FogUpdate.run` → `tick += 1`. When a probe is given, `probe.begin()` comes first and `probe.mark(SimProbe.Phase.X)` follows each phase (ids below). Each phase module is a `class_name` with static functions taking `world`. A PR that adds a phase adds exactly one call line and its `mark` line. The one exception is `GRIDS`, which holds one rebuild line per grid: P39b adds `world.sep_grid.rebuild(world.pos_x, world.pos_y, n)` with the `GRIDS` mark, and P41 adds the `target_grid` line above that mark. No other code rebuilds a grid (`Separation.run` and `Targeting.run` only read them) **Revision 15 (ADR 0013 §3):** the FLOW line is `FlowCache.run(world, budget)` (static: the recount, then `world.flow.advance`; a budget of 0 on checkpoint ticks). (P26o docs, 2026-10-14, ADR 0012 §5 status note: `Sim.step` counts the tick last (`tick += 1` after every phase; the FLOW line P37b adds comes before it, ADR 0007 §4), and `MatchLoop` hashes after it on the new tick, so inside `Sim.step` the checkpoint tick is `world.tick + 1`: the budget is 0 when `(world.tick + 1) % Replay.CHECKPOINT_PERIOD == 0`.) From each port PR on, the phase's entry starts with the dispatch `if ((world.native_phases >> SimProbe.Phase.X) & 1) == 1: world.kernels.x(world[, int]); return` (FLOW in `FlowCache.run`, the others in `Targeting.run`, `Separation.run`, `Movement.integrate`, `FogUpdate.run`, and `Movement.run`, `Combat.advance_projectiles`, `Combat.fire` only if their conditional rows fire). P31a's helper sizing and P32's fog blocks code against this `player_count` and `max_rows`), `prepare() -> void` (idempotent; P29a: pads every `TYPE_ARRAYS` table to `type_ids.size()` with the ADR 0007 §2 defaults: max hp 1, `INFANTRY`, `TRACKED`, sight 0, acquire 0, weapon -1, name = the capitalized type id, 0 for v0 tables; P29a2: sizes the scratch arrays to `max_rows`, `stats` to `Stat` count and the projectile slots to `max_projectiles` with the free stack filled in descending order; P29b: derives an open-grass grid from `map_w`/`map_h` (rounded up to whole tiles) when no map is loaded: terrain 0 on every cell, cost 10 for every class, `terrain_cost` one terrain of 10s and `terrain_ids = [&"grass"]` (`const World.GRASS_ID`, pinned against terrain 0 of `data/terrain/standard.tres`; P29c), and a size of 0 or less on either axis gives no map (`map_cols` and `map_rows` both 0); `MapView` (P36) and `MatchSnapshot.terrain_ids` rely on this; P31a: configures the helpers), P29b: `cell_of(x: int, y: int) -> int` (clamps into the map: column `clampi(x / 1000, 0, map_cols - 1)`, row `clampi(y / 1000, 0, map_rows - 1)`, so a position outside the map gives the nearest edge cell, never an index outside the map; 0 when `map_cols == 0` (no map); `MatchSnapshot.cell_of` uses the same formula and the same early return), `is_passable(cell: int, move_class: int) -> bool` (true with no map), `set_terrain(cell: int, terrain_index: int) -> void` (`map_version += 1`; from P31a, once the World is prepared, it then calls `flow.invalidate(map_version)`: a decision of the lead, P26i, 2026-10-13, see "Lead decisions and answers (P26i)"). Each hash block is `[tag, byte count]` as two little-endian int64, then the bytes (`_feed`; the tag is the section index); the projectiles (the live slots, then the used free stack) and the fog (`FogGrid.HASHED`: `visible`, `explored`) feed two blocks each under one tag; an empty block feeds only its 16-byte prefix, because `HashingContext.update` rejects an empty chunk. | `FixedMath` | Adds (P37a) `static func step_toward(x: int, y: int, tx: int, ty: int, speed: int) -> Vector2i` (ADR 0003 §5 truncation, snap when `d2 <= speed^2`) and `static func line_clear(world: World, x0: int, y0: int, x1: int, y1: int, move_class: int) -> bool` (supercover). `isqrt` may get a faster body with identical results. No sine table or `dir_to` in Phase 2 (the header line changes in P37a). **P37a as merged** (#86; P26p docs, 2026-10-14; ADR 0008 §1/§2 status note): `isqrt` is Newton's method from above ("a power of two at or over the root, found from the bit length of n, then x = (x + n / x) / 2 until it no longer falls, which ends on the floor"), about 3 times faster (0.37 µs instead of 1.15 µs in #86) with identical results (the exhaustive tests unchanged). `line_clear` reads a point in the tile `World.cell_of` gives it: a tile is half-open ("its left and top edge belong to it, its right and bottom edge to the next tile, and the far edge of the map to the last tile"); the segment touches the tiles of its points, and where it crosses a tile corner strictly between its ends (and is along no axis) also the two tiles beside the corner (the flow field's rule against corner cutting); a segment along a tile edge touches the tiles on its right or lower side only. Points are clamped into the grid; true while no map is loaded (`map_cols == 0`), false for a class outside `World.MoveClass`; only a cost of 0 blocks |
| `FlowField`, `FlowCache` | `game/sim/FlowField.gd` (P30a): `const UNREACHED: int = 0x3FFFFFFF`, `const BUCKETS: int = 512`, `const STATE_ARRAYS: Array[StringName]` (the six Packed arrays `advance` reads or writes: `cost`, `next`, `pad_cost`, `bucket_head`, `link_next`, `link_prev`), `var goal_cell: int`, `var move_class: int`, `var complete: bool`, `var cost: PackedInt32Array` (served), `var next: PackedInt32Array` (being integrated; swapped into `cost` on completion), `var pad_cost: PackedByteArray` (the class's terrain cost per padded cell, 0 = blocked, the ring too), `var bucket_head: PackedInt32Array` (`BUCKETS` entries, -1 = empty bucket), `var link_next: PackedInt32Array`, `var link_prev: PackedInt32Array` (the doubly linked bucket lists), the int members `grid_cols`, `cell_count`, `stride`, `level` and `pending`, `func reserve(cols: int, rows: int) -> void` (sizes `cost`, `next`, `pad_cost`, `link_next` and `link_prev` to the padded size and `bucket_head` to its 512 entries, once: a no-op while the padded size is unchanged; `begin()` calls it), `func begin(goal_cell: int, move_class: int, costs: PackedByteArray, cols: int, rows: int) -> void`, `func advance(budget_cells: int) -> int` (cells settled), `func best_neighbour(cell: int) -> int` (-1 at the goal or when unreached). **Rules of the merged P30a** (P26h, 2026-10-13, P30a review): **the step rule**: a step costs the terrain cost of the cell it labels (the cell a unit leaves when it follows the field) times `STEP_PERCENT / STEP_BASE`, 10/10 orthogonally and 14/10 diagonally (integer division); the goal is seeded at 0 whatever its terrain (an attack field may be keyed on ground the class cannot enter); no corner cutting: no diagonal step past a blocked orthogonal neighbour. **The tie rule of `best_neighbour`**: it scans the neighbours NW, N, NE, W, E, SW, S, SE (`DX`/`DY`, ascending padded index) and takes one only if it is strictly cheaper than the best so far (starting from the cell's own served cost), so a tie goes to the lower cell; a diagonal counts only past two reached orthogonal cells (neither served `UNREACHED`); it reads only `cost`. **The key rule of `begin()`**: the same (`goal_cell`, `move_class`) as the field `cost` holds while `complete` is true keeps serving the old `cost` during the re-integration (`complete` stays true); any other key serves nothing (`complete` false, `cost` all `UNREACHED`) until it completes; an unusable key (a goal outside the map, a negative class, or a class `costs` does not cover) completes at once, all `UNREACHED`, so a cache never waits on it. `FlowField` cannot see `map_version`, so a caller that recycles a slot for the same key on another map version clears `complete` before `begin()` (P30b). `cost` and `next` are indexed by the padded cell `(ty + 1) * (cols + 2) + tx + 1` (one blocked ring, `(cols + 2) * (rows + 2)` entries); every method argument and result is a plain cell. Movement calls `best_neighbour` only at event cadence (a `FIELD` unit entering a new tile, or its field completing) and stores the result in `nav_next`; no per-tick call (ADR 0007 §1, ADR 0008 §2). `game/sim/FlowCache.gd` (P30b): `func configure(cols: int, rows: int, classes: int, slots: int) -> void`, `func acquire(goal_cell: int, move_class: int, map_version: int, tick: int) -> int` (slot or -1; the slot it hands out counts as referenced until the next `recount()`, a provisional reference, so two new keys in one tick never share a slot: P30b as merged, a decision of the lead, P26i, 2026-10-13), `func recount(field_column: PackedInt32Array, count: int) -> void` (references derived from the rows), `func refs(slot: int) -> int`, `func field(slot: int) -> FlowField`, `func advance(costs: PackedByteArray, budget_cells: int) -> int`, `func invalidate(map_version: int) -> void` (referenced slots re-integrate into `next`, unreferenced slots are dropped; called by `World.set_terrain()` right after it bumps `map_version`, once the World is prepared, never by `FlowCache.run`: a decision of the lead, P26i, 2026-10-13). P30b's members (as merged, #58): the ints `cols`, `rows`, `classes` and `requests`, `const STATE_ARRAYS: Array[StringName]` (the seven Packed arrays `key_goal`, `key_class`, `key_version`, `last_used`, `ref_count`, `requested`, `status`), `var fields: Array[FlowField]` (the slots) and `enum Status { FREE = 0, WAITING = 1, ACTIVE = 2 }`; `advance()` calls `FlowField.begin()` on a `WAITING` slot, because only it has the costs. Rules: ADR 0008 §3 **Revision 15 (ADR 0013 §2-§3):** all state that `advance` changes lives in Packed arrays or int members of `FlowField` (P30a), plus the bool `complete`, which its swap sets (P26h; ADR 0013 §2's status note of 2026-10-13); `configure()` sizes every slot's `cost`, `next` and buckets once, so `begin` never resizes (P30b, by calling `field.reserve(cols, rows)` on every slot; ADR 0008 §3 as amended); the completion swap stays a member swap. P30n adds `static func run(world: World, budget: int) -> void` (the recount from the World's `field` column, then `world.flow.advance`, with the FLOW dispatch as its first line; it does not call `invalidate()`, P26i), which P37b's `Sim.step` line calls |
| `SpatialGrid` | `game/sim/SpatialGrid.gd`: `func configure(map_w: int, map_h: int, cell_milli: int, capacity: int) -> void`, `func rebuild(xs: PackedInt32Array, ys: PackedInt32Array, count: int) -> void` (O(units): resets only the cells occupied last time, inserts rows in descending order so each cell lists ascending rows, no allocation after configure), `var cols: int`, `var rows: int`, `var head: PackedInt32Array` (per cell, -1 = empty), `var next: PackedInt32Array` (per row, -1 = end), `var cell_milli: int` (public, at least 1, set by `configure()`; targeting reads it to turn a reach into cells, so the size is written in one place), `func cell_of(x: int, y: int) -> int`. **Edge rules of the merged P32** (P26h, 2026-10-13, P32 review): `map_w` and `map_h` are in milli; `cols` and `rows` round up, so the last cell of a row or a column may be partial; a cell covers `[k * cell_milli, (k + 1) * cell_milli)` on each axis; a point outside the map goes to the nearest edge cell (a negative coordinate divides toward zero, then clamps to column or row 0), in `rebuild` and in `cell_of` alike; an empty map (a size or `cell_milli` of 0 or less) gives 0 cells, a rebuild then inserts nothing and `cell_of` returns 0; `capacity` bounds the rows (rows from `capacity` on are left out); `next` entries from the last rebuild's count on are -1 |
| `FogGrid` | `game/sim/FogGrid.gd`: `func configure(cols: int, rows: int, player_count: int) -> void` (blocks indexed `player - 1`), `func add_mask(sight_milli: int) -> int`, `func stamp(player: int, cell: int, mask: int, delta: int) -> void`, `func is_visible(player: int, cell: int) -> bool`, `func is_explored(player: int, cell: int) -> bool`, `func version_of(player: int) -> int`, `func dirty_cells(player: int) -> PackedInt32Array` (read only for the view), `func dirty_overflowed(player: int) -> bool`, `func begin_update(player: int) -> void` (clears that player's dirty list), `var visible: PackedInt32Array`, `var explored: PackedByteArray`, `var version: PackedInt32Array`, `const HASHED: Array[StringName] = [&"visible", &"explored"]`, `const UNHASHED: Array[StringName]`, `func copy_into(dst: FogGrid) -> void` (ADR 0012 §3: sets every array of `HASHED` and `UNHASHED` on `dst` to a `duplicate()` of its own and copies every scalar, so `dst` answers every read method above and a later write to the source never reaches it). Rules: ADR 0010 §1 **Revision 15 (ADR 0013 §2, P32):** each player's dirty list is a fixed block of one flat `PackedInt32Array` (`player_count` blocks of `cells / 4`), sized in `configure()`, with a `PackedInt32Array` of counts and one of "full" flags; `begin_update` resets that player's count and flag and never resizes; no `Array` of Packed arrays; `dirty_cells(player)` keeps its signature and returns a copy of the used part (only the view calls it, on the snapshot's copy). **As merged in P32f** (P26h, 2026-10-13, P32f review): `var listed: PackedByteArray`, one mark per player and cell (same layout as `visible`), so a flipped cell is listed once per update however often it flips; `begin_update` clears only the marks of its listed cells (O(count)) with the count and the flag; a cell that finds its block full sets the flag and is not marked. `UNHASHED` lists 8 arrays (`version`, `dirty`, `dirty_count`, `dirty_full`, `listed`, `mask_start`, `mask_radius`, `mask_half`) and 5 ints (`cols`, `rows`, `cells`, `player_count`, `dirty_capacity`). `dirty_capacity` is `cells / 4` rounded down (the test pins 7 x 5 -> 8). The masks are rows of half-widths in Packed arrays: `mask_start` (where a mask's rows start in `mask_half`), `mask_radius` (rows above and below the centre) and `mask_half` (each row's half-width in tiles, from `dy = -radius` down); `add_mask` returns ids 0, 1, 2 in call order, and `configure()` keeps the masks. The stamping order of a moving row is ADR 0010 §1's status note of 2026-10-13 (row P46) |
| `Replay` | `game/sim/Replay.gd`: `const FORMAT: int = 1`, `const CHECKPOINT_PERIOD: int = 15`. `var scenario_path: String`, `var seed: int`, `var defs_hash: int`, `var commands: PackedInt32Array`, `var checkpoint_ticks: PackedInt32Array`, `var checkpoint_hashes: PackedInt64Array`, `var final_tick: int`, `var final_hash: int`. `static func begin(scenario_path: String, seed: int, defs_hash: int) -> Replay`, `func record(command: Command) -> void`, `func add_checkpoint(tick: int, hash: int) -> void`, `func finish(world: World) -> void`, `func to_bytes() -> PackedByteArray`, `static func from_bytes(bytes: PackedByteArray) -> Replay`, `static func play(replay: Replay, world: World, until_tick: int) -> int` (-1 = every checkpoint matched, else the first differing tick; -2 = `defs_hash` differs; it drives the world through `MatchLoop.create(null, replay.scenario_path, replay.seed, world)` and feeds each tick's logged commands as `queued`, because the log already holds the stamped scenario orders). ADR 0007 §9. **P35a and P35b as merged** (#81, #82; P26o docs, 2026-10-14; ADR 0007 §9 status note): `const KINDS: Dictionary[String, int]` (the nine keys in ADR 0007 §9's order, each with its Variant type; the array kind is part of the type: `commands` and `checkpoint_ticks` `TYPE_PACKED_INT32_ARRAY`, `checkpoint_hashes` `TYPE_PACKED_INT64_ARRAY`), `const PLAY_MATCHED: int = -1`, `const PLAY_DEFS_DIFFER: int = -2`, `const PLAY_BAD_LOG: int = -3`. `to_bytes()` is `var_to_bytes` of an untyped `Dictionary` with the keys in ADR 0007 §9's order (a typed one writes its key and value types into the header), so equal replays give equal bytes. `from_bytes` first walks the Godot 4.7.2 wire format (`_is_plain_dictionary`, `_wire_size`: one Dictionary of ints, Strings, `PackedInt32Array`s and `PackedInt64Array`s, each inside the buffer, nothing after it), because `bytes_to_var` prints an engine `ERROR` line for every buffer it cannot decode and `verify.sh` fails on that line; the walk refuses a String that is not UTF-8 (`_is_utf8`). Only then does it call `bytes_to_var` (never the objects variant). It returns null also for a wrong `format`, a missing key, a value of another type or array kind than `KINDS` says, checkpoint arrays of unequal sizes, trailing bytes, and a command log that `Command.decode` does not read from 0 to exactly its end (`_log_decodes`). `play` returns `PLAY_MATCHED` when every recorded checkpoint with a tick <= `until_tick` matched, else the tick of the first one that differs or that the loop never reaches; `PLAY_DEFS_DIFFER` before any step; `PLAY_BAD_LOG` for a record that does not decode (when the loop reaches its tick) or checkpoint arrays of unequal sizes (before any step); both only in a replay built in memory, because `from_bytes` refuses them; also for a record behind the world's tick or a negative checkpoint tick. The checkpoints are checked in array order as the loop reaches each tick (a recorded log's ticks ascend), so a checkpoint behind an unreached one, or any checkpoint when `until_tick <= world.tick`, is not examined. It drives `MatchLoop.create(null, ...)`, feeds each tick's records as `queued` in log order, steps at most `until_tick - world.tick` ticks and does not check `final_hash` (the caller compares it) |

An order whose player has no mover (no row of that player whose type has a speed above 0) still yields a Command with no ids, which `Sim.step` drops and counts in `dropped_commands`. A null scenario, and a null entry in its orders, give no orders |
| `MatchLoop` | `game/sim/MatchLoop.gd` (P35b): `static func create(scenario: ScenarioDef, scenario_path: String, seed: int, world: World) -> MatchLoop` (a null `scenario` yields no scenario orders; `Replay.play` uses that), `var replay: Replay`, `var last_hash: int`, `var next_sequence: int`, `func step(world: World, queued: Array[Command], probe: SimProbe = null) -> void` (scenario orders first, then `queued` in submission order; stamps tick and sequence; records; `Sim.step`; on `tick % 15 == 0` adds a checkpoint and sets `last_hash`). The reused command list allocates nothing on ticks without commands. The one driver for the runner, `Replay.play`, `tests/perf` and Phase 4's headless matches. **P35b as merged** (#82; P26o docs, 2026-10-14; ADR 0007 §9 status note): `create` prepares the world. The checkpoint is `world.state_hash()` taken after `Sim.step` when the new tick is a multiple of `Replay.CHECKPOINT_PERIOD` (15): the first at tick 15, none at tick 0 and none at a final tick off the grid. `last_hash` is 0 until then. `next_sequence` starts at 0. A null in `queued` is skipped before stamping: it takes no sequence, is not recorded and is not counted as dropped. The queued Commands change hands here (ADR 0012 §2): they are stamped in place, not copied, and each object may be passed at most once per match. `queued` is only read; its owner (`MatchRunner` in P38, `TickJob.run()` from P38j) clears it after the step, because a command passed again is stamped again, recorded again and applied again |
| Defs | `UnitDef` + `display_name: String`, `max_hp: int = 1`, `armour: int` (`@export_enum("Infantry", "Light", "Heavy", "Building")`), `move_class: int` (`@export_enum("Foot", "Wheeled", "Tracked")`), `sight_range: int`, `acquire_range: int`, `weapon: WeaponDef`. `WeaponDef`: `id: StringName`, `range`, `min_range`, `damage`, `cooldown_ticks`, `projectile_speed: int`, `vs_armour: PackedInt32Array` (4). `RulesDef`: the 15 ints of `data/rules.tres`, whose script defaults equal that file (pinned by a test, the `InputSettings` precedent). `TerrainDef`: `symbol: String`, `id: StringName`, `cost: PackedInt32Array` (3). `TerrainSetDef`: `terrains: Array[TerrainDef]`. `MapDef`: `rows: PackedStringArray`, `terrain: TerrainSetDef`, `func size_tiles() -> Vector2i`, `func validate() -> PackedStringArray` (empty when valid). `OrderDef`: `tick: int`, `player: int`, `type: int` (Command.Type), `target_tile: Vector2i`. `ScenarioDef` + `map: MapDef`, `rules: RulesDef`, `orders: Array[OrderDef]`, `player_count: int` (0 = the highest spawn owner), `roster: Array[UnitDef]` (types registered at setup even if nothing spawns them). `SpawnDef` + `jitter: int` (milli, default 0; two draws per unit if and only if above 0). Every scenario file sets `rules` to `data/rules.tres` (a data test checks it) |

| `MatchRunner` v1 | **P38:** v0 contract plus `@export var match_seed: int = 1`, `var match_serial: int` (incremented by every `start()`), `var loop: MatchLoop`, `var replay: Replay` (= `loop.replay`), `var last_hash: int`, `var last_tick_usec: int` (`Sim.step`), `var last_runner_tick_usec: int` (the whole tick), `var probe: SimProbe` (null unless a HUD sets a `TimingProbe`; typed as the base class so P38 does not need P38t). `start()` builds the World with `DetRng.create(match_seed, DetRandom.STREAM_SIM)` and a `MatchLoop`. Each tick, in order: copy prev positions as Phase 1 does; drain `CommandQueue` into the reused list; `loop.step(world, list, probe)`; time it; emit `ticked` (P26o docs, 2026-10-14, superseded by the decisions below: `loop.step(world, list, <forwarding probe>)`, which forwards to `probe` and times `Sim.step`; clear the list; emit `ticked`). Prev re-alignment by id and projectile prevs are not in P38: they live in the snapshot (P38s, P38j). The lead's decisions given to P38 (P26o docs, 2026-10-14; P38 is in flight, its outcome is not recorded here; P26p docs: P38 has merged, see "P38 as merged" next): `last_tick_usec` is `Sim.step` only, measured by a `game/core` forwarding probe (from its `begin()` to the last mark) that the runner always passes to `loop.step`; the runner clears its queued list after every step (the `MatchLoop` row: a command passed again is applied again); sequences start at 0 (`MatchLoop.next_sequence`), so four assertions of `tests/core/test_match_runner.gd` change. **P38 as merged** (#85; P26p docs, 2026-10-14): the forwarding probe is `game/core/StepProbe.gd` (`class_name StepProbe extends SimProbe`, which never names its own class at run time): `var target: SimProbe` and `var step_usec: int`; `begin()` calls `target.begin()` (when `target` is not null), then sets `step_usec = 0` and reads the clock; `mark(phase)` reads the clock into `step_usec` (the time since `begin()`), then calls `target.mark(phase)`, so the target's own calls fall outside `step_usec`. The runner builds one (`var _step_probe: StepProbe = StepProbe.new()`) and sets `_step_probe.target = probe` before every step. `_tick()` in order: copy prev positions by index (resizing them only when the row count changed); `CommandQueue.drain_into(_commands)` into the reused list; `loop.step(world, _commands, _step_probe)`; `_commands.clear()`; `last_hash = loop.last_hash`; `last_tick_usec = _step_probe.step_usec` (`Sim.step` only); `last_runner_tick_usec` (the whole tick from its first line, the checkpoint hash included); `ticked.emit(world.tick)`. `start()` sets `last_hash` from the new loop, both times to 0, clears `_commands` and the `CommandQueue`. `DetRng.create(match_seed, stream_id)` returns `DetRandom.create(match_seed, stream_id)`. `tests/core/test_match_runner.gd` holds 18 public tests (with `before_test` and `after_test`, gdlint's limit of 20 public methods), so a later PR's new runner tests go into a new suite. **P38j (ADR 0012 §3):** the tick's work moves into `TickJob`; `var snapshot: MatchSnapshot` (the published snapshot: a new object for every tick, captured with the previous one as `previous`, published before `ticked`); `start()` publishes the tick-0 snapshot before it returns (a new snapshot whose `match_serial` and `last_hash` it sets first, then `capture(world, null)`, no `ticked`); every tick's new snapshot gets the runner's `match_serial` when the runner creates it, before the job runs, and the job gets the runner's current `world`, `loop` and `probe` (private fields behind guarded properties from P38w1b on) at every run or post, so a setter's swap takes effect on the next tick (P26p docs, 2026-10-14: the probe the job passes to `loop.step` is the runner's `StepProbe`, whose `target` the runner sets to its current `probe` at each run or post); `last_hash`, `last_tick_usec` and `last_runner_tick_usec` are set on the main thread at each publish from the snapshot; `prev_pos_x`/`prev_pos_y` become read-only properties returning `snapshot.prev_pos_x`/`prev_pos_y` (for Phase 1's `UnitLayer` until P40; P26s docs, 2026-10-14: since P40, #95, no `game/view` file reads them, only `tests/view/test_unit_layer.gd`'s `_world_hash`, `tests/core/test_match_runner.gd` and `tests/core/test_match_runner_snapshot.gd` (which also pins that the forwards are empty before the first `start()` and are the snapshot's own arrays), so `MatchRunner.gd`'s comment "for Phase 1's UnitLayer until P40" is stale; whether the forwards stay is open for P38w, section P38w). Their identity now changes with every tick; a frame without a tick keeps the same `snapshot` object. **P38j as merged** (#91; P26r docs, 2026-10-14): `_tick()` drains the `CommandQueue`, creates the next snapshot with the runner's `match_serial`, sets `_step_probe.target = probe`, hands the reused `_job` the `world`, `loop`, `_step_probe`, `_commands`, the new snapshot and `previous = snapshot`, sets `job.post_usec`, runs it inline, sets the job's `world`, `loop`, `snapshot` and `previous` to null, publishes and emits `ticked(next.tick)`. `_publish` sets `last_hash`, `last_tick_usec` (`step_usec`) and `last_runner_tick_usec` (`job_usec`, no longer the queue drain or the old prev copy) from the published snapshot (contract row `TickJob`). **P38w (ADR 0012 §1):** `enum TickMode { SYNC = 0, WORKER = 1, AUTO = 2 }`, `@export var tick_mode: TickMode = TickMode.SYNC`, `static func resolve_mode(mode: int, threads: bool, web: bool, headless: bool) -> int`, `enum HandOff { IDLE = 0, RUNNING = 1, FINISHED = 2 }`, `enum HandOffEvent { FRAME = 0, FRAME_TAKEN = 1, BOUNDARY = 2, WAIT_IDLE = 3, START = 4, RUN_TICKS = 5, STOP = 6 }`, `static func hand_off(state: int, event: int) -> Vector2i` (next state, action; the ADR 0012 §1 table; the frame's `try_wait()` result is part of the event, so the function stays pure), `static func shared_access_allowed(mode: int, state: int) -> bool`, `func wait_idle(timeout_msec: int = 5000) -> bool`, `func job_in_flight() -> bool`, `func worker_active() -> bool`, `static func live_workers() -> int`, `func set_probe(probe: SimProbe) -> void` (waits idle first). `world`, `loop`, `replay` and `probe` become properties over private fields whose getters `push_error` in `WORKER` mode while a job is `RUNNING`; each also has a setter (Godot 4.7.2 silently ignores an assignment to a getter-only property): the `world`, `loop` and `replay` setters assign the private field under the same rule (tests only, main thread, no job `RUNNING`), and the `probe` setter calls `set_probe()`. `static func mode_after_start(mode: int, start_error: int) -> int`: a failed `Thread.start()` resolves the runner to `SYNC` for good, with one `push_warning`, and the job it was about to post runs inline. In `WORKER` mode: one dedicated `Thread` with the semaphores `work` and `done`; the token is taken only in `RUNNING`, and every wait is a timed `try_wait()` loop (5 s cap, `push_error`, false); one tick ahead (at a boundary: publish the finished job and emit `ticked`, drain, create the next snapshot and set its `match_serial`, hand the job the current World, `loop` and probe, post the next job); a refill drains and posts; a job not finished at its boundary is held (`alpha()` 1.0, clock stopped), never two jobs at once and no command dropped; `run_ticks(n)` waits, publishes the finished job as the first of its n ticks and runs the rest inline; `start()` discards a job in flight; `_join_worker()` runs from `_exit_tree()` and `NOTIFICATION_PREDELETE`, only for a started thread. **P38w2:** `var held_ticks: int`, `var late_ticks: int` (post-to-done above `TICK_USEC = 1_000_000 / Sim.TICK_RATE`, 66 666 µs, never a literal), `var latency_usec_max: int`, all reset by `start()`; at most one publish per frame with the whole ticks owed after it dropped; the clock at 0 on every refill and resume, the refill frame not advancing it, and restarting at 0 in the frame that ends a hold. The World, `loop`, `replay` and `probe` belong to the worker while a job runs; the main thread reads the World only inside `ticked` or after `wait_idle()`. **P54r (ADR 0013 §3, revision 15):** `enum SimImpl { GDSCRIPT = 0, NATIVE = 1, AUTO = 2 }`, `@export var sim_impl: SimImpl = SimImpl.GDSCRIPT` (the default mirrors `tick_mode = SYNC`), `static func resolve_impl(impl: int, has_library: bool, os_name: String) -> int` (pure; `AUTO` without the library resolves to `GDSCRIPT`, and `start()` logs that fallback: `push_error` on Android, a warning on other desktops, nothing on the web; tests inject the OS name, so none triggers the Android `push_error`); `NATIVE` attaches `NativeKernels.create()` and sets `world.native_phases = kernels.phases()` |
| Native kernels | Revision 15, ADR 0013 §2-§3. `native/sim_kernels/src/bind/`: `SimKernels` (a stateless `RefCounted`: `abi_version()`, `phases() -> int` from `kernel_list.h`, `build_info()`, `check_world(world, phase) -> int` (pure, 0 = ok), and one method per ported phase taking the World and plain ints: `flow(world, budget)`, `separation(world)`, `integrate(world)`, `targeting(world)`, `fog(world)`; conditionally `intents(world)`, `grids(world, which)`, `fire(world)`, `projectiles(world)`), `world_access.h` (the only file with the internal-pointer access; the checked get and set table of every ported phase), `kernel_list.h` (one line per kernel), `kernel_<phase>.cpp`, `register_types`. `src/core/`: plain C++17 over `WorldView`'s `int64_t` accessors, no Godot header. `game/core/NativeKernels.gd` (lead, P54r): `create() -> Object` (null without the class or on an ABI mismatch), `self_check(scenario: ScenarioDef, seed: int, ticks: int, kernels: Object, mask: int) -> bool`. `tests/sim/support/sim_impls.gd` (P54): attaches the kernels and `kernels.phases()` after `prepare()`, asserts `EXPECTED_PHASES` and `get_class() == "SimKernels"`, and runs a module's tests under both implementations. Rules: ADR 0013 §2, §4, §6, and the "Native kernels" section of `rts-sim-conventions`. **As merged** (P26l docs, 2026-10-13, P54, #70): `check_world(world, phase)` returns 0 or `table_index * 5 + fault` (`status_of` in `src/core/world_status.h`; `Fault`: 1 unknown phase, 2 missing owner object, 3 wrong Variant type, 4 wrong length; a null World gives fault 2 at the phase's first name). `world_access.h` holds one table, `SIM_CHECKED_TABLE`, of 106 `X(owner, name, kind, size, phases)` entries, each with a phase bitmask (FLOW 30 names, TARGETING 32, SEPARATION 28, INTEGRATE 53, FOG 29); `tests/sim/native/test_kernel_abi.gd` reads the table from `world_access.h` itself, so there is no test-only binding. `kernel_list.h` is `SIM_KERNEL_LIST(K0, K1)` (empty until P30n), from which `sim_kernels.h`'s method declarations, the `ClassDB` bindings and `phases()` are generated. `src/core/` starts with `world_status.h` (the `Fault` enum and `status_of`), the one `core/` file without a kernel (its header records the exception); `WorldView` and the internal-pointer fetch (`ptr`/`ptrw`) land with P30n's first kernel, and the event-resize helper with P42n. `sim_impls.gd`'s `use(world, impl, kernels)` returns its problems as an `Array[String]` (no library, a mask other than `EXPECTED_PHASES`, kernels that are no `SimKernels`); with a wrong mask or kernels of another class it still attaches them, and with no library it attaches nothing and returns its problem list (P26m, 2026-10-14: it does not attach "either way"), and `is_native(world, phase)` says whether the phase really runs in C++; a module test's native pass asserts that `use(world, SimImpls.Impl.NATIVE)` returned an empty list and that `is_native(world, phase)` holds (through the preload name `SimImpls`, as `test_kernel_abi.gd` does; after `use(world, SimImpls.Impl.GDSCRIPT)` `is_native` is false). `check_world` is a diagnostic, not a hot-path call: each call checks its phase's whole list (FLOW about 8.7 µs on the arena, all five phases about 25 µs). Which check a kernel runs on entry (ADR 0013 §2's accepted text: "The kernel calls it on entry"), given that cost, is open for P30n, the first kernel (ADR 0013 §2's status note of 2026-10-13); no decision here. **As merged** (P26m, 2026-10-14, P30n #74 and P30n2 #75): the contract's `WorldView` is realised as `ArrayView<T>` per array (`src/core/array_view.h`: the pointer is private, and `get` returns and `set` takes `int64_t`) with `flow::Slot` (`src/core/flow.h`) as the aggregate; the header calls it "the per-array piece of the contract's WorldView, with flow::Slot as the aggregate" and says "The raw pointer is private, so core/ reaches an element only through get() and set()" and "Only bind/world_access.h builds a view from a pointer". `world_access.h` holds the internal-pointer fetch (`Borrowed<T>`, a Variant that keeps the array alive, with its view; `read_i32`/`read_bytes` through `ptr()`, `write_i32`/`write_bytes` through `ptrw()`). `kernel_list.h` has its first line (`flow`), `sim_impls.gd`'s `EXPECTED_PHASES` is `[1]`, and FLOW's list holds 26 names. `SimKernels.flow` runs `check_world(FLOW)` on every call, so the entry check is settled as the full check. |
| `MatchSnapshot` | P38s. `game/core/MatchSnapshot.gd` (`extends RefCounted`, no Node). `const COPIED: Array[StringName]` and `const NOT_COPIED: Array[StringName]` (their union is exactly the World's `ROW_ARRAYS`, `TYPE_ARRAYS`, `WEAPON_ARRAYS`, `PROJECTILE_ARRAYS` and `MAP_ARRAYS`; a coverage test; `move_cost` and `terrain_cost` are in `NOT_COPIED`, because the view needs only `terrain` and `terrain_ids`). Header ints `match_serial`, `tick`, `rows_version`, `map_version`, `last_hash` (`capture` sets `tick`, `rows_version` and `map_version` from the World. ADR 0012 §3). Rows (exactly `unit_count()` entries each): `unit_id`, `owner`, `type_index`, `pos_x`, `pos_y`, `hp`, `order`, `target`, `target_row` (the target's row here, -1 for none or gone), plus `prev_pos_x`, `prev_pos_y` aligned to these rows. The type and weapon tables of ADR 0011 §1 under their World names, with `type_armour` and `weapon_ids` (ADR 0012's amendment): `type_names`, `type_max_hp`, `type_speed`, `type_selection_radius`, `type_weapon`, `type_armour` and `weapon_projectile_speed` are in `COPIED`; `type_ids`, `weapon_ids` and `terrain_ids` are `Array[StringName]` in no World shape list, so they are named in `const ID_LISTS: Array[StringName] = [&"type_ids", &"weapon_ids", &"terrain_ids"]` and `capture` copies each with `duplicate()` (a shallow copy of a few immutable StringNames). Projectile slots `proj_id`, `proj_owner`, `proj_weapon`, `proj_x`, `proj_y` and `prev_proj_id`, `prev_proj_x`, `prev_proj_y` (the previous snapshot's arrays; a slot whose `proj_id` differs from `prev_proj_id` is new). Map `map_cols`, `map_rows`, `terrain`, `terrain_ids`. `var fog: FogGrid` (filled by `FogGrid.copy_into`). `events`, `stats` (this tick's). Timings `phase_usec: PackedInt64Array` (a copy of the `TimingProbe`'s, empty without one), `step_usec`, `job_usec`, `post_usec`, `end_usec`. `func unit_count() -> int`, `func index_of(id: int) -> int` (binary search, -1 when absent), `func cell_of(x: int, y: int) -> int` (the clamped formula of `World.cell_of`, with its early return: 0 when `map_cols == 0`), `func capture(world: World, previous: MatchSnapshot) -> void` (every `COPIED` array and every `ID_LISTS` list by `duplicate()`; unchanged `rows_version`: `prev_pos_*` are `previous.pos_*`; changed: a merge walk by id, survivors keep their prev, new rows start at their position; `previous` null or with a `match_serial` other than this snapshot's, already set by the runner: prev = current; World arrays aliased into locals before every loop). One new snapshot per publish, created by the runner on the main thread; a published snapshot is never written. Rules: ADR 0012 §3. **P38s as merged** (#88; P26q docs, 2026-10-14; the text above stays as written, and where it differs this note wins): `capture` also takes `events` and `stats` with `duplicate()`, in two explicit lines (`events = world.events.duplicate()`, `stats = world.stats.duplicate()`); they are not in `COPIED`, so `COPIED` (21 names) and `NOT_COPIED` (28) still cover exactly the five shape lists. A null `previous`, or one of another match, gives every `prev_*` as a `duplicate()` of the current array (equal values, not the same object), so `prev_proj_*` equal the current `proj_*` and no slot is new. `target_row` takes one `bsearch` per row over the snapshot's ascending `unit_id`, aliased into a typed local (O(n log n); the targets are not ascending, so there is no merge walk); `_align_prev` is the merge walk. The loops run over the snapshot's own copies (aliased into typed locals) and, in `_align_prev`, the previous snapshot's arrays as typed parameters; never over World arrays. The capture costs about 20-24 µs at 150 rows (tick 450, `SNAPSHOT capture_us=…`) |
| `TickJob` | P38j. `game/core/TickJob.gd` (`extends RefCounted`, no Node; its property list holds only World, `MatchLoop`, `SimProbe`, `MatchSnapshot`, `Array[Command]`, `Semaphore`, int and bool, and nothing reachable from it is a Node or a Resource). `var world: World`, `var loop: MatchLoop`, `var probe: SimProbe` (the runner sets all three from its current `world`, `loop` and `probe` (P26o docs, 2026-10-14: the probe is the runner's forwarding probe, which forwards to its `probe`; P26p docs, 2026-10-14, from P38 as merged, #85: that is the runner's one `StepProbe`, `game/core/StepProbe.gd`, whose `target` the runner sets to its current `probe` at each run or post), private fields behind guarded properties from P38w1b on, at every run or post, so a setter's swap takes effect on the next tick), `var commands: Array[Command]`, `var snapshot: MatchSnapshot` (the new one this job fills; its `match_serial` is already set by the runner), `var previous: MatchSnapshot`, `var post_usec: int`, `var end_usec: int`. `func run() -> void` (one tick: `loop.step(world, commands, probe)` with the forwarding probe (P26o; the `StepProbe`, P26p), `snapshot.last_hash = loop.last_hash`, `snapshot.capture(world, previous)`, timings; clears `commands`). The job never sets `match_serial`. P38w adds `func serve() -> void` (the worker loop: wait on `work`, return when `quit` is set, `run()`, set `end_usec` (P26r docs, 2026-10-14: superseded, `run()` sets `end_usec` since P38j and `serve()` keeps that), post `done`). Rules: ADR 0012 §1-2. **P38j as merged** (#91; P26r docs, 2026-10-14; where it differs from the text above, this note wins): P38j adds `post_usec` and `end_usec` (`Time.get_ticks_usec()` when the runner handed the job over, and when `run()` ended); P38w does not add the two timing fields: it adds `serve()` and the `work` and `done` semaphores and the `quit` flag that `serve()` uses (the merged `TickJob.gd` has neither a `Semaphore` nor a `bool` field; `test_tick_job.gd`'s `ALLOWED_TYPES` already admits both). In `SYNC`, `_tick()` sets `job.post_usec` just before the inline `job.run()`, and `run()` sets `end_usec`; P38w's `serve()` keeps both. `run()` in order: `loop.step(world, commands, probe)`, `commands.clear()`, `snapshot.last_hash = loop.last_hash`, `snapshot.capture(world, previous)`, then the timings: `step_usec` only from a `StepProbe` (`probe as StepProbe`), `phase_usec` (a `duplicate()`) only from that `StepProbe`'s `target` when it is a `TimingProbe`, `post_usec`, `end_usec`, and `job_usec = end_usec - started`: the run on the thread that ran it, from its first line through the capture and the timing copies, not the wait since the post. So a direct driver (P49a's `test_sim_perf.gd` on `TickJob`) passes a `StepProbe` whose `target` is the `TimingProbe`; a bare `TimingProbe` as `probe` leaves `step_usec` 0 and `phase_usec` empty. After `run()` the runner sets `job.world`, `job.loop`, `job.snapshot` and `job.previous` to null (the reused `_job` keeps only its `probe`, the runner's `_step_probe`, and `commands`, the runner's `_commands`), so a restart frees the old match at once. The runner's `last_runner_tick_usec` is the published snapshot's `job_usec`: it no longer includes the queue drain or the old prev copy. Guards in `tests/core/test_tick_job.gd` (fixtures `tests/core/support/holding_probe.gd` and `tests/core/support/capturing_snapshot.gd`); the runner's publishing cases in `tests/core/test_match_runner_snapshot.gd` |
| `TimingProbe`, `PerfStats`, `PerfGateDef` | P38t. `game/core/TimingProbe.gd` (`extends SimProbe`): `var phase_usec: PackedInt64Array` (size `SimProbe.Phase.COUNT`, indexed by phase id, last tick; `begin()` zeroes it and reads the clock, `mark(k)` stores the time since the previous mark or `begin()`, so a phase that is not marked reads 0). The per-phase split is always reported as `Phase.COUNT` values in id order: the `PERF` line prints `phases=commands:0.12,flow:0.00,...` with every name of `PHASE_NAMES`, and `PerfHud.summary()["phases_ms"]` is an `Array` of `Phase.COUNT` floats, labelled from `PHASE_NAMES` on the card. A split therefore compares between PRs (P37a2's before and after). `game/core/PerfStats.gd` (pure): `static func percentile(sorted: PackedFloat64Array, p: int) -> float` (nearest rank), `static func mean(...)`, `static func worst_window_sum(values: PackedFloat64Array, window: int) -> float`. Used by both `tests/perf` and `PerfHud`. `game/core/PerfGateDef.gd` (Resource): the thresholds of ADR 0012 §4 as defaults (`heavy_rigs` 135, `heavy_frames_min` 600, `on_time_ms` 17.5, `on_time_share_pct` 95, `work_p95_ms` 5.6 (main-thread frame work over all heavy-window frames), `work_tick_p95_ms` 5.6 (publish frames only), `sim_p95_ms` 13.3 (the worker job p95), `job_p99_ms` 26.7, `late_ticks_max: int` 0, `fog_update_p95_ms` 0.4, `draw_calls_max` 60, `release_only: bool` true, `ci_ratio_mean: float` 4.4 and `ci_ratio_p95: float` 5.9 (from the release run's worker mode, not thresholds)) and `golden_ticks: PackedInt32Array`, `golden_hex: PackedStringArray` **Revision 15.** ADR 0014 (P38t): `PerfGateDef` drops `on_time_ms` and `on_time_share_pct` and adds `drop_slots` 0.5, `dropped_pct_max` 1.0, `cadence_pct_min` 99.0, `hitches_max` 0, `jitter_ms` 17.5 (a diagnostic), `median_tolerance_pct` 2.0 (one-sided), `refresh_tolerance_pct` 1.0, `ref_iterations` 1000 and `ref_burst_ms` 400 (`pin_busy_pct` not taken (calibration 2026-10-13), so exception 8 is unused); new pure `game/core/RefLoop.gd` (one static function); `TimingProbe` does not change (the worker takes no reference samples). ADR 0013 (P54r): `ci_ratio_native_mean` 9.4 and `ci_ratio_native_p95` 14.0, printed estimates, not thresholds. (P26o docs, 2026-10-14) `TimingProbe.gd` has a script base and must not name itself at run time (P56c's open item, "Answers and follow-ups (P26o docs)"). **P38t as merged** (#89; P26q docs, 2026-10-14; the text above stays as written, and where it differs this note wins): "one static function" is two. `RefLoop` (`extends RefCounted`) has `static func run(iterations: int) -> int`, the P26n2 calibration body (`acc = (acc * 31 + i) & 0xFFFFFF` from 1; `run(1000)` = 16346357, pinned), and `static func normalised_p95(work: PackedFloat64Array, samples: PackedFloat64Array, peak: float) -> float` (the p95 over frames of work_i × min(1, peak / sample_i); a frame without a sample (0, or past the end of `samples`) and a `peak` of 0 keep the raw value). `PerfStats` (`extends RefCounted`) has `static func mean(values: PackedFloat64Array) -> float`, and its edge rules are: an empty array gives 0.0; `percentile` clamps `p` to 0..100 and takes the integer rank `(p * n + 99) / 100`, at least 1 (p 0 is the smallest value, p 100 the largest); `worst_window_sum` gives the whole sum when the series is shorter than the window and 0.0 for a window below 1. `PerfGateDef` `extends Resource` with 21 scalar `@export` fields plus `golden_ticks: PackedInt32Array` and `golden_hex: PackedStringArray`, and no `on_time_*` field: `int` are `heavy_rigs`, `heavy_frames_min`, `late_ticks_max`, `draw_calls_max`, `hitches_max`, `ref_iterations` and `ref_burst_ms`; `release_only` is `bool`; the other 13 are `float`. `TimingProbe` sits beside the runner's `StepProbe` as the runner's `probe` (the `StepProbe` passes every `begin()` and `mark()` of `Sim.step` on to it) and never names its own class at run time |
| `MapView` | `game/view/MapView.gd`, `class_name MapView extends Node2D`, replaces `TestMapView` (deleted). `@export var runner: MatchRunner`, `@export var colors: Dictionary[StringName, Color]` (the keys of STYLE.md § "Fog and terrain": `<id>` for each terrain id, and `<id>_b` and `<id>_c` for the second and third tone of its tile, for example `&"grass"`, `&"grass_b"`, `&"scrub_c"`; every terrain id must have its base key, `_b` and `_c` keys are optional; a missing base key bakes magenta, and the data test and P52 enforce the keys (P26l docs, 2026-10-13)), `@export var tiles: Dictionary[StringName, String]` (STYLE.md's rect table: per terrain id the filled rects `x,y,w,h,key` of its 8x8 tile in drawing order, the first being the base; `grass_b` is the grass tile on tiles with x + y odd; the same keys and strings as the gallery's `TERRAIN_TILES`), `@export var bake_px_per_tile: int = 8`. `func map_rect_px() -> Rect2i`, `func bake_count() -> int` and `func image() -> Image` (the CPU-side bake; for tests). Each tile is baked as its base rect, then its other rects in table order. The map has no grid (Michael, 2026-10-12: "fjern gitter"), so no texel is darkened and tiles meet edge to edge. Bakes the terrain into one `ImageTexture` per match (the World's instance id until P38 adds `match_serial`; P26l docs) and per `map_version`, and draws it with one textured rect with `texture_filter` NEAREST. P36 reads `runner.world` (it merges before the snapshot exists, and every scene runs `SYNC` until P50b); P40 switches it to `runner.snapshot` (`terrain`, `terrain_ids`, `map_cols`, `map_rows`, `map_version`; ADR 0012 §3). `main_view.gd` sizes the camera from it. **As merged** (P26l docs, 2026-10-13, P36, #71): the STYLE.md colours and tile rects are `MapView.gd`'s `@export` defaults of `colors` and `tiles`, not values set in `main.tscn`, so P52 reads both `MapView.new()` and the `MapView` node of `main.tscn`; `const TILE_GRID: int = 8` (the grid of the rects) and `const MISSING: Color = Color.MAGENTA`; besides `map_rect_px()`, `bake_count()` and `image()` the test hooks are `draw_count()` and `drawn_rect()`. The match key is the World's instance id until P38 adds `match_serial`; P40, which moves the reads to `runner.snapshot`, keys it on the snapshot's `match_serial` and keeps `tests/view/test_map_view.gd`'s `test_one_bake_per_match_and_one_after_set_terrain_however_many_frames_pass` green. The bake indexes its tiles by `terrain[cell]` with no range check; P48 adds the guard |
| `UnitLayer` v2 | Phase 1 API kept (`rig_count`, `unit_screen_position(row)`, `fill_pick_snapshot`, `set_selected`, `team_color`). No `World` or `MatchSnapshot` member: each entry point reads `runner.snapshot` afresh, and per-row loops alias its arrays into typed locals (ADR 0012 §2-3). Rigs follow rows by id, re-mapped when `rows_version` changes; `_sync_rigs()` (comparing `match_serial` and `rows_version`) runs first in `_process`, `set_selected`, `fill_pick_snapshot` and `unit_screen_position`. A death frees its rig, a new row gets a new rig at its position (no pool in Phase 2). The layer's children are only the rigs, in row order, named `Unit<id>` (Phase 1 tests iterate them). A ring is the inner class `Ring extends Sprite2D` with the shared ring texture: named `Ring`, created on the unit's first selection as the rig's first child (so an unselected tank rig has exactly `Hull`, `Team`, `Turret`), with `radius` and `unit` computed exactly as in Phase 1 and `scale = radius / RING_TEX_RADIUS_PX` (the texture's centre-line radius from STYLE.md, P34). A type without `<type_id>_turret.svg` (checked with `ResourceLoader.exists`) gets Hull and Team only. Layer `z_index` (absolute, one z per batch group, ADR 0012 §6): Ring 1, Hull `10 + type_index`, Team `20 + 8 * type_index + owner`, Turret `100 + type_index` (type indexes 0-9 and owners 0-7; beyond that the band's last group), so neither mixed types nor interleaved owners break a batch. **P40 as merged** (#95; P26s docs, 2026-10-14; where it differs from this row, this note wins): public additions `const RING_ART: String = "res://assets/art/svg/ui/selection_ring.svg"`, `const RING_TEX_RADIUS_PX: float = 72.0` (the texture's centre-line radius), the z band constants `Z_RING` 1, `Z_FIRST` `[10, 20, 100]`, `Z_LAST` `[19, 99, 109]` (per layer of `LAYER_FILES`: hull, team, turret), `Z_TYPES` 10 and `Z_OWNERS` 8, and `static func layer_z(layer: int, type: int, owner_id: int) -> int`: `Z_LAST[layer]` when `type >= Z_TYPES` or (the Team layer and `owner_id >= Z_OWNERS`), else `Z_FIRST[layer]` plus `type * Z_OWNERS + owner_id` (Team) or `type` (Hull, Turret; the owner is not read). The ring: `radius = maxf(ViewScale.milli_to_px(type_selection_radius + RING_PAD_MILLI), RING_MIN_DP * unit)` with `RING_PAD_MILLI` 50, `RING_MIN_DP` 18.0 and `unit = base px per dp / camera zoom`, and `scale = Vector2.ONE * radius / RING_TEX_RADIUS_PX`; on screen the ring's centre-line radius is the larger of the padded selection radius in px times the zoom and 18 dp, and it is sized again when the zoom or the window changes. Every ring sits below every hull (z 1), so where tanks overlap a neighbour's hull can cover a ring (ADR 0012 §6). Every read goes through `runner.snapshot` (ADR 0012 §3): rows, `prev_pos_*`, `hp`, `target`, the type tables and `snapshot.fog`; never `runner.world`. P44a: turrets (or squad rigs) turn towards the row in `snapshot.target_row` (no per-frame `index_of`). P45: `func hit_time_ms(row: int) -> int` (frame clock of the last hp drop; `last_hp` and hit times are re-aligned by the rig id merge, never plain-resized) and `func is_ringed(row: int) -> bool` (the selection as last given to `set_selected`). P47: `func is_shown(row: int) -> bool`; enemy rigs are hidden on cells not visible to `local_player`, unless `@export var reveal_enemies: bool = false` is set (the perf arena, and tests whose subject is not fog). `is_shown(row)` and `fill_pick_snapshot` (P47p) read `runner.snapshot.fog` and `reveal_enemies` at call time, with no cached flag, so a test that sets `reveal_enemies` or steps the fog sees the change on the next call. Hiding sets only the rig's `visible`: a hidden rig keeps its position, heading and tint, and `unit_screen_position` still answers for it |
| `PickSnapshot`, `Picker` | P47p: `PickSnapshot` gains `pickable: PackedByteArray` (row order kept, resized in place). `fill_pick_snapshot` sets 0 for enemy rows that are not shown and fills every other field for them as before. `Picker.pick` and the box queries skip rows whose entry is 0; a row with no entry (`row >= pickable.size()`, as in the hand-built snapshots of `test_picker` and `test_player_controller`'s `TwoTypeLayer`) is pickable |
| `ProjectileLayer`, `EffectsLayer` | `game/view/ProjectileLayer.gd` (`extends Node2D`, `@export var runner`): draws every live slot of `runner.snapshot` in one `_draw()` as atlas quads at `lerp(prev_proj, proj, alpha)`, turned along `prev_proj` to `proj`: the `ROCKET` region when `weapon_ids[proj_weapon]` is `rocket`, else `SHELL` (STYLE.md § "Combat effects"); a slot whose `proj_id` differs from `prev_proj_id` is new in the last tick, has no heading and is not drawn until the next tick (ADR 0012's amendment). `game/view/EffectsLayer.gd` (`extends Node2D`, `@export var runner`, `@export var capacity: int = 128`): reads `SHOT`, `IMPACT` and `DIED` from `runner.snapshot.events` in `runner.ticked`, keeps a fixed ring (oldest overwritten), draws tracers as lines and the rest as atlas quads in one `_draw()`, expires by the frame clock. The effect per event follows STYLE.md § "Combat effects": the weapon is `weapon_ids[i]`, hitscan is `weapon_projectile_speed[i] == 0`, and a `DIED` unit is infantry when `type_armour[type_index] == World.Armour.INFANTRY`. `func active_count() -> int` on both. Both reset on a new `match_serial` |

Sizes and placement from STYLE.md § "Health bars", as exported view constants: the class is infantry when `type_armour[type_index] == World.Armour.INFANTRY`, else vehicle; width 22 dp (vehicle) or 16 dp (infantry), height 6 dp; centred on the unit, its bottom edge r x zoom + 1 dp above the unit's screen position, with the sprite reach r = 26 world px (vehicle) or 14 (infantry). The ring radius does not place it |

On `ticked`, when `runner.snapshot.fog.version_of(local_player)` changed, it patches the dirty cells (full rebuild on a new `match_serial` or an overflowed list). `func upload_count() -> int`, `func last_update_usec() -> int`, `func image() -> Image` (the CPU-side image that is uploaded; tests read this). | Main scene | `scenes/main.tscn`, children in order: `MatchRunner`, `MapView`, `UnitLayer`, `HealthBarLayer`, `ProjectileLayer`, `EffectsLayer`, `FogLayer`, `MarkerLayer`, `Camera`, `PlayerController`, `TouchInput`, `Hud` (unchanged). `match_seed = 1`; from P50b `tick_mode = AUTO` (ADR 0012 §1: the worker on Android and desktop, `SYNC` on the web and headless). On entry: `Engine.max_fps = 60`, `keep_on_sink.call(true)`, all player-1 units selected (view only); on exit both are restored. `game/ui/PerfHud.gd` (`class_name PerfHud extends PanelContainer`): `@export var runner: MatchRunner`, `@export var gate: PerfGateDef`, `@export var run_ticks: int = 900`, `var clipboard_sink: Callable = DisplayServer.clipboard_set` (P50a2), `func summary() -> Dictionary` (keys `runs`, `frames`, `fps_avg`, `frame_p50_ms`, `frame_p95_ms`, `frame_max_ms`, `on_time_pct`, `heavy_frames`, `heavy_on_time_pct`, `work_p95_ms` (main thread, all heavy-window frames), `work_tick_p95_ms` (frames that publish a tick), `work_other_p95_ms`, `render_cpu_p95_ms`, `draw_calls_p95`, `drawn_rigs_p50`, `drawn_rigs_max`, `tick_p50_ms`, `tick_p95_ms`, `tick_max_ms` (`Sim.step`, from `snapshot.step_usec`), `job_mean_ms`, `job_p50_ms`, `job_p95_ms`, `job_p99_ms`, `job_max_ms` (the whole job, from `snapshot.job_usec`), `late_ticks`, `held_ticks`, `latency_usec_max`, `flow_slice_p95_ms`, `tick_mode`, `view_p95_ms`, `fog_update_p95_ms`, `phases_ms`, `alive`, `hash_hex`, `checkpoints`, `golden_match`, `invalid`, `layer_mode`, `refresh_hz`, `screen`, `zoom`, `model`, `os`, `engine`, `debug`, `verdicts`, `gate_pass`), `func restart() -> void`, `func copy_report() -> void` (P50a2, through `clipboard_sink`). Card: ADR 0011 §5; gate thresholds and verdicts: ADR 0012 §4 (`sim_p95_ms` against `job_p95_ms`, `job_p99_ms`, `work_p95_ms` and `work_tick_p95_ms` against the summary keys of the same names, `late_ticks_max`, the `build` verdict that fails a debug template when `release_only` is set, and `INVALID` when the heavy-window draw-call or render CPU p95 is 0). Checkpoints come from `snapshot.last_hash` at ticks that are multiples of 150; the `TimingProbe` is installed with `runner.set_probe()`. **Headless rule:** tests never read the `DisplayServer` clipboard or keep-on state, nor an `ImageTexture` after `update()` (all verified unreadable or stale on 4.7.2 headless); they assert on the injected sinks and on `image()` (the Phase 1 `vibrate`/`clock` precedent). `Engine.max_fps` reads back headless and is asserted directly **Revision 15 (ADRs 0013 and 0014):** the scene's runner carries no `scenario`; `perf_arena.gd`'s `_ready` calls `hud.preflight(runner)` (the self-check seam, then the burst seam; with a job in flight the pure `preflight_allowed` refuses it and the run is `INVALID` with the reason `preflight_job_in_flight`, never a `push_error`), then sets `perf_arena.tres` and calls `runner.start()`, then `hud.restart()`; `sim_impl = AUTO`. `summary()` gains ADR 0014 §4's keys (`on_time_pct` and `heavy_on_time_pct` become `jitter_pct` and `heavy_jitter_pct`, `refresh_hz` becomes `refresh_hz_start` and `refresh_hz_end`, plus the dropped-frame, cadence, hitch, reference-loop and `_norm_` keys of the amendment's ADR 0014 table) and ADR 0013's (`sim_impl`, `native_phases`, `self_check`, `phases_p95_noncp_ms`, `job_p95_noncp_ms`, `job_minus_step_p95_cp_ms`, `job_minus_step_p95_noncp_ms`). Verdicts: the main-thread CPU limits on the `_norm_` keys, `sim_p95_ms` and `job_p99_ms` raw, the pacing fields on the new keys, the `impl` verdict (on Android a resolved implementation other than `NATIVE` or a self-check mismatch fails `GATE` alone), `INVALID` also by ADR 0014 §2. "Run again" (P50a3) re-runs the burst seam after `wait_idle()` and before the runner restarts |
| Perf test | `tests/perf/test_sim_perf.gd`. P38p: report only (catastrophic cap 20 ms mean), times `MatchLoop.step`, prints `est_phone_mean_ms` and `est_phone_p95_ms` (ADR 0012 §4). P49a: times `TickJob.run()` in `SYNC` (the gate's `job_usec` quantity) with `Sim.step` and the phases as sub-lines; deterministic cost bounds, load bounds, memory bound; the wall clock stays report-only. P49b: `tests/perf/budget.cfg` (`[sim] mean_ms`, `p95_ms`, `ticks=900`, `units=150`), a regression ratchet at 1.5 times the worst calibrated min-of-3 (ADR 0012 §4; no device ceiling). Prints the `PERF` line of ADR 0011 §4 and writes `reports/perf/sim.json`. The wall-clock budget lives only in this file. P49a also adds `tests/perf/test_golden_hashes.gd`: one seed-42 arena run through `MatchLoop` whose checkpoints every 150 ticks and final hash must equal `perf_gate.tres`, with no timing at all, so it can run alone by path (P51b) **Revision 15 (ADR 0013):** P38p keeps one GDScript `PERF` line (its `phases=` values are per-phase means over the measured ticks, which clause (a) of ADR 0013 §1's port rule reads); P38pn adds the second line `impl=native` with its estimate from the native ratios; P49a checks the golden hashes under both implementations against the same `perf_gate.tres`, times `TickJob` on native (the GDScript timing report-only) and prints two report lines without a bound (the `COMMANDS` phase on the arena's largest order tick, and the p95 of `TickJob` minus `Sim.step` on checkpoint ticks); P49b calibrates the ratchet on the native tick. **P38p as merged** (#92; P26r docs, 2026-10-14; where it differs from the text above, this note wins): two more files, `tests/perf/support/perf_report.gd` (pure, no clock and no World: the run statistics, the best of the runs, the phone estimates, the `PERF` line and `sim.json` from one report dictionary) and `tests/perf/test_perf_report.gd` (pins it on fake samples, and checks `perf_gate.tres`). The suite prints one line per run, starting `RUN sim ` (`RUN sim seed=… run=… mean_ms=… p50_ms=… p95_ms=… max_ms=… hash=…`; three seed-42 runs and one seed-43 run), and one summary line starting `PERF sim `, the one P51 copies (`^PERF `). Its key order is `PerfReport.LINE_KEYS`, then three groups: `PERF sim units= ticks= runs= mean_ms= p50_ms= p95_ms= p99_ms= max_ms= worst15_ms= ticks_per_s= ref_ms= est_phone_mean_ms= est_phone_p95_ms= alive_end= proj_peak= projectile_overflow= stats_max=<stat:max,…> phases=<phase:ms,…> hash=<16 hex>` (floats with three decimals; `stats_max` keys are the `World.Stat` names in lower case). Every statistic on the line (`mean_ms`, `p50_ms`, `p95_ms`, `p99_ms`, `max_ms`, `worst15_ms`) and every phase mean is the minimum over the three seed-42 runs, each taken separately (`best_of`, `best_phases`), so the best p95 may come from another run than the best mean, and the phase means sum to a little less than `mean_ms` (the checkpoint hash that `MatchLoop.step` takes is in no phase). `ref_ms` is the smallest of 40 timed `RefLoop.run(ref_iterations)` calls: `_time_reference()` runs before and after the four runs, each time one untimed warm-up call and then `REF_SAMPLES` (20) timed calls. `units`, `alive_end`, `proj_peak`, `projectile_overflow`, `stats_max` and `hash` come from the first seed-42 run. The gate-file case compares every `PerfGateDef` field with `PerfGateDef.new()` (type and value), except the `ci_ratio_*` fields, which it only range-checks (today `ci_ratio_mean` and `ci_ratio_p95`: a float from 1.0 to 100.0; exception 5, the lead changes them in the file alone), and the golden arrays, which only pair up; a second case detects a field with no `field = ` line in the file. Neither pins the field count (lower bounds only), so P54r and P38pn add fields without touching the qa suite |
| CI summary | `ci.yml` job `verify`, step "Job summary": appends every `^PERF ` line of the verify log in a code block. P51b: a job on an arm64 Linux runner that runs `tests/sim/test_determinism.gd`, `tests/sim/test_world_hash.gd` and `tests/perf/test_golden_hashes.gd` by path. P51r: a job that exports the release-template perf APK (arm64, `dev_menu` feature kept) for the gate (ADR 0012 §4) **Revision 15 (ADR 0013 §10):** the verify job builds the library first and installs SCons, the pinned `clang-format` and binutils (P53), and runs `scripts/ci/check-native-tests.sh` after `verify.sh` (P55); a `native-clang` job (x86_64, clang, `optimize=speed`; P55, and P51 adds its golden-hash step with `--require test_golden_hashes`); the Android job builds `template_debug` and `template_release` arm64 and checks the APK (P55a); P51b builds a Linux arm64 clang library and runs the native suites and the golden hashes under both implementations; the sanitizer job (P57) |
| Art | `assets/art/svg/units/rifle_squad_{hull,team}.svg`, `rocket_squad_{hull,team}.svg` (64x64, import scale 0.5), `assets/art/svg/ui/selection_ring.svg`, `assets/art/svg/fx/effects_atlas.svg` (+ `.import` files). STYLE.md gains § "Combat effects" (atlas regions, sizes in dp or world px, lifetimes in ms, tracer width and colour), § "Health bars" (width per unit class, height, the density rule, fill colours that are not team colours, own and enemy shapes) and § "Fog and terrain" (tile tones keyed `<id>`, `<id>_b`, `<id>_c`, the tile rects, fog alphas; no grid, Michael 2026-10-12). The gallery gains both squads in the four slots, a terrain patch row, the ring and the atlas |
| HOW_TO_PLAY | Shared file. Each PR edits only the rows listed for it under "HOW_TO_PLAY rows" |

### World v1 columns
The struct-of-arrays is the contract between the two gameplay-dev threads. Every row array is in
`ROW_ARRAYS` and hashed; scratch arrays are in `SCRATCH_ARRAYS`, sized `max_rows` and not hashed;
`events` and `stats` are in `RUNTIME_ARRAYS` (not hashed, not compacted).
"Readers" lists the PRs whose code reads the column (the view reads only the copy in
`MatchSnapshot`, ADR 0012 §3, of what ADR 0011 §1 lists).

| Column | Type and range | At spawn | Writer PRs | Reader PRs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `unit_id` | int32, 1.. (never reused) | next id | P29a | all |
| `owner` | 0..`player_count` | spawn owner | P29a | all |
| `type_index` | 0..types-1 | spawn type | P29a | all |
| `pos_x`, `pos_y` | 0..`map_w`-1 / `map_h`-1 milli | grid point (+ jitter) | P29c (jitter), P33 (Phase 1 movement), P37a2 (integrate), P39 (push) | all |
| `goal_x`, `goal_y` | 0..map-1 milli | position | P33 (Formation), P37a (Formation: blocked target moved), P41 (`ATTACK` fallback: the target's last position), P46b (target left sight: its last seen position) | P37a2, P37b, P41 |
| `order` | `World.Order` | `IDLE` | P33, P37a2 (arrival sets `IDLE`), P37b (stall), P41 (`ATTACK` fallback), P46b (target left sight) | P37a2, P37b, P39, P41, view |
| `hp` | int32; at most max hp; may go below 0 by at most one hit | type max hp (at least 1) | P42a, P42b | P31a (removal), P42a, view |
| `order_target` | unit id, 0 = none | 0 | P33 (`ATTACK`), P41, P46b (cleared when the target leaves sight) | P37b, P41 |
| `target` | unit id, 0 = none | 0 | P41, P46b (cleared when not visible) | P37a2, P37b (`CHASE`), P42a/b, view |
| `cooldown` | 0..`cooldown_ticks` | 0 | P42a | P42a |
| `nav_mode` | `World.NavMode` | `NONE` | P37a2, P37b, P41 (P26s docs, 2026-10-14, P37a2 as merged, #94: `Movement.reset_nav(world, rows, mode)` is the command-cadence hook, `Sim._apply_point` and `Sim._apply_attack` set `DIRECT`, `Sim._apply_stop` sets `NONE`; `Movement.run` turns `NONE` into `DIRECT` for a unit with an order (a hand-set order); arrival in `Movement.integrate` sets `NONE`; `HOLD` and `CHASE` are set only by combat, P41) | P37a2, P37b, P39 |
| `field` | flow slot 0..`flow_cache_size`-1, -1 = none | -1 | P37b | P30b (recount), P37b |
| `nav_cell` | cell, -1 = line not checked yet | -1 | P37a2 (P26s docs, 2026-10-14, as merged, #94: `reset_nav` and arrival set -1; a unit with an order, not in `CHASE` or `HOLD`, with -1 checks its line on its first tick where `(tick + id) % line_check_period_ticks == 0` and writes `world.cell_of(x, y)`, so the cell is set once per order; `CHASE` and `HOLD` units are not checked; the check's result is discarded until P37b) | P37a2, P37b, P41 |
| `nav_next` | plain cell a `FIELD` unit steers to, -1 = none (ADR 0008 §2) | -1 | P37b (set at event cadence, -1 on every new order) | P37b |
| `best_dist` | plain distance in milli, 0..`0x3FFFFFFF` (at most about 91 000 used) | `0x3FFFFFFF` | P37b | P37b |
| `stall` | 0..`stall_ticks` | 0 | P37b | P37b |
| `fog_cell` | cell, -1 = not stamped | -1 | P46 | P46 |
| `intent_x`, `intent_y` (scratch) | -speed..speed milli per tick | - | P37a2 (P26s docs, 2026-10-14, as merged, #94: `Movement.run` fills both arrays with 0, all `max_rows` entries, then writes every row with an order that is not in `HOLD`: the step towards its goal, or in `CHASE` towards the target's position at the start of the tick, truncated as in Phase 1, and the exact rest when the aim is within one step) | P37a2, P39 (a unit is "moving" when its intent is not 0) |
| `push_x`, `push_y` (scratch) | -`separation_push_max`..`separation_push_max` | - | P39 | P37a2 (integrate; P26s docs, 2026-10-14, as merged, #94: `Movement.integrate` adds `push_x`/`push_y` to the intent for every row, `IDLE`, `HOLD` and `CHASE` rows included, so an idle row with a push moves; nothing clears the arrays (`prepare()` only sizes them, with zeros), so P39's `Separation.run` writes every row, or leaves the zeros) |

Projectile slots (`PROJECTILE_ARRAYS`, capacity `max_projectiles`): `proj_id` (0 = free, else from
`next_projectile_id`), `proj_owner`, `proj_weapon` (weapon index), `proj_target` (unit id or 0),
`proj_x`, `proj_y`, `proj_aim_x`, `proj_aim_y` (milli), `proj_ticks` (0.. at most
`max(1, ceil(max weapon range / min projectile speed))`, which is 17 with the shipped data:
shots fire only within range), `proj_free` (slot indices) with `proj_free_top`
(0..`max_projectiles`). Writer P42b, readers P42b and the view. Only live slots and the stack below
the top are hashed.

Map grid (`MAP_ARRAYS`, P29b; amended 2026-10-12 after the P29b and P29c reviews): `terrain`
(`PackedByteArray`, per cell an index into `terrain_ids`), `move_cost` (`PackedByteArray`, entry
`class * cells + cell`, 0 = blocked, 10 = normal, up to 40) and `terrain_cost` (`PackedByteArray`,
entry `terrain * 3 + class`, values 0..40: the costs `set_terrain` copies into `move_cost`).
`terrain_cost` is written only by `from_scenario` and `prepare()` (open grass: one terrain, 10 for
every class), so it is constant during a match. All three are hashed in the cached map digest
(P31b), which covers every `MAP_ARRAYS` entry; `terrain_cost` costs nothing extra there because it
never changes during a match, and it is not part of `defs_hash()`. `MatchSnapshot` copies only
`terrain` (with `terrain_ids`); `move_cost` and `terrain_cost` are `NOT_COPIED` (P38s). After
setup only `set_terrain` writes the grid (`terrain` and `move_cost`, with `map_version += 1`).

## File ownership (disjoint; nobody else edits these)
