P26k (2026-10-13): `scripts/cloud/install-android.sh` (P55b) |
| Shared, row by row | `docs/HOW_TO_PLAY.md` (rows listed below) |

Each PR commits only the `.uid`/`.import` files next to its owner's files. A change needed in
someone else's file goes in a "Requests" section of the PR, not into the diff.

**Named one-line exceptions** (the owner reviews them in the PR; without them `main` would go
red between two PRs):
1. P36 changes the guard path in `tests/project/test_scripts_compile.gd` from `TestMapView.gd`
   to `MapView.gd`. (P26l docs, 2026-10-13: P36's split is taken, so P36b, the scene swap, makes
   this change.)
2. P39b (separation enters the tick) changes two pair bounds to 90% of the radii sum (720 milli
   for two tanks, ADR 0008 §4), each one line with the reason in a comment: in
   `tests/scenes/test_controls_flow.gd` (qa-engineer) `is_greater_equal(spacing)` in
   `test_tap_ground_450_ticks_idle_slots`, and in `tests/input/test_player_controller.gd`
   (touch-ux-dev) `is_greater(1199.0)` in
   `test_the_ordered_tanks_stand_idle_on_distinct_slots_around_the_point_after_450_ticks`. The
   centre bounds next to them (3800 milli and `spacing * 3.17`) stay: the separation prototype
   on exactly this order ends with every tank at most 3794 milli from the point. Only if P39b's
   measurement exceeds a centre bound does the same exception set it to the measured maximum
   plus 100, with the number in the comment. qa-engineer and touch-ux-dev review these lines in
   P39b.
3. A sim PR after P49a that changes the seed-42 arena run (in this plan P46b, whose fog rules
   change who fires, and any later tuning PR) updates, with a note in the PR that says why they
   moved:
   - the golden hashes in `data/dev/perf_gate.tres`;
   - the run-outcome bounds in `tests/perf/test_sim_perf.gd` (`alive_end` and the projectile
     peak), only if the new measured values fall outside them, re-pinned around the new values
     with the margins P49a used.

   Nothing else in `tests/perf` moves under this exception. The deterministic cost bounds
   (including `FOG_CELLS`, measured in P49a with fog in the tick) and `budget.cfg` (P49b, after
   P46b) are never raised by a sim PR: if one is exceeded, the PR stops and asks qa-engineer in
   its "Requests" section, because catching that rise is what the bounds are for. If the rise is
   legitimate (a new tick phase, such as Phase 3's production and economy), qa-engineer raises
   the bound in its own PR, with before and after numbers and the reason recorded there (ADR 0012
   §4).
4. The PR that meets a ROADMAP box ticks it in `docs/ROADMAP.md` as a one-line edit the lead
   approves in review, as Phase 1 did: box 1 in P26, box 2 in P38, box 3 in P48 (or in the later
   of P44a and P44b), box 6 in P49b, box 4 in P46b, box 5 in P50b. The gate box is ticked only by
   the lead, after Michael writes that the gate passed.
5. The release run of the P26s probe (ADR 0012 §4) sets `ci_ratio_mean` and `ci_ratio_p95` in
   `data/dev/perf_gate.tres` (qa-owned) in a one-value PR by the lead; so does a later
   re-derivation from the first release perf-arena card, if the real ratio differs by more than
   10 %. No `budget.cfg` value moves with them (it is a ratchet, ADR 0012 §4). qa-engineer
   reviews those lines. Their twin, `flow_cells_per_tick`, stays a one-value PR by gameplay-dev in
   their own files (P26s) and, after P49a, re-pins the golden hashes under exception 3.
   The release run's in-frame mode gave 3.6 and 3.8 (2026-10-12, ADR 0012 acceptance note); the
   worker-mode run, on which ADR 0012 §4 defines them, gave 4.4 and 5.9 (revision 14, ADR 0012
   acceptance note addendum). P38t and P38p had not merged then, so they ship 4.4 and 5.9
   directly. An exception-5 PR is needed only if the first release perf-arena card changes them
   after P38p has merged; before that, the open PR carries the new values. The same holds for
   `ci_ratio_native_mean` and `ci_ratio_native_p95` (revision 15, ADR 0013 §15), which the first
   release perf-arena card re-derives by the same 10 % rule.
6. (Revision 15, ADR 0013 §9.) P53 adds the seed call to qa's `verify.sh` (with the build,
   `clang-format` version and format steps) and `run-tests.sh`, one line apart from `verify.sh`'s
   steps, plus `run-tests.sh`'s library check (exit 3 with `NATIVE LIBRARY MISSING: run bash
   scripts/build-native.sh first` when the generated `.gdextension` or its host library is
   absent), and updates qa's `tests/project/test_export_presets.gd` pin for `native/*`.
   qa-engineer reviews these lines. `run-sim-smoke.sh` gets its seed call when Phase 4 makes it
   start Godot.
7. (Revision 15, ADR 0013 §13.) P53's skeleton `register_types` and `SimKernels` in
   `native/sim_kernels/src/bind/`: release-engineer writes it, gameplay-dev reviews it and owns it
   from P54 on.
8. (Revision 15, ADR 0014 §5.) P50lp, only if it exists, adds the one field `pin_busy_pct` (85) to
   the lead's `game/core/PerfGateDef.gd` with its literal pin in `tests/core/test_timing_probe.gd`,
   and the one value to qa-engineer's `data/dev/perf_gate.tres`. The lead and qa-engineer review
   these lines in P50lp. Not taken (calibration 2026-10-13): ADR 0014's acceptance note records
   the per-frame method, not §5's fallback, so P50lp does not exist and this exception is unused.
9. (P26k, 2026-10-13.) P55b (release-engineer) changes the lead's `CLAUDE.md`: the
   `export-android.sh` line under "Commands" only, to name `scripts/cloud/install-android.sh` for
   a session that needs Android. The lead reviews the line, and the PR text names it; because
   `CLAUDE.md` changes only after Michael's yes, the line lands only with his "merge P55b".

No PR allowlists a kernel mismatch message in `scripts/verify-allowlist.txt` (ADR 0013 §2).

Other rules:
- The two gameplay-dev threads own disjoint files at any one time. Lane B's sim PRs (P28, P30a,
  P30b, P32) create new files only.
- If two open PRs both add a call line to `Sim.step`, the second rebases onto `main`. This is the
  only expected conflict.
- **Phase 1 tests that separation changes (audited).** Every Phase 1 test that runs two or more
  ticks with more than one unit (`run_ticks` or `Sim.step` in `tests/`) was checked against the
  separation rule (a pair pushes only when closer than the radii sum, 800 milli for two tanks;
  the controls block stands 1500 apart, so one tick from spawn never pushes). It also covers
  every Phase 1 test that calls a sim module other than `Sim.step` directly on a hand-built,
  unprepared World: only `test_formation` does (`FixedMath` and `Command` take no World,
  `from_scenario` ends with `prepare()`, and `test_world_setup`'s `World.new()` cases use only
  `add_unit` and `index_of`, which need no padded table). Rule: a sim entry point that a Phase 1
  test calls directly and that reads a padded table or the derived grid calls `world.prepare()`
  first.

## Work packages and PRs
Two lanes run in parallel (project instructions: at most two threads; P26p docs, 2026-10-14:
`docs/PROJECT_INSTRUCTIONS.md` is not in use, because Michael works in Claude Code cloud sessions,
not a claude.ai Project, and `CLAUDE.md` is the source of the rules; the lanes are unchanged, and
this note takes no decision on the two-thread limit; P26q docs, 2026-10-14: the two-thread limit
is superseded; how many lanes run at once is Michael's call through his start commands, three at
a time in practice since P33 (lead decision, "Answers and follow-ups (P26q docs)")). A lane is a
thread, so its owner changes from PR to PR:
- **Lane A** (the sim spine with its C++ ports, then verification; revision 15): P27a → P27b →
  P27c → P29a → P29a2 → P29b → P29c (merged) → P31a → P31b → P54 → P33 → P35a → P35b → P37a →
  P37a2 → P37b → P37n → (P37n2) → P39 → P39b → P39n → P41 → (P41g) → P41n → P42a → P42b → (P42n)
  → P48 → P46 → P46n → P49a → P51 → P51b → P51r → P46b → P49b → P52. A row in brackets is
  conditional (ADR 0013 §1's port rule, applied by the lead in the review of the named PR's
  `PERF` line); if P46's line fires the rule for a phase not yet ported, its row goes right after
  P46n. By the port-size rule (P26m, 2026-10-14), P37n3, P39n2, P41n2, (P42n2) and P46n2 are
  expected, and each takes the slot right after its port. If both FIRE and PROJECTILES fire,
  P42n's parts are P42n → P42n2 → P42n3 → P42n4 (P26n docs, "Split triggers").
- **Lane B** (standalone modules first, then the native build, the Node side and the view;
  revision 15): P28 → P27q → P34 → P34b → P34c (merged) → P30a → P30b → P32 → P32f → P53 → P55 →
  P55a → P55a2 → P55b → P56 → P36 → P36b → P38 → P38t → P38p → P30n → P30n2 → P38s → P38j → P40 → P43a → P43b → P54r → P38pn →
  P57 → P38w → P38w2 → P50a → P50a2 → P50a3 → (P50lp) → P45 → P44a → P44b → P47 → P47p → P50w →
  P50b. Only P30n sits before P38s, because P37n needs it; P54r comes after P43b (its self-check
  tests need P37n), and P38pn and P57 follow it because nothing waits for them before P50b.
  P50lp is not taken (calibration 2026-10-13), so its slot is empty. P26n2's outcome is recorded
  (ADR 0014's acceptance note, 2026-10-13), so P50a waits for no row outside the lane. A named
  split takes the slot right after its PR: P36b after P36, P38s2 after P38s, P38j2 after P38j,
  P38w1b after P38w (expected), P38w3 after P38w2; the same holds for P29ap, P29c (P29b's split,
  taken 2026-10-12) and P32f (expected), and for P34b and P34c (P34's split, 2026-10-12: P34 →
  P34b → P34c), and for P55a2 (P55a's split, taken by the lead 2026-10-13, P26j: P55a →
  P55a2), and for P36b (P36's split, taken 2026-10-13 in P36, #71; P26l docs: P36 → P36b), and for
  P30n2 (P30n's split at the lockstep, both merged 2026-10-14, #74 and #75; P26m: P30n → P30n2). A
  PR that depends on a split PR waits for all of its parts: "P32" in a Depends cell means P32
  and P32f, "P29b" means P29b and P29c, "P55a" means P55a and P55a2, and so on, whether
  or not the cell spells out "(and ...)". P55b (release-engineer, a new row of 2026-10-13, P26k:
  the cloud session's on-demand Android installer) takes the slot right after P55a2. It touches
  no other lane's files (as merged, #69; P26l docs: the new `scripts/cloud/install-android.sh` and
  `scripts/ci/test-install-android.sh`, the `ci.yml` step with its `test_ci_workflow.gd` pin, the
  headers of `export-android.sh` and `build-native.sh` (with the latter's missing-NDK message),
  one `install-ndk.sh` comment and its `--check` with cases in `test-native-scripts.sh`, the
  README, and the `CLAUDE.md` `export-android.sh` line under exception 9), and no row waits for
  it: no Depends cell names P55b, so P36 and the lane-B rows after it did not wait for its
  merge (merged 2026-10-13, #69). P56b and P56c (qa-engineer, new rows of 2026-10-14, P26n docs:
  the C++ scan's `view_pointer` rule and its qa follow-ups) take lane-B slots after P30n2 while
  P38 waits for lane A's P35b. They touch qa-engineer's test files (and P56b one line of its agent
  memory), and no row outside them waits for them (P56b merged 2026-10-14, #78; P56c merged 2026-10-14, #83, P26o docs).
  P57b (release-engineer, a new row of 2026-10-14, P26s docs, lead decision 2 in "Answers and
  follow-ups (P26s docs)") sits outside the arrow list: it waits for P57 and lane A's P37b, and
  no Depends cell names it, so P38w and the lane-B rows after it do not wait for P57b.
- **Outside the lane order:** P26l (release-engineer, revision 14), which touches no lane's files
  and lands now; no PR waits for it. P26n2 (the lead's calibration build on the never-merged spike
  branch; Michael ran it), done 2026-10-13: its outcome, the per-frame method, is recorded in ADR
  0014's acceptance note (P26g), which P50a implements. The lead's one-line docs
  follow-ups: the `clang-format` version number in `CLAUDE.md` after P53 merges (done in P26i,
  2026-10-13: 20.1.8), and ADR 0007 §4's status line if P41g's rule fires. P27f is withdrawn (revision 15: ADR 0013 is accepted, so
  ADR 0012 §5's rule gives 512 with the C++ slice, and P27b's 512 stays).
- **If lane B runs behind** (revision 15): P54 (lane A, after P31b) needs P53 (lane B, after
  P32f). If P53 has not merged when lane A reaches P54, lane A goes on with P33 and the rows after
  it and takes P54 as soon as P53 merges; nothing in lane A needs P54 before P37n.
- **Two gameplay-dev threads with C++** (revision 15): two port PRs are open together only when a
  conditional row or a later port overlaps another. Their files are disjoint (one
  `bind/kernel_<phase>.cpp` each) except one line each in `bind/kernel_list.h` and in
  `sim_impls.gd`'s `EXPECTED_PHASES`, their names in `bind/world_access.h`'s lists, and their
  dispatch cases in `test_kernel_abi.gd`: the expected rebase conflicts next to the `Sim.step`
  call line. `FlowCache.gd` joins them only if P37b opens before P30n has merged.

**The GDScript twin rule** (revision 15, ADR 0013 §5). Every GDScript row is built as written; it
is the twin (the web path, the readable spec and the lockstep oracle) and is never deleted after
its port. A port PR follows straight after its module's last main GDScript PR and adds the
dispatch as the first line of the phase's entry, the C++ kernel, its line in `bind/kernel_list.h`,
its bit in `sim_impls.gd`'s `EXPECTED_PHASES`, its case in P54's dispatch test and its lockstep
test. Once a port has merged, any row whose file list edits that module (whatever its title: in
Phase 2 P41 for `Movement.gd` after P37n, P46b for `Targeting.gd` after P41n, and every later
row) edits the C++ kernel in the same PR and passes its lockstep test there; its estimate grows
by the kernel edit and its cases, and the reviewer checks its Files line against the ports
merged by then. Every port names a split trigger (kernel | lockstep and crafted cases, unless the
row names another); the first part always holds the module's tests under both implementations,
and a `core/` function lands only with the kernel that calls it (ADR 0013 §2). (P26m,
2026-10-14: by Michael's port-size rule, "ja til port-reglen", the port rows P37n, P39n, P41n,
P42n and P46n are estimated at about 1,000 changed lines and all split at the lockstep, as P30n
and P30n2 did; "Split triggers" below.)

The probe P26s ran on the gate phone on 2026-10-12, right after P26 merged. It contradicted
ADRs 0008 and 0011, so P34, the first art PR, starts only after Michael accepts ADR 0012 (P26s
rule; P34's content does not change). Michael accepted it on 2026-10-12, so P34 and P27f are
unblocked. The sim PRs never waited for it. The snapshot PRs (P38s,
P38j) come before P40, because every view PR from P40 on reads the snapshot. The worker PRs (P38w,
P38w2) need only P38j and default to `SYNC`, so they are harmless wherever they merge; they sit
before P50a, whose card reads their fields. No scene runs the worker before P50b sets `AUTO`. By
then P40 and P43a have moved the last Phase 1 readers of `runner.world` (`UnitLayer`, `MapView`,
`PlayerController`, `SelectionPanel`) to the snapshot, and P50w has run the main scene on the
worker headless and added the snapshot-only scan. Nothing in either lane waits for Michael's
release run (ADR 0012 §7): the snapshot and worker PRs are needed in every outcome, and the CI
budget no longer depends on a phone ratio. P49 is split (ADR 0012 §4): P49a (bounds, golden
hashes, the printed phone estimate) is what P50b, P51 and P51b need; P49b (the wall-clock
ratchet) follows P46b, so it is calibrated on the final Phase 2 tick. Lane B starts with P28
because P29a needs `DetRandom`; nothing early waits for P27q. Lane B's sim modules have no
`World` dependency, so gameplay-dev runs two threads at once in the first week without touching
the same file. P37a2 (the first PR that changes the tick's movement) waits for P38p, so its
`PERF` line exists. P42a (the first deaths) needs P40 and P43a merged, so the view and the
selection handle removed rows before any unit can die. P39b (separation enters the tick) needs
P40, which re-aims the two Phase 1 `test_unit_layer` cases that separation would change. P46
(fog updates enter the tick) comes before P49a, so the cost bounds (including
`FOG_CELLS` and the `fog` phase) and the golden hashes are measured on the full tick; P47 then
needs only P46 and the view PRs, not the whole of lane A through P49a. P46b (fog rules) is the
one sim PR after P49a that changes the arena run, under exception 3. The lead proposes each thread
to Michael before it starts and merges only after "merge Px".

~Lines are first-commit estimates including tests, counted from the listed contents. Review
usually adds up to 30% (Phase 1). "Split trigger" names the cut if a first commit passes 400.
(P26n docs, 2026-10-14: by Michael's PR-size rule, in chat, verbatim **"ja til PR-reglen"**, the
400 counts changed lines of production code from now on; tests and fixtures don't count. The
~Lines column still counts tests.)

It accepts the SDK licences only with an explicit flag, used only when a session needs Android. Michael, in chat, 2026-10-13, verbatim: **"ja til install-android"** (the PR) and **"ja til SDK-licens"** (AppCode ApS accepts the Android SDK License Agreement). Added in P26k; it also changes `CLAUDE.md`'s `export-android.sh` line (named exception 9). No row waits for it (no Depends cell names P55b). **Merged** (#69, 2026-10-13; P26l docs). Its files: the new `scripts/cloud/install-android.sh [--accept-android-sdk-licenses]` and its offline test `scripts/ci/test-install-android.sh` (a `ci.yml` step of the verify job, pinned in `tests/project/test_ci_workflow.gd`); the headers of `scripts/export-android.sh` and `scripts/build-native.sh` (and the latter's missing-NDK message); one comment in `scripts/ci/install-ndk.sh` and its new `--check` (installs nothing and calls no sdkmanager; its cases are in `scripts/ci/test-native-scripts.sh`); the README; the `CLAUDE.md` `export-android.sh` line. The sdkmanager of cmdline-tools 23.0 records the licence acceptance by itself on any install, without asking (`--licenses` does nothing there), so the script's gate, which runs before any sdkmanager call (and before any download or new folder), is the only licence control: without the flag a missing SDK piece is exit 5. The Android CLI that the sdkmanager shim runs is pinned by version (1.0.16500706) and SHA-256 and passed in through `ANDROID_CLI_BIN`, so the shim never fetches the moving "latest". The export runs with `XDG_CONFIG_HOME=<sdk>/.godot-config`, because Godot copies `JAVA_HOME` and `ANDROID_HOME` only into a new settings file; that folder is one to delete if `JAVA_HOME` or `ANDROID_HOME` change. The real run, with the flag on Michael's consent and a fresh SDK folder: the install exits 0 in 77 s, the host and Android arm64 builds pass, `export-android: OK build/android/rts-mobile-debug.apk`, and a second run without the flag exits 0 in 0 s with nothing downloaded | no first-commit estimate (added in flight); 750 changed lines at merge (738+, 12−; about 436 of them the offline test) | P55a (and P55a2) | - |

    cases", could not be built: `SimKernels.flow` is `FlowCache.run`, so every bound call runs
    the recount and the slot pick in C++, and test-only entries are forbidden (ADR 0013 §2);
  - **The port-size rule** (Michael, 2026-10-14, "ja til port-reglen"; P26m): the native port
    rows P37n, P39n, P41n, P42n and P46n are estimated at about 1,000 changed lines each (P42n per
    kernel it holds), and each splits at the lockstep, as P30n and P30n2 did: part 1 is the
    kernel with its `core/` functions, its dispatch and the module's tests under both
    implementations, part 2 the lockstep test (with the crafted cases in it, as in P30n2 and
    ADR 0013 §2: "the second part adds the 900-tick lockstep and the crafted cases"). The split
    is the expected outcome for each:
    - P37n (expected): the INTEGRATE kernel with `best_neighbour` and `acquire` in `core/`, and
      `test_movement` under both | the lockstep test (P37n3);
    - P39n (expected): the kernel with its module tests under both | the lockstep test (P39n2);
    - P41n (expected): the kernel with its module tests under both | the lockstep test (P41n2);
    - P42n (expected): the kernel with `test_combat` and `test_counter_triangle` under both | the
      lockstep test (P42n2);
    - P46n (expected): the stamp, the kernel and the stamp cases under both | the 900-tick
      lockstep and the crafted cases (P46n2; unchanged, it already split at the lockstep);
    - the lines named before (P37n: integrate, slide, arrival and FIELD | stall and the ATTACK
      re-key; P39n: the kernel | the crowd cases and the lockstep; P41n: acquisition |
      engagement; P42n: FIRE | PROJECTILES) are replaced. P42n's row still holds only the phase
      whose rule fires; if both FIRE and PROJECTILES fire, P42n is first split per kernel
      (FIRE | PROJECTILES), and each part then splits at its lockstep. (P26n docs, 2026-10-14:
      the four parts are then P42n, the FIRE kernel with `test_combat` and
      `test_counter_triangle` under both | P42n2, its lockstep test | P42n3, the PROJECTILES
      kernel with the same module tests under both | P42n4, its lockstep test; about 2,000
      changed lines in all, about 1,000 per kernel as the row says, and ADR 0013 §5's status
      note of P26n docs sizes it the same way);
  - P37n2: `line_clear` in `core/` with its cases | the INTENTS kernel;
  - P49a: bounds, golden hashes and native timing | the GDScript-twin golden pass and its report
    line (P49a2);
  - P52: end-to-end scenes | cross-file facts (P52b).

### P26 docs: plan, ADRs, skill (lead)
- Files: this plan; `docs/adr/0007` to `0011`; the status lines in `docs/adr/0003` and `0004`;
  `.claude/skills/rts-sim-conventions/SKILL.md`; `docs/ROADMAP.md` (box 1); the architect memory
  files (`.claude/agent-memory/architect/MEMORY.md`, `project_phase2_decisions.md`,
  `pitfalls_phase2_research.md`), as Phase 1's docs PR carried them. Docs only, exempt from the
  ~400-line rule (lessons). After Michael's answers: an "Answers" section,
  the ADR status set to "accepted" with the acceptance note of ADR 0011 (thresholds), ROADMAP
  box 1 ticked, and:
  - one status line each in the accepted ADRs 0003 and 0004 (a status note, not a rewrite):
    ADR 0003 "§2-3 partly superseded by ADR 0007 (Command v1, World v1, removal)", ADR 0004
    "§5 partly superseded by ADR 0009 (a tap on an enemy issues `ATTACK`); §7 partly superseded
    by ADR 0011 §3 (selection rings are sprites of one ring texture, not drawn in code)";
  - the gate rule of answer 1 in ADR 0011 §5 and its acceptance note: Michael's own phone is the
    gate device for good, with the 2021 limits divided by k = 2.5. Acceptance does not wait for
    the P26s probe (Michael approved P26 before it ran), and no later phone revises the
    thresholds; a slower test phone needs a new ADR;
  - these `rts-sim-conventions` edits (one complete list):
    - Time and units: directions and the sine table arrive with the first rule that needs facing.
    - Randomness: xoshiro128\*\* (not PCG32 or xorshift64\*), `mul32` with parenthesised shifts,
      state as `PackedInt64Array`.
    - Commands: `payload`; validation per type; `MatchLoop` stamps tick and sequence.
    - Entities: stable compaction through `ROW_ARRAYS` (no swap-remove); projectiles in fixed
      slots with a free stack; `spawn_refused` and `projectile_overflow` instead of errors.
    - Movement: flow fields only when the straight line is blocked; staggered line checks;
      double-buffered fields with derived references; a 1-tile separation grid and a 4-tile
      targeting grid as linked lists (not one 2-tile grid).
    - Targeting: priority is the highest damage percent against the candidate's armour, then the
      smaller distance, then the lower id (no threat term); chase only while the line to the
      target is clear.
    - Fog: per-player visible counts (not bit grids) and explored bytes, one player per tick,
      dirty lists for the view; AI code reads the World only through `PlayerView` once it exists.
    - Determinism: SHA-256 through `HashingContext`; hashed lists cover helper objects; `hash_hex`
      is the only formatter; checkpoints every 15 ticks through `MatchLoop`; the replay header
      holds `defs_hash` and checkpoints; golden arena hashes.
    - Numbers and code: no int64 overflow, never a squared distance or a 32-bit unsigned word in a
      `PackedInt32Array`; in hot loops alias World arrays, inline lookups, never allocate; helper
      calls only at event cadence (line checks, `best_neighbour` into `nav_next`).
    - Data: `rules.tres` holds every tuning int.
    - Probe: `SimProbe.Phase` ids are fixed; a new phase takes an id in pipeline order through an
      ADR, never by renumbering.
- Phone check: none.

### P26s probe: phone perf probe (lead, draft PR, never merged)
- **Closed** (P26m): PR #35 (this probe) and PR #41 (the C++ spike P26n, which also carried P26n2)
  were closed unmerged on 2026-10-14 on Michael's word in chat, verbatim: **"luk #35 og #41"**.
  Their records stay where they are: ADR 0012's acceptance note and ADR 0013's Context (the
  probe's runs), ADR 0013's evidence comment on PR #41 and ADR 0014's acceptance note (the
  calibration reports, embedded verbatim). The comments of a closed PR stay readable, so every
  link to them holds. Their branches, `claude/p26s-perf-probe` and `claude/p26n-cpp-spike`, stay
  unmerged.
- Purpose: measure the gate phone before the art and view PRs build on the draw contracts.
  Runs right after P26 merges; P34 waits for its report, the sim PRs do not.
- Files (on the draft branch only): `scenes/dev/perf_probe.tscn` and its script, a dev menu
  button, `tests/perf/probe_tick.gd` (headless run of the same mock tick on CI).
- The scene: 150 rigs with the real tank SVGs at absolute z; 75 ring sprites (and a toggle to
  the Phase 1 code-drawn rings for comparison); 150 health bars as rects; 60 projectiles and 128
  effects as quads from a placeholder atlas; a 64x64 fog texture patched at 5 Hz; the optimised
  mock tick (ADR 0008 Context) run in the frame at 15 Hz; camera zoom 0.75 with every unit on
  screen; `Engine.max_fps = 60`.
- The card: frame interval p95 and on-time share, process p95, render CPU p95, draw calls, tick
  p95, CPU time on tick frames against other frames, `OS.get_model_name()`, refresh rate, and a
  "Copy report" button.
- Delivery: the lead publishes the probe's debug APK as the separate pre-release
  `android-perf-probe` (the CI artifact of the draft PR) and posts the web preview link. Michael
  installs it, runs it once, and pastes the report. The delivery message tells Michael, in plain
  words: "Uninstall the rts-mobile app on your phone first. The probe is signed with a different
  key than the normal test build (ADR 0005), so Android refuses to install it over the old one.
  After the probe, install the normal test build again from `android-dev-latest`." (Closed,
  history: delivered and run on 2026-10-12; PR #35 was closed unmerged on 2026-10-14, P26m, so
  there is nothing to install. P26n docs.)
- Output, posted by the lead as a comment in the P26s draft PR (the record P49a quotes), with
  `OS.get_model_name()` of the gate phone. It changes no gate threshold (ADR 0011's acceptance
  note):
  - the phone-to-CI speed ratios (ADR 0012 §4: the printed phone estimate, no longer a
    `budget.cfg` ceiling);
  - `flow_cells_per_tick`: the cell count whose slice costs at most about 0.2 ms on the gate
    phone (ADR 0008 §3; superseded by ADR 0012 §5: about 2 ms on the worker, so 256 from the
    debug run and 320 from the release run; 320 is superseded, revision 14: P27f is held;
    withdrawn in revision 15). If it differs from 512, gameplay-dev ships it as one value (in
    `data/rules.tres`, the `RulesDef` default and their pinned literals): inside P27b if P27b has
    not merged, else as its own one-value PR;
  - the draw calls and frame numbers against the ring, atlas and fog-patch contracts. A contract
    the probe contradicts changes through a new ADR before P34 starts.
- Phone check: uninstall the current rts-mobile build, install the probe APK, then Dev → Perf
  probe, wait 60 s, tap "Copy report" and paste it in chat. (Closed, history: done 2026-10-12;
  PR #35 closed unmerged on 2026-10-14, P26m. P26n docs.)
- **Outcome (2026-10-12, PR #35; ADR 0012 Context):** the gate phone is a Pixel 9a. On the debug
  build the render side passed (13 draw calls with sprite rings, render CPU 1.94 ms p95, fog patch
  0.29 ms). The sim side failed: every tick frame missed (20.6 ms p95, 83.5 % of frames on time),
  and the mock tick took 15.3 ms p95. The phone-to-CI ratio is 4.4. A slice of 0.2 ms would hold
  only about 28 cells, and alternating team tints cost 161 calls. That contradicts ADRs 0008 §3
  and 0011 (budget, gate, team-tint batching), so ADR 0012 decides:
  - the sim tick moves to a worker thread with a view snapshot;
  - the gate gets new numbers and is measured on the release template;
  - `flow_cells_per_tick` is 256 at proposal, 320 after the release run (superseded, revision
    14: 64 by the headroom rule, P27f held; withdrawn in revision 15);
  - the draw z is per batch group.
- **Release run** (in-frame mode done 2026-10-12; the worker mode is still open and needs a new
  build that adds it; closed, history: the worker mode ran on 2026-10-12, "Worker-mode run"
  below, and PR #35 was closed unmerged on 2026-10-14, P26m; P26n docs): the same probe built on
  the release template,
  with a worker mode added first (ADR 0012 §4; about 40 lines on this draft branch:
  `PerfProbeTick.step()` on a dedicated `Thread` with the two-semaphore hand-off, one tick ahead,
  the view unchanged, and a mode toggle on the card). Michael runs both modes in the same session.
  The card adds the worker job mean and p95, held ticks, the main thread's frame work with the
  tick out of the frame, and draw calls and render CPU, which must read above 0 on release. The
  lead posts it in PR #35. It sets the data values of ADR 0012 §4:
  - `ci_ratio_mean` (worker job mean / 2.51 ms) and `ci_ratio_p95` (worker job p95 / 3.36 ms):
    the lead's one-value PR (exception 5);
  - `flow_cells_per_tick` (the ADR 0012 §5 headroom rule): gameplay-dev's one-value PR in
    `data/rules.tres`, the `RulesDef` default and their pinned literals, if it differs from 320
    (P27f's value; superseded, revision 14: P27f is held; withdrawn in revision 15).

  It changes no gate threshold, and no lane waits for it. If it arrives before P27f opens, P27f
  ships the release value directly, so the value changes only once.
- **Release run outputs, in-frame mode** (2026-10-12, PR #35, Pixel 9a, release template, mode
  1; recorded by the lead, the record P49a quotes; the full table is in ADR 0012's acceptance
  note):
  - mock tick mean / p95 9.02 / 12.63 ms (debug 10.96 / 15.33 ms, 18 % faster); flow slice of
    512 cells 3.13 ms mean; 90.5 % of frames on time (the tick still in the frame); work p95 on
    the other frames 4.18 ms; render CPU mean 1.65 ms; 13 draw calls. Draw calls and render CPU
    read above 0 on release, so there is no finding for P50a's render line;
  - CI side (same mock, GitHub runner, min of 3): 2.51 ms mean, 3.36 ms p95;
  - ratios (rounded up to one decimal): `ci_ratio_mean` 9.02 / 2.51 = 3.6, `ci_ratio_p95`
    12.63 / 3.36 = 3.8 (superseded by the worker-mode values 4.4 and 5.9, revision 14, which
    P38t and P38p ship);
  - `flow_cells_per_tick`: floor(512 x 2.0 / 3.13 / 64) x 64 = 320 by the 2 ms rule (the
    worker-mode run then gave 64 by the headroom half, and P27f is held, revision 14; withdrawn
    in revision 15). The headroom condition (worker job p95 plus the slice at or below 12.0 ms) needs the
    worker-mode p95. 320 holds only if that p95 is at or below 10.0 ms; above 11.6 ms the value
    becomes 64 with an early-warning finding (risk 1). The in-frame p95 is already 12.63 ms;
  - the margin: 12.63 ms is inside `sim_p95_ms` (13.3 ms) by about 5 %, and the worker's
    measured 4-5 % extra cost on CI would use that up, so the real tick is expected to be
    marginal (risk 1). (Superseded, revision 14: the worker-mode job p95 of 19.75-20.10 ms
    misses 13.3 ms by 48-51 %, which is risk 1's early-warning finding; see the next bullet.)
- **Worker-mode run (done 2026-10-12, revision 14):** PR #35 release run 2, job mean 11.03 ms
  and p95 19.75 ms (spike PR #41: p95 20.10 ms). The ratios become 4.4 and 5.9 (P38t and P38p
  ship them), and the headroom rule gives `flow_cells_per_tick` 64; P27f is held (see P27f;
  withdrawn in revision 15, when ADR 0013's C++ slice keeps 512).

### P27q test: sim purity v2 (qa-engineer, lane B)
- Files: `tests/project/test_sim_purity.gd` (+ fixtures).
- Adds to the existing scan of `game/sim`:
  - bans `hash(` as a call, with fixtures that keep `state_hash(`, `defs_hash(` and `hash_hex(`
    legal;
  - if ADR 0012 is accepted before this PR opens (otherwise P38p carries them; P26r docs,
    2026-10-14: P38p carried them, #92, section P38p "As merged"): bans
    `static var`, `signal ` (a declaration), `.emit(`, `call_deferred(`, `load(` and
    `preload(` as calls, `ResourceLoader`, `ResourceSaver`, `Thread`, `Mutex`, `Semaphore`,
    `WorkerThreadPool` and `await` anywhere in `game/sim`. These are the thread-safety rules of
    ADR 0012 §2. Each has one fixture that is flagged, plus legal look-alikes (`static func`, a
    comment, names such as `emit_count`, `payload(`, `unloaded`, `thread_id`). A grep of
    `game/sim` on 2026-10-12 finds none of them, so no sim code changes;
  - an indentation-aware allocation check: inside the body of a `for` or `while`, flags array
    and dictionary literals, `PackedInt32Array(`, `PackedByteArray(`, `PackedInt64Array(`,
    `.duplicate()` and `.slice(`, unless the line ends with `# alloc-ok`; fixtures for both
    outcomes (a nested loop, a literal after the loop at the outer indentation, a comment line,
    an index expression such as `world.pos_x[row]` or `pos_x[row] = x` inside a loop (legal, the
    Phase 1 `Sim._move_units` style), and an inline array literal such as `x = [1, 2]` or
    `[1, -1][k]` inside a loop (flagged)).
- Phone check: none.

### P28 sim: DetRandom (gameplay-dev, lane B)
- Files: `game/sim/DetRandom.gd`, `tests/sim/reference/det_random_ref.py` (the transcription
  that produces the seeding literals; not run by verify).
- Tests `tests/sim/test_det_random.gd`:
  - `set_state([1, 2, 3, 4])`, then the first 6 `next_u32()` equal the published reference
    outputs (from Vigna's `xoshiro128starstar.c` compiled in a scratch folder; the command and
    the numbers go in the PR description).
  - The first 5 `next_u32()` of `create(0, 1)` and `create(42, 1)` equal the literals of
    `det_random_ref.py`.
  - `mul32` (through a test hook) equals `(a * b) & 0xFFFFFFFF` for a table of operand pairs
    including `0xFFFFFFFF`, and `rotl` likewise.
  - Streams 1 and 101 of one seed differ. `create(2^31 + 5, 1)` equals `create(5, 1)`.
  - 100 000 draws are all in [0, 2^32). Over 100 000 draws, `next_int(10)` puts each bucket
    within 9 700-10 300 (deterministic, so never flaky).
  - `next_int(0)` and `next_int(-3)` return 0; `range_int(-5, 5)` stays inside the bounds.
  - `shuffle` gives a permutation, and the same seed gives the same permutation; `pick_random`
    of an empty array returns 0.
  - `state()`/`set_state()` round-trip with words above 2^31, and continuing after them gives
    the same sequence.
- Phone check: none.

### P27f data: `flow_cells_per_tick` from ADR 0012 (gameplay-dev, one value)
- **Withdrawn (revision 15).** Michael accepted ADR 0013 on 2026-10-12. With the C++ slice (0.21 ms
  per 512 cells on the phone) and the C++ job p95 (0.88 ms), ADR 0012 §5's rule gives the 512
  cap, which P27b already ships, so no data PR follows (ADR 0013 §15). The rest of this section
  is history.
- **Held (revision 14).** The 320 below is superseded: the worker-mode run's job p95 (19.75-20.10
  ms) is above 11.6 ms, so ADR 0012 §5's headroom half gives 64. P27f waits for Michael's answer
  on ADR 0013: withdrawn (512 stays) if it is accepted, 64 if it is rejected. Nothing waits for
  it. The rest of this row is the revision 11 text, kept for the files it names.
- After ADR 0012 is accepted (2026-10-12: unblocked), outside the lane order (lane A takes it
  between any two of its PRs). It sets `flow_cells_per_tick` to **320** (superseded, above): ADR 0012 §5's 2 ms rule
  with the release run's 512-cell slice mean of 3.13 ms (floor(512 x 2.0 / 3.13 / 64) x 64; the
  debug run's 256 is superseded). The headroom half of the §5 rule needs the worker-mode run;
  if that gives a lower value, gameplay-dev ships it as a one-value follow-up (no exception before
  P49a). Nothing waits for P27f, so lane A may also hold it until the worker-mode run, so the
  value changes only once. It changes
  `data/rules.tres`, the `RulesDef` script default (`game/sim/defs/RulesDef.gd`) and the pinned
  literals in `tests/sim/test_defs.gd` (P27a) and `tests/sim/test_defs_data.gd` (P27b), all
  gameplay-dev's own files.
- If it merges before P49a (expected), no golden hashes exist yet and no exception applies. If
  it merges after P49a, or a later release-run value does, it re-pins them under exception 3.
- `FlowField` and `FlowCache` tests pass explicit budgets, so they do not move.
- Phone check: none (no field is computed on the open controls map).

### P30a sim: flow field (gameplay-dev, lane B)
- (Revision 15: read with its entry in the amendment's "ADR 0013: changes to existing rows";
  where they differ, the amendment wins.)
- Files: `game/sim/FlowField.gd`.
- Tests `tests/sim/test_flow_field.gd`:
  - On an open 16x16 grid the costs equal the octile distance (10 per orthogonal step, 14 per
    diagonal).
  - A wall with one gap: `best_neighbour` chains from the far side lead through the gap.
  - No corner cutting past a blocked orthogonal neighbour.
  - Scrub is reachable for FOOT and `UNREACHED` for TRACKED; an enclosed cell stays `UNREACHED`.
  - Slicing: on a 64x64 grid, `advance(1024)` completes after exactly 4 calls, `advance(512)`
    after 8, and the costs equal one `advance(100000)`.
  - Double buffer: during a re-integration `cost` keeps the old values until completion, then
    holds the new ones; array sizes do not change after the first `begin` (no allocation).
  - The cells settled per call never exceed the budget.
  - `cost` has `(cols + 2) * (rows + 2)` entries; the padding ring is `UNREACHED`; `begin` and
    `best_neighbour` take and return plain cells.
- Phone check: none.

### P29c sim: World v1, part 4 (gameplay-dev)
- P29b's named split (2026-10-12). Files: `game/sim/World.gd` (`from_scenario` v1 tables, rules
  through `_copy_rules`, roster, `player_count`, jitter, `terrain_ids`, `const GRASS_ID`, events
  cleared, `prepare()` at the end of `from_scenario`), `game/sim/defs/SpawnDef.gd` and
  `game/sim/defs/WeaponDef.gd` (comments).
- Fills, the setup rules of the contract row `World` v1 (P31a's helper sizing and P32's fog
  blocks code against them):
  - type, weapon and rules tables from the scenario (rules from `scenario.rules` through
    `_copy_rules`, else the `_init()` defaults); spawn types, then roster types; a weapon is
    shared by its id, the first definition wins;
  - `terrain_ids` from the terrain set; open grass is `[&"grass"]` (`const World.GRASS_ID`, pinned
    against terrain 0 of `data/terrain/standard.tres`); `MapView` (P36) and
    `MatchSnapshot.terrain_ids` rely on it;
  - the map rules are P29b's: a map that fails `validate()` (a cost outside 0..40 included) is
    open grass of `map_size_tiles`, so P31a and P32 find every sizing rule in one place;
  - `player_count` is the scenario value raised to the highest spawn owner (0 = the highest spawn
    owner);
  - spawns run before `prepare()`, so `add_unit` refuses nothing: `from_scenario` neither stops at
    `max_rows` nor counts `spawn_refused` during setup. Data keeps the spawn total within
    `max_rows` (a data test);
  - spawn jitter: two draws per unit (x, then y) whenever `jitter > 0`, none for 0; the point is
    clamped into the map, and when the clamped tile is not passable for the unit's class the unit
    starts on its unjittered grid point. Data keeps the jitter in 0..8000, because
    `DetRandom.range_int` draws nothing for a span above 2^31, and every grid point inside the map
    and passable for its class (a data test);
  - `from_scenario` ends with `prepare()` and then clears the setup's `SPAWNED` events.
- Tests in a new file `tests/sim/test_world_scenario.gd` (gdlint's max-public-methods is 20,
  which `test_world_v1.gd` would exceed), plus the `GRASS_ID` pin in `tests/sim/test_world_v1.gd`
  and one case in `tests/sim/test_defs_data.gd`:
  - the arena's unit, weapon and terrain tables equal the data; the rules come from the scenario,
    else the defaults, and size the fixed arrays; roster types follow the spawn types, and a
    weapon is shared by its id;
  - `player_count` is the scenario value raised to the highest spawn owner (0 or a negative value
    counts as 0, so the highest owner wins);
  - jitter draws x then y for each unit of a spawn with a jitter and nothing for jitter 0; the
    arena jitter stays within 300 and follows the seed, and a null rng equals the seed-0 sim
    stream; a draw outside the map is clamped, and a
    blocked tile keeps the grid point;
  - `from_scenario` ends with `prepare()` and drops the spawn events;
  - data (`test_defs_data`): every shipped scenario places each grid point inside the map and on
    ground its class may enter, keeps the jitter in 0..8000, the spawn total within `max_rows`,
    and `player_count` 0 or at least the highest spawn owner.
- Size: 435 changed lines measured, about 340 of them tests.
- Phone check: none.

### P30b sim: flow cache (gameplay-dev, lane B)
- (Revision 15: read with its entry in the amendment's "ADR 0013: changes to existing rows";
  where they differ, the amendment wins.)
- Files: `game/sim/FlowCache.gd`; as merged (#58), also `game/sim/FlowField.gd` (`reserve()`
  clears `complete` when it resizes, the optional hardening of the amendment's P30b entry) and
  `tests/sim/test_flow_field.gd` (one case: a map of another size ends `complete` even for the
  same key) (P26i, 2026-10-13, P30b review).
- Tests `tests/sim/test_flow_cache.gd`:
  - the same key shares a slot; `recount` from a field column gives each slot's references;
  - LRU eviction takes only unreferenced slots, with ties to the lower slot; a full cache
    returns -1;
  - `advance` serves one field at a time in request order within the budget;
  - `invalidate`: referenced slots re-integrate (old costs served until the swap), unreferenced
    slots are dropped;
  - array sizes do not change after `configure` and the first fields (`configure()` calls
    `field.reserve(cols, rows)` on every slot);
  - a slot dropped by `invalidate` and re-acquired for its old key on the new `map_version`
    serves nothing until it completes (`acquire()` clears `complete` before `begin()`; P26h,
    2026-10-13, see the amendment's P30b entry).
- As merged (P26i, 2026-10-13, P30b review): `acquire()` counts the slot it hands out as
  referenced until the next `recount()`, so two new keys in one tick never share a slot (a
  decision of the lead, "Lead decisions and answers (P26i)"; a test case pins it).
- Phone check: none.

### P32 sim: spatial and fog grids (gameplay-dev, lane B)
- (Revision 15: read with its entry in the amendment's "ADR 0013: changes to existing rows";
  where they differ, the amendment wins.)
- Files: `game/sim/SpatialGrid.gd`, `game/sim/FogGrid.gd`.
- Tests:
  - `tests/sim/test_spatial_grid.gd`:
    - every row is in the list of its cell, ascending within the cell;
    - units on cell and map edges land correctly;
    - a rebuild after units moved leaves no row in its old cell (only occupied cells reset);
    - a rebuild with fewer units keeps the array sizes.
  - `tests/sim/test_fog_grid.gd`:
    - a 6000 mask covers exactly the tiles with `(dx*1000)^2 + (dy*1000)^2 <= 6000^2`, compared
      with a brute force over the square;
    - masks clip at the map edges;
    - two overlapping discs, then one unstamped: the overlap stays visible; explored stays 1;
    - players are independent, and player 1 and player `player_count` use the first and last
      blocks (checked at both grid ends);
    - `version_of` increments only when a cell changes visible or explored state;
    - the dirty list holds exactly the flipped cells, `begin_update` clears it, and overflow sets
      the flag;
    - `copy_into` (ADR 0012 §3): after a copy, every read method of the copy answers as the
      source does. A later `stamp` or `begin_update` on the source changes nothing in the copy,
      and every array of `HASHED` and `UNHASHED` on the copy is a distinct object
      (`is_same` false).
- Size: about 425 with `copy_into` (about 25 lines), so the named split (P32f, `FogGrid` with
  `copy_into`) is the expected outcome. If P32 has merged before ADR 0012 is accepted,
  `copy_into` becomes a 40-line gameplay-dev PR before P38s.
- Phone check: none.

### P31a sim: helpers, removal, events (gameplay-dev)
- Files: `game/sim/World.gd` (helper members `fog`, `flow`, `sep_grid`, `target_grid` and their
  configuration in `prepare()`; `remove_dead` over `ROW_ARRAYS`; event helpers),
  `game/sim/Sim.gd` (`prepare()` first, clear events and stats, `remove_dead` last).
- Tests `tests/sim/test_world_v1.gd` (part 4) and `tests/sim/test_removal.gd`:
  - Removal: removing rows 2 and 5 of 8 keeps the others in id order with every `ROW_ARRAYS`
    array 6 long; `index_of` finds the survivors and returns -1 for the dead; `rows_version`
    increments; each dead row writes one `DIED` record with its position, type and owner;
    `stats[ROWS_COMPACTED]` counts the moved rows.
  - `prepare()` is idempotent and sizes fog, flow and grids from the World; after it, `add_unit`
    refuses an owner above `player_count` and a row beyond `max_rows` (`spawn_refused`).
  - A world from a hand-built `UnitDef` (no `max_hp` set) survives 10 `Sim.step` calls.
  - The Phase 1-shaped 10-tick case of P29a gets a twin without the explicit `prepare()` (the
    proof that `Sim.step` prepares a hand-built world).
  - The shape-list coverage case now includes the helper members (an object property is in no
    shape list; P31b adds their hashed lists).
- The helper configure (P26h, 2026-10-13, P32 review): `configure()` of `SpatialGrid`, `FogGrid`
  and `FlowCache` is O(cells) and re-creates or resets their arrays (`FogGrid.configure()`
  re-creates the hashed `visible` and `explored` and `listed`), and `Sim.step` calls `prepare()`
  every tick. ADR 0007 §2's one bool check gates all of it: `prepare()` configures `fog`,
  `flow`, `sep_grid` and `target_grid` once, behind that check, so no configure runs again
  inside `Sim.step` and no helper needs a size guard of its own. A World whose size, players or
  `max_rows` change after `prepare()` is not supported (as for a type appended after it). The
  separation grid has 1000-milli cells and the
  targeting grid 4000-milli cells: the 1-tile and 4-tile cells of ADR 0008 §4 and ADR 0009
  (§"Candidates"). Neither comes from `data/`: `RulesDef` has no grid field (the 4000 in
  `data/rules.tres` is `attack_rekey_milli`, unrelated). They are structural sizes fixed by
  those ADRs, not balance numbers, and the targeting code (P41) reads `target_grid.cell_milli`,
  never a second 4000. The idempotence case also asserts that a second `prepare()` keeps a
  rebuilt grid's lists, a stamped fog's `visible`, `explored` and `listed`, and the flow slots
  (`is_same` on the arrays, equal contents).
- Flow invalidation (a decision of the lead, P26i, 2026-10-13, P30b review; "Lead decisions and
  answers (P26i)"): `World.set_terrain()` calls `flow.invalidate(map_version)` right after it
  bumps `map_version`, once the World is prepared. `FlowCache.run` (P30n) does not call
  `invalidate()`. P31a implements the call.
- **As merged** (P26j, 2026-10-13, P31a review, #62; the contract's `World` row says the same):
  - `fog` (`FogGrid`), `flow` (`FlowCache`), `sep_grid` and `target_grid` (`SpatialGrid`) are
    created at their declaration, so they are never null, and are configured once by
    `prepare()` behind `var prepared: bool` (ADR 0007 §2's one bool check). Before the first
    `prepare()` they exist unconfigured.
  - `const SEP_CELL_MILLI: int = MILLI_PER_TILE` and `const TARGET_CELL_MILLI: int = 4 *
    MILLI_PER_TILE` are the only source of the two cell sizes. P41 and the kernels read
    `sep_grid.cell_milli` and `target_grid.cell_milli`, never the constant or a literal.
  - `test_world_v1.gd`'s coverage case skips the World members that are neither an `int` nor a
    `Packed*Array`: `prepared` and the helper objects, beside `type_ids`, `weapon_ids`,
    `terrain_ids` and `rng` (it pins that list by name).
  - `Sim.step` calls `prepare()`, then clears `events` and `stats` at the start of every tick,
    so the `SPAWNED` records of a hand-built world's setup are gone after tick 0
    (`from_scenario` already cleared them). `remove_dead()` calls `prepare()` first (a
    hand-built world), writes one `DIED` record per dead row, compacts every `ROW_ARRAYS` column,
    bumps `rows_version` and counts `Stat.ROWS_COMPACTED` (ADR 0007 §4's status note). Unstamping
    a dead unit's fog stays in P46.
- Phone check: none.

### P31b sim: state hash (gameplay-dev)
- Files: `game/sim/World.gd` (`state_hash`, `hash_parts`, `hash_hex`, `defs_hash`, the cached map
  digest); P26h: the `HASHED` and `UNHASHED` const lists in `game/sim/FlowField.gd`,
  `game/sim/SpatialGrid.gd` and `game/sim/FlowCache.gd` (the helper-walk case below).
- Tests `tests/sim/test_world_hash.gd`:
  - Coverage: every `int` and `Packed*Array` property of World and of each helper object is in
    exactly one list (dotted names such as `fog.visible`).
  - The helper walk (P26h, 2026-10-13, P30a, P32 and P32f reviews) covers each helper's ints
    as well as its Packed arrays, private members included (`get_property_list()` lists them).
    `FogGrid` already declares both lists (P32f): `HASHED` = `visible`, `explored`; `UNHASHED`
    = its 8 other Packed arrays and its 5 ints (`cols`, `rows`, `cells`, `player_count`,
    `dirty_capacity`). `FlowCache`, each `FlowField` slot (reached through `FlowCache`) and both
    `SpatialGrid`s are derived and never hashed (ADR 0007 §2's helper row and §8, ADR 0008 §3).
    Per ADR 0007 §8 every reachable helper declares its own lists, so P31b gives each of the
    three classes `const HASHED: Array[StringName] = []` and an `UNHASHED` naming every int
    and Packed array it has: `FlowField` the six `STATE_ARRAYS` and `goal_cell`, `move_class`,
    `grid_cols`, `cell_count`, `stride`, `level`, `pending`; `SpatialGrid` `head`, `next`,
    `_occupied`, `cols`, `rows`, `cell_milli`, `_capacity`, `_occupied_count`, `_inserted`;
    `FlowCache` (as P30b merged it; P26i, 2026-10-13) `HASHED = []`, like `SpatialGrid`, and
    `UNHASHED` = the ints `cols`, `rows`, `classes`, `requests` plus its `STATE_ARRAYS` (the
    seven Packed arrays `key_goal`, `key_class`, `key_version`, `last_used`, `ref_count`,
    `requested`, `status`); `fields` (the typed `Array[FlowField]`, through which the walk
    reaches each slot) and the `Status` enum need no list. `FlowField.UNHASHED` also names the bool
    `complete`, so P54's name test (every fetched or set name is in a helper's lists) finds it.
    For helpers the walk therefore collects `TYPE_BOOL` script variables as well as ints and
    Packed arrays and keeps the exact two-way check of P32f's
    `test_every_int_and_packed_array_is_listed_once` (found == `HASHED` + `UNHASHED`): a
    helper's bool member must be listed. This differs on purpose from World's rule (a World
    member that is neither an int nor a Packed array sits in no list, `test_world_v1.gd`);
    `FogGrid` and `SpatialGrid` have no bool member today, so their lists are unchanged. One case per helper: changing one of its unhashed
    members leaves the hash unchanged. Files gain those const lists in `game/sim/FlowField.gd`,
    `game/sim/SpatialGrid.gd` and `game/sim/FlowCache.gd` (about +20 lines; no port exists yet,
    so no twin edit).
  - Equal worlds hash equal. Changing one element of each hashed array, each hashed scalar and
    `rng` state changes the hash; changing an unhashed one, or a free projectile slot, does not.
    `set_terrain` changes it (map digest). One changed entry of each `MAP_ARRAYS` array
    (`terrain`, `move_cost`, `terrain_cost`) changes the `"map"` part of `hash_parts()` once the
    digest is recomputed (the case bumps `map_version`).
  - `hash_hex` of 0, 1, -1, -2 and `-9223372036854775808` gives 16 lower-case hex digits
    (`"0000000000000000"`, `"0100000000000000"`, `"ffffffffffffffff"`, `"feffffffffffffff"`,
    `"0000000000000080"`).
  - `hash_parts` names the differing section when one array differs.
  - The hash of `controls_test` at tick 0 with seed 1 is pinned as a literal. It is updated only
    in a PR that changes the layout or the scenario, with a note.
  - `defs_hash` changes when a weapon's damage changes.
- The PR states the measured hash cost for the arena at tick 0.
- `map_version` is already in `HASHED_SCALARS` (P29a2, ADR 0007 §2), so the header section
  carries it and the map section carries only the cached digest. The digest covers every
  `MAP_ARRAYS` entry in list order: `terrain`, `move_cost` and `terrain_cost` (amended
  2026-10-12 after the P29b review; `terrain_cost` is constant during a match and only a few bytes
  (15 for the arena), so it needs no special case, and it is not part of `defs_hash()`). The
  digest's members join a list, and P31b updates the pinned lists and property counts of the
  coverage test in `tests/sim/test_world_v1.gd` (P29a2) in the same PR; the counts it starts from
  are P29b's 55 `Packed*Array` properties and 29 ints (P26j, 2026-10-13: still current after
  P31a, whose members are objects and a bool; the merged `test_world_v1.gd` pins 55 and 29).
- **The helpers as P31a merged them** (P26j, 2026-10-13, recorded for P31b, which is in flight;
  no change to its design): `fog` is hashed through `FogGrid.HASHED` (`fog.visible`,
  `fog.explored`); `flow`, `sep_grid` and `target_grid` are derived (as merged they declare
  no such lists; P31b gives each `HASHED = []` plus an `UNHASHED` list, above). All four exist from `World.new()` on but stay unconfigured (empty
  arrays) until the first `prepare()`, so a walk over an unprepared World meets empty helper
  arrays, never a null helper.
- **As merged** (P26k, 2026-10-13, P31b review, #65; recorded, no change to the design):
  `World.RULE_SCALARS` (the 15 rule ints, pinned against `RulesDef`, which `defs_hash()` feeds)
  and `World.HASH_OTHER_SECTIONS = 4` (header, map, projectiles, fog: 23 sections with the 19
  `ROW_ARRAYS` columns) are new; the digest cache ints `_map_digest` and `_map_digest_version`
  joined `UNHASHED_SCALARS`, so the counts are 55 `Packed*Array` properties and 31 ints (32 once
  P54 adds `native_phases`); the block layout (`[tag, byte count]` as two little-endian int64,
  then the bytes; two blocks under one tag for the projectiles and for the fog; an empty block
  feeds only its 16-byte prefix, since `HashingContext.update` rejects an empty chunk) is the
  one the contract's `World` row records. `prepare()` resets the digest cache and computes the
  digest; `defs_hash()` also covers `type_ids`, `weapon_ids` and the display names
  (`type_names`). Every `*_ARRAYS`, `*_SCALARS`, `HASHED` and `UNHASHED` list of World and the
  four helpers is a literal `Array[StringName]` (20 lists, `is_typed()` pinned), because Godot
  4.7.2 drops the element type of a typed const built with `+`. `FlowCache.fields` is in no list
  on purpose; P54's name test checks it by its kind (row P54, P26k). The measured hash cost is in
  ADR 0007 §8's status note.
- Phone check: none.

### P33 sim: Command v1 and the pipeline (gameplay-dev)
- Files: `game/sim/Command.gd`, `game/sim/SimProbe.gd` (`enum Phase`, `PHASE_NAMES`, `begin`,
  `mark`), `game/sim/Sim.gd` (ADR 0007 §4 order with the phases that exist; Phase 1 movement
  until P37a2, marked `INTEGRATE`; per-type validation), `game/sim/Formation.gd` (unchanged
  unless needed), `tests/sim/recording_probe.gd` (a `SimProbe` that appends `-1` for `begin()`
  and the id for each `mark`).
- `ATTACK` before P41: the units take order `ATTACK` and `order_target`, and their goal follows
  the target's position each tick.
- Tests:
  - `tests/sim/test_command.gd`: v1 factories, field defaults, `compare` unchanged. Every factory
    duplicates the arrays it is given (`unit_ids`, and `payload` where it takes one; ADR 0012
    §2, "Commands change hands once"): one case per factory changes the caller's array after the
    call and checks that the Command's array is unchanged.
  - `tests/sim/test_sim_step.gd`:
    - Each drop rule, counted, with no `push_error`: player 0 or above `player_count`; 0 or 257
      ids; 17 payload ints; an unknown type 5 and a reserved type 10 (also with zero ids and with
      a speed-0 row: dropped as unknown, not as a bad group); `ATTACK` on an own unit, a neutral
      unit and an unknown id.
    - `MOVE` reaches the same slots as `ATTACK_MOVE` (compared between two runs, so it holds
      after separation); `STOP` makes moving units idle where they are; `ATTACK` follows a
      moving target (one attacker, so no pair ever overlaps).
    - Probe ids: `Phase.COUNT == 11`, `PHASE_NAMES.size() == Phase.COUNT`, and the 11 ids and
      names literally. A recording probe sees `begin()` once, then marks in strictly ascending
      id order, each at most once per tick, here exactly `COMMANDS`, `INTEGRATE`, `REMOVE`.
      Every later phase PR adds one assertion here that its id is marked (P37a2 `INTENTS`, P37b
      `FLOW`, P39b `GRIDS` and `SEPARATION`, P41 `TARGETING`, P42a `FIRE`, P42b `PROJECTILES`,
      P46 `FOG`). Hashes with and without a probe are equal.
    - All Phase 1 cases stay green unchanged. Together with the Phase 1-shaped 10-tick case of
      `test_world_v1` (P29a, P31a), they are the proof that hand-built worlds with only the five
      v0 type arrays still step.
- Phone check: the preview behaves as before (ground taps still attack-move).
- **As merged** (P26n docs, 2026-10-14; #77, `67401d4`): 712 changed lines (663+, 49−), about
  265 production lines in `game/sim` and about 447 of tests, against this row's 340; the named
  split was not taken. That is within the PR-size rule (Michael, 2026-10-14, "ja til
  PR-reglen"), and Michael accepted it by merging it on 2026-10-14 ("merge P33, P56b, P26m").
  Facts from its reviews (no new decision):
  - Every drop is counted in `world.dropped_commands`, the World header int, and raises no
    `push_error`.
  - The per-type validation is `Sim._apply`'s `match command.type` over `_apply_point`
    (`ATTACK_MOVE` and `MOVE`), `_apply_attack` and `_apply_stop`, each of which validates and
    applies, with the shared helpers `_own_rows` and `_enemy_target` (ADR 0007 §5's status
    note). `_enemy_target` is where P46b adds the visibility rule.
  - `Sim.step` marks `COMMANDS`, `INTEGRATE` and `REMOVE` today, and `FLOW` only from P37b,
    when it calls `FlowCache.run`. Each later phase PR (P37a2 `INTENTS` onward, the list above)
    extends two tests in `test_sim_step.gd` that pin the marks: the per-tick list of
    `test_a_probe_sees_begin_then_the_marks_in_ascending_order` gets its mark, and the call count
    of `test_hashes_are_equal_with_and_without_a_probe` (`80 * 4` today: `begin()` and three
    marks per tick) grows by one per tick.
  - The `MOVE`, `STOP` and `ATTACK` behaviour cases are in `tests/sim/test_sim_orders.gd`, a
    file of its own because gdlint allows at most 20 public methods per file and
    `test_sim_step.gd` holds 18.
  - `FlowCache.PHASE_FLOW`, P30n's stand-in, is gone: `FlowCache.run`'s dispatch and the tests
    use `SimProbe.Phase.FLOW`, and `tests/sim/native/test_kernel_abi.gd`'s
    `test_the_phase_mirrors_in_world_access_h_equal_simprobe_phase` pins `world_access.h`'s
    `PHASE_` and `PH_` mirrors to `SimProbe.Phase`.
  - `STOP` (`_apply_stop`) sets `order_target` and `target` to 0 and `field` to -1. `MOVE` and
    `ATTACK_MOVE` (`_apply_point`) do not clear a stale `order_target`, which is harmless while
    only `ATTACK` reads it. P41 keeps both (section P41).
  - Until P41, `_follow_targets` finds each `ATTACK` row's target every tick with
    `ids.bsearch(order_target[row])` over the aliased `world.unit_id`: a lookup inlined in the
    per-row loop, one binary search per `ATTACK` row per tick, and no `world.index_of` call
    (`_own_rows` and `_enemy_target` call `world.index_of` at command cadence). Its doc comment
    says "P41 replaces this"; it stands in until then.

### P34 art: squads and combat look (art-director)
- Starts after Michael accepts ADR 0012 (the P26s rule for a contradicted contract; accepted
  2026-10-12, so P34 is unblocked). The probe
  confirmed the ring sprite, the atlas quads and the rect bars, and ADR 0012 §6 changes no art
  (team tints and type textures are grouped by draw z in P40), so the content below is unchanged.
- Files:
  - `assets/art/svg/units/rifle_squad_{hull,team}.svg` and `rocket_squad_{hull,team}.svg`;
    `assets/art/svg/ui/selection_ring.svg`; `assets/art/svg/fx/effects_atlas.svg` (+ `.import`
    files);
  - `assets/art/STYLE.md`: catalogue rows, § "Combat effects", § "Health bars", § "Fog and
    terrain", and the Open points: the own/enemy cue point records Michael's answer 3 (the
    notched enemy bar for Phase 2, no always-on marker, revisited in Phase 5 polish if
    playtesters have trouble);
  - `assets/art/STYLE.md` § "Feedback markers": the intro sentence and the "Own selection ring"
    row change, because a sprite scales its baked stroke with the ring (ADR 0011 §3). The radius
    R stays (35.2 world px for the light tank; 31.5 / 35.2 / 52.8 base px on the 6-inch phone at
    zoom 0.75 / 1.0 / 1.5, which `test_feedback` pins). The outline and the white stroke are
    baked into `ui/selection_ring.svg` so that they are exactly 1 + 3 + 1 dp when R is at its
    18 dp minimum, and grow with R above it (about 5 dp of white at 52.8 px). The row states the
    texture's centre-line radius in px (`RING_TEX_RADIUS_PX`, which P40 copies as a view
    constant). The other four marker rows are unchanged;
  - `assets/art/STYLE.md`, the two per-rig draw-order lines (the vehicle layers' "Draw order:
    hull, then team, then turret" and the feedback section's "each rig (ring, hull, team layer,
    turret)"): layers are drawn across all rigs by z band (rings, then every hull, then every
    team layer, then every turret; ADR 0012 §6), and the art must read right with a
    neighbour's hull drawn over its own team layer for a moment;
  - `scenes/dev/art_gallery.tscn` and `game/view/art_gallery.gd`: the squads in the four slots,
    a terrain patch row (8 x 2 map tiles of grass in two tones, scrub, rock, water and sand, with
    no grid, the second row under the explored fog alpha, both squads on scrub; STYLE.md
    § "Fog and terrain"), the ring and the atlas.
- The spec gives, for each effect: atlas region, size (dp or world px), colour from the palette,
  lifetime in ms and draw layer. Tracers are lines (width, colour). Health bars: width per unit
  class (about the sprite width in dp, so that bars do not overlap in a formation at its own
  spacing: 900 milli for squads, 1200 for tanks and mixed groups; placed by sprite reach, P34b),
  height, the density rule (selected, or hit within a recent window in ms), fill colours
  that are not team colours, own plain and enemy notched (a rect notch).
- Acceptance:
  - the readability check of STYLE.md for both squads (32 px greyscale, team share 20-35%); the
    squads read apart in the pure silhouette;
  - a greyscale check of a mixed blue and red group with bars shown (own selected, enemy hit):
    own and enemy bars tell apart without colour through the notch (answer 3; no always-on
    marker);
  - scrub reads apart from grass in greyscale;
  - the ring texture at the three zoom levels in the gallery, on grass and sand, in greyscale:
    it reads as the own-selection circle and apart from the long-press anchor;
  - gdformat and gdlint pass; the gallery opens headless with no error line;
  - `Grass/ZoomRow` and `Sand/ZoomRow` keep their node paths in `art_gallery.tscn`, with one
    `Node2D` rig per zoom level as children; `tests/project/test_cross_file_facts.gd` (qa) stays
    green unchanged. New rows go next to them, not inside them. The gallery adds no button, so
    `test_touch_target_sizes` and `test_scenes_instantiate` stay green (audited table).
- Split trigger (P34b): ring, atlas, terrain patch and the STYLE.md sections.
- Split on 2026-10-12 (897 changed lines in all, 258 of them `.import` files): P34 holds the
