### P38 core: MatchRunner v1 (lead)
- Files: `game/core/MatchRunner.gd`, `game/core/det_rng_service.gd`,
  `game/core/command_queue_service.gd` (header points to ADR 0007); as merged (P26p docs,
  2026-10-14) also `game/core/StepProbe.gd` (the forwarding probe), with the test support file
  `tests/core/support/stamp_probe.gd`.
- **As merged** (#85, 2026-10-14; P26p docs; 484 changed lines, 113 of them production in
  `game/core`; row P38):
  - `game/core/StepProbe.gd` (`class_name StepProbe extends SimProbe`; it never names its own
    class at run time, P56c's open item): `var target: SimProbe` and `var step_usec: int`.
    `begin()` calls `target.begin()` (when not null), then sets `step_usec = 0` and reads the
    clock; `mark(phase)` reads the clock into `step_usec`, then calls `target.mark(phase)`. So
    `step_usec` runs from `begin()` to the last mark, and the target's own calls fall outside it.
  - The runner builds one `StepProbe` (`_step_probe`) and sets its `target` to `probe` before
    every step. `_tick()` in order: copy the prev positions by index; drain `CommandQueue` into
    the reused `_commands`; `loop.step(world, _commands, _step_probe)`; clear `_commands`; set
    `last_hash` (`loop.last_hash`), `last_tick_usec` (`_step_probe.step_usec`, `Sim.step` only)
    and `last_runner_tick_usec` (the whole tick, the checkpoint hash included); emit `ticked`.
  - Tests: `tests/core/test_match_runner.gd` (18 public tests, which with `before_test` and
    `after_test` is gdlint's limit of 20 public methods, so new runner tests go into a new
    suite) adds the seed and replay header with `match_serial`, an arena run (seed 7, 45 ticks)
    that `Replay.play` matches (`PLAY_MATCHED`), `last_hash` and the tick times (bounded by a HUD
    `StepProbe`, so a time that took in the checkpoint hash fails), the probe forwarding (with
    `support/stamp_probe.gd`, 2 ms in `begin()` and `mark()`), array identity with a command
    applied once, and frame-rate independence (the arena, seed 42, 300 ticks at 30, 60 and 120
    fps and with jittered frames); `tests/core/test_det_rng_service.gd` (2 tests). ROADMAP box 2
    is ticked.
- Tests:
  - `tests/core/test_match_runner.gd` (updated):
    - `world.rng.state()` equals `DetRandom.create(seed, STREAM_SIM).state()` after `start()` on
      `controls_test` (no jitter, so no draws);
    - `match_serial` increments on every `start()`;
    - `Replay.play(runner.replay, fresh world, 45)` returns -1;
    - `last_tick_usec > 0` and `last_runner_tick_usec >= last_tick_usec`;
    - ticks without row changes keep every array size and identity (`is_same`), as in Phase 1;
    - frame-rate independence: `perf_arena` with seed 42 for 300 ticks, driven through
      `_process` at 30, 60 and 120 fps and with jittered deltas, ends with the same 15-tick
      checkpoints as a pure `MatchLoop` run; at 120 fps `alpha()` changes on every frame;
    - the Phase 1 cases stay green (prev sizes equal the row count, arrays never reassigned).
  - `tests/core/test_det_rng_service.gd`: `DetRng.create(7, 1)` equals `DetRandom.create(7, 1)`.
- The lead's decisions given to P38 (P26o docs, 2026-10-14, after P35b, #82; P38 is in flight in
  parallel and its outcome is not recorded here; P26p docs: merged as described in "As merged"
  above):
  - `last_tick_usec` is `Sim.step` only. A `game/core` forwarding probe measures it from its
    `begin()` to the last mark, and the runner always passes that probe to `loop.step`.
  - The runner clears its queued list after every step: `MatchLoop.step` only reads `queued`,
    and a command passed again is stamped, recorded and applied again (contract row
    `MatchLoop`).
  - Sequences start at 0 (`MatchLoop.next_sequence`; the Phase 1 runner started at 1), so four
    assertions of `tests/core/test_match_runner.gd` change: one in
    `test_start_builds_the_world_and_clears_the_queue`, two in
    `test_commands_get_the_next_tick_and_an_increasing_sequence` and one in
    `test_process_stamps_commands_emits_ticked_and_keeps_prev_positions_on_both_axes`.
  - The forwarding probe is a file with a script base (`SimProbe`) that must not name itself at
    run time (P56c's open item, "Answers and follow-ups (P26o docs)").
- Not in P38 (ADR 0012 §3): prev re-alignment by id and projectile prevs. They live in
  `MatchSnapshot` (P38s) and reach the runner in P38j. Until then, prev positions are copied by
  index as in Phase 1. Nothing dies or spawns mid-match in production before P42a, and P42a
  needs P40, which needs P38j.
- Size: about 270 without the re-alignment, so it names no split.
- Phone check: none (nothing visible). Ticks box 2 (the lead's own one-line `docs/ROADMAP.md`
  edit).

### P38t core: timing probe, perf stats, gate def (lead)
- (Revision 15: read with its entries in the amendment's "ADR 0013: changes to existing rows" and
  "ADR 0014: changes"; where they differ, the amendment wins.)
- (P26o docs, 2026-10-14.) `TimingProbe.gd` (`extends SimProbe`) has a script base and must not
  name itself at run time (P56c's open item, "Answers and follow-ups (P26o docs)").
- Files: new `game/core/{TimingProbe,PerfStats,PerfGateDef}.gd` (P26q docs, 2026-10-14: and
  `game/core/RefLoop.gd`, revision 15).
- **As merged** (#89, 2026-10-14; P26q docs; 536 changed lines, 158 of them production in
  `game/core`; row P38t; contract row `TimingProbe`, `PerfStats`, `PerfGateDef`):
  - `RefLoop` has two static functions: `run(iterations: int) -> int`, the P26n2 calibration
    body (`run(1000)` = 16346357, pinned with `run(0)` = 1 and `run(3)` = 29824), and
    `normalised_p95(work, samples, peak) -> float`, checked on 20, 21, 37, 39 and 100 frames.
  - `PerfStats.mean(values)`; the edge rules: an empty array gives 0.0, `p` is clamped to
    0..100, the rank is `(p * n + 99) / 100` and at least 1, a window longer than the series
    gives the whole sum and a window below 1 gives 0.0.
  - `PerfGateDef extends Resource`: 21 scalar `@export` fields plus `golden_ticks`
    (`PackedInt32Array`) and `golden_hex` (`PackedStringArray`), no `on_time_*` field. `int`:
    `heavy_rigs`, `heavy_frames_min`, `late_ticks_max`, `draw_calls_max`, `hitches_max`,
    `ref_iterations`, `ref_burst_ms`; `bool`: `release_only`; the rest `float`. The test pins
    the values, the types and `get_class()` "Resource". `data/dev/perf_gate.tres` comes with
    P38p.
  - `TimingProbe` sits beside the runner's `StepProbe` as the runner's `probe` and never names
    its own class at run time; `phase_usec` is a `PackedInt64Array`, the type of
    `MatchSnapshot.phase_usec`.
  - The PR took four fix commits after its first. The lead's review rule that applies from here
    on (at most three rounds of the final check) is under "Answers and follow-ups (P26q docs)".
- Tests:
  - `tests/core/test_perf_stats.gd`: nearest-rank percentiles, mean and worst window on small
    literal samples.
  - `tests/core/test_timing_probe.gd`: `phase_usec` has `SimProbe.Phase.COUNT` entries; on the
    runner it fills the phases `Sim.step` marks (each at least 0, and `begin()` zeroes them each
    tick, so an unmarked phase reads 0); a probe fed fake marks (`begin`, then `COMMANDS` and
    `REMOVE`) puts the time between them under `REMOVE`; the 15-tick checkpoints equal a run
    without it; `PerfGateDef.new()` has the values of the contract row literally (ADR 0012 §4 as
    ADR 0014 changes it, revision 15: work 5.6, work on publish frames 5.6, sim 13.3, job p99
    26.7, late ticks 0, fog 0.4, draw calls 60, `drop_slots` 0.5, `dropped_pct_max` 1.0,
    `cadence_pct_min` 99.0, `hitches_max` 0, `jitter_ms` 17.5, `median_tolerance_pct` 2.0,
    `refresh_tolerance_pct` 1.0, `ref_iterations` 1000, `ref_burst_ms` 400, 135, 600,
    `release_only` true, `ci_ratio_mean` 4.4, `ci_ratio_p95` 5.9; no `on_time_ms` or
    `on_time_share_pct`); it also checks `RefLoop`'s normalisation formula on fake samples, the
    per-frame method that ADR 0014's acceptance note names (calibration 2026-10-13): the p95
    over frames of work_i × min(1, peak / sample_i) (ADR 0014 table, row P38t).
- Phone check: none.

### P37a sim: movement v2, part 1 (gameplay-dev)
- Files: `game/sim/FixedMath.gd` (`step_toward`, `line_clear`, optional faster `isqrt`, header
  line); `game/sim/Formation.gd` (skip blocked slots, move a blocked target). `Sim.gd` and the
  tick's movement are unchanged. As merged (P26p docs, 2026-10-14) the tests are
  `tests/sim/test_fixed_math.gd`, `tests/sim/test_formation.gd` and the new suites
  `tests/sim/test_formation_tiles.gd`, `tests/sim/test_formation_shapes.gd` and
  `tests/sim/test_line_clear_edges.gd` (`test_fixed_math.gd` is at gdlint's 20 public methods),
  with the support files `tests/sim/support/grid_world.gd` (a prepared World from one string per
  tile row: `.` grass, `^` scrub, `#` rock, `w` and `t` that tell wheeled and tracked apart, `2`
  and `4` at costs 20 and 40) and `tests/sim/support/formation_v1.gd` (Phase 1's
  `Formation.assign`, frozen, the oracle for "on an open map the slots equal Phase 1's").
- **As merged** (#86, 2026-10-14; P26p docs; 2,148 changed lines, 294 of them production in
  `game/sim`; row P37a; ADR 0008 §1/§2 status note):
  - `isqrt` is Newton's method from above (a power of two at or over the root from the bit length
    of n, then `(x + n / x) / 2` until it no longer falls): about 3 times faster (0.37 µs instead
    of 1.15 µs in #86), identical results, the exhaustive tests unchanged.
  - `line_clear` reads a point in the tile `World.cell_of` gives it (half-open tiles, the far map
    edge in the last tile). It tests every tile the segment touches under that rule: a segment
    along a tile edge touches the tiles on its right or lower side only, and a corner crossed
    strictly between the ends also touches the two tiles beside it (the flow field's rule against
    corner cutting). Only a cost of 0 blocks.
  - `Formation.assign`: a tile is blocked for the group when any move class in the group is
    blocked there (its slot is skipped); a target on such a tile moves by `_open_centre` to the
    centre of the first open tile of the ring search (rings of tiles around the target's tile,
    each row by row, first hit wins), counting only tile centres inside the radius-inset box, so
    the slot lattice always starts inside it. It aliases the World's type tables and `pos_x`,
    `pos_y`, `goal_x`, `goal_y` and `unit_id` before its loops; the microbench in #86 gives about
    30 % faster than Phase 1 from the aliasing (75 units 835 → 591 µs, 150 units 2,389 → 1,622
    µs, minimum on the PR's VM). P38p had not merged, so there is no `PERF` line.
- `Formation.assign` calls `world.prepare()` first (idempotent, one bool check; a no-op inside
  `Sim.step`, which has already prepared). Its move-class check reads `type_move_class` and the
  tile grid, which only `prepare()` pads and derives, and `tests/sim/test_formation.gd` calls
  it on an unprepared `World.new()` with the five v0 type arrays (first audited table). Every
  Phase 1 case there adds its types and units before its first `assign`, so the rules that apply
  after `prepare()` (no new types; `add_unit` refuses owners above `player_count`) never bite,
  and the Phase 1 cases stay green unchanged.
- `Formation.assign` aliases `world.pos_x`, `world.pos_y` and `world.unit_id` into typed locals
  before its slot and pair loops, and passes them to its helper (ADR 0012 §2, the alias rule:
  it runs on the worker from P38w on, and per-pair `world.pos_x[row]` reads ran 1.8 times slower
  there while the main thread moved rigs). Results are unchanged.
- Tests `tests/sim/test_fixed_math.gd` and `tests/sim/test_formation.gd`, on maps built in code:
  - `step_toward` equals the Phase 1 step for a table of cases (truncation, snap when
    `d2 <= speed^2`).
  - `line_clear` supercover cases: axis lines, diagonals through a corner, a line grazing a
    blocked tile, per move class (scrub).
  - A target on rock moves to the nearest passable tile centre; slots on blocked tiles are
    skipped; on an open map the slots equal Phase 1's.
  - Pin: `Formation.assign` on an unprepared hand-built world (only the five v0 type arrays)
    gives the Phase 1 goals with no error line, and afterwards `type_move_class.size()` equals
    `type_ids.size()` (it prepared the world).
  - If `isqrt` changes, the exhaustive test still passes.
- The PR prints a `Formation.assign` microbench for 75 and 150 units, and quotes the `PERF` line
  before and after if P38p has merged (otherwise it says so; this PR does not change the tick).
- Phone check: tanks move and spread exactly as before (open map).

### P37a2 sim: movement v2, part 2 (gameplay-dev)
- Files: `game/sim/Movement.gd` (new: intents for `DIRECT`, `HOLD` and `CHASE`, staggered line
  checks, integrate and slide, arrival); `game/sim/Sim.gd` (`Movement.run`,
  `Movement.integrate`, replacing the Phase 1 movement). A blocked line keeps `DIRECT` with the
  slide until P37b adds fields.
- (P26p docs, 2026-10-14, from P37a as merged, #86.) Build the test maps with
  `tests/sim/support/grid_world.gd` (`from_rows`, `grass`, `add_block`). `line_clear` reads a
  point in the tile `World.cell_of` gives it, so a unit standing on a rock tile's left or top
  edge (x or y a multiple of 1000 on that tile's near side) is inside that tile, and its line
  starts blocked. P37a2 must not rely on a clear line from such a position (ADR 0008 §2's status
  note).
- **As merged** (#94, 2026-10-14; P26s docs; 1,092 changed lines, 225 of them production in
  `game/sim`; P37a3 not taken; row P37a2, the World v1 columns `nav_mode`, `nav_cell`,
  `intent_*` and `push_*`; ADR 0008 §2's arrival status note):
  - `Movement.reset_nav(world, rows, mode)` is the command-cadence hook: `Sim._apply_point` and
    `Sim._apply_attack` call it with `World.NavMode.DIRECT`, `Sim._apply_stop` with
    `World.NavMode.NONE`; it sets `nav_mode` and sets `nav_cell` to -1. `Sim.step` calls
    `Movement.run`, marks `INTENTS`, calls `Movement.integrate` and marks `INTEGRATE`.
  - `Movement.run` (INTENTS): `HOLD` and an order of `IDLE` give no intent; `NONE` with an order
    becomes `DIRECT`; `CHASE` aims at the position of the unit in `target` at the start of the
    tick (one `bsearch` per row), and a `CHASE` unit whose target is gone aims at its goal,
    reaches it and stands there with its order and mode for good, because `integrate` never ends
    a `CHASE` order (the code calls it a fallback that combat replaces, P41; a test pins it).
    Every other unit with an order and `nav_cell` -1 checks its line on its first tick where
    `(tick + id) % line_check_period_ticks == 0` (period at least 1), counts it in
    `stats[LINE_CHECKS]` (added with `+=` after the loop) and writes `world.cell_of(x, y)` into
    `nav_cell`, so the cell is set once per order; `FixedMath.line_clear`'s result is discarded
    until P37b (a blocked line keeps `DIRECT` with the slide).
  - `Movement.integrate` (INTEGRATE): for every row it adds `intent + push` (so an `IDLE` row
    with a push moves too, contract column `push_*`), clamps to 0 below and to
    `mini(map_w, map_cols * 1000) - 1` (and the same for y) above, or, on a world with no map
    (`map_cols * map_rows == 0`), to `Movement.NO_BOUND` (`0x7FFFFFFF`, the largest int32, the
    one constant of the file). It tests only the destination tile for the unit's class: blocked,
    then the x move alone, then the y move alone, else the unit stays; so a unit that started on
    a blocked tile may move to a passable one, and a diagonal step may pass between two blocked
    orthogonal neighbours (pinned, for the C++ twin). Arrival is Phase 1's snap rule: a unit
    with an order other than `IDLE` and `ATTACK`, not in `HOLD` or `CHASE`, whose goal is within
    one step at the start of the tick (`d2 <= speed * speed`) goes `IDLE` with `nav_mode`
    `NONE` and `nav_cell` -1 on that tick, also when a push or the slide keeps it off the goal.
    `HOLD` and `CHASE` never arrive; `ATTACK` keeps its order until combat ends it (P41).
  - `Sim._follow_targets` stands in for TARGETING until P41 and runs before `Movement.run`, so
    its cost is counted in the `INTENTS` phase until P41.
  - Suites `tests/sim/test_movement.gd` and `tests/sim/test_movement_integrate.gd`, with the
    fixtures `tests/sim/support/movement_v1.gd` (Phase 1's `Sim._move_units`, frozen: the oracle
    of "on an open map every tick equals Phase 1") and `tests/sim/support/phase_view_probe.gd` (a
    test-only `SimProbe` that reads row 0 at each mark); `test_sim_step.gd`'s two probe-mark
    cases gain `INTENTS`.
  - `PERF` (the PR's lines: before at `844b0bc`, main; after at `3862ab4`, the PR's last commit,
    whose tree is the merged `499ff4e`): `mean_ms` 0.062 to 0.129, `est_phone_p95_ms` 1.32 to 1.77
    (against `sim_p95_ms` 13.3), `intents` 0.000 to 0.058 ms and `integrate` 0.039 to 0.047 ms,
    `line_checks` max 30. The arena hash changed from `5bc4cbe88d141273` to `39b341a76c56d18e`
    because units no longer walk through rock and water: they slide along it and, without
    fields until P37b, about 90 of 150 stay ordered for all 900 ticks. `INTENTS` at 0.058 ms is
    not a port candidate by ADR 0013 §1 clause (a) (0.058 x 4.4 = 0.26 ms, under 0.67 ms); the
    formal reading is P37b's `PERF` line (row P37n2).
  - Left for P37b (section P37b): the three `line_clear` mutants this PR's review left alive,
    and the `IDLE` bounds of `test_two_runs_give_the_same_hashes`.
- Tests `tests/sim/test_movement.gd`, on maps built in code:
  - Open map: positions tick by tick equal Phase 1 (the `test_sim_step` cases and the
    Phase 1-shaped case of `test_world_v1` pass unchanged). The exactness cases written here use
    one unit, or units that never come within their radii sum, so they stay valid after P39b
    adds separation (see the audited table under "File ownership").
  - A unit steering `DIRECT` into a wall slides and its centre is never on a blocked tile
    (checked every tick).
  - A 150-unit order spreads its line checks: `stats[LINE_CHECKS]` is at most 30 + the units
    entering a new tile in any tick.
  - Arrival sets `IDLE` at the goal.
  - `HOLD` produces no intent; `CHASE` steers at the target (set by hand; P41 sets it for real).
  - Two runs give the same hashes.
- The PR quotes the `PERF` line before and after (P38p is a dependency).
- Phone check: tanks move and spread exactly as before (open map).

### P38p test: perf harness, report only (qa-engineer)
- (Revision 15: read with its entries in the amendment's "ADR 0013: changes to existing rows" and
  "ADR 0014: changes"; where they differ, the amendment wins.)

  `ci_ratio_mean` 4.4 and `ci_ratio_p95` 5.9 from the release run's worker mode (ADR 0012
  acceptance note addendum),
  golden hashes empty),
  and the purity bans of
  ADR 0012 §2 in `tests/project/test_sim_purity.gd` if P27q opened before ADR 0012 was accepted
  (see P27q). `tests/project/test_layout.gd` needs no change (it lists only `res://tests`).
- **As merged** (#92, 2026-10-14; P26r docs; 1,075 changed lines: 29 production lines in
  `data/dev/perf_gate.tres`, the rest tests plus 8 lines of qa-engineer memory, a size Michael
  accepted by merging; row P38p; where these bullets differ from the description below, they
  win):
  - Two more files: `tests/perf/support/perf_report.gd` (pure: the run statistics, the best of
    the runs, the phone estimates, the `PERF` line and `reports/perf/sim.json` from one report
    dictionary) and `tests/perf/test_perf_report.gd` (pins it on fake samples and checks
    `perf_gate.tres`).
  - The lines: one per run starting `RUN sim ` (seed, run number, `mean_ms`, `p50_ms`, `p95_ms`,
    `max_ms`, hash; three seed-42 runs and one seed-43 run) and one summary line starting
    `PERF sim `, the one P51 copies. Key order (`PerfReport.LINE_KEYS`, then three groups):
    `units`, `ticks`, `runs`, `mean_ms`, `p50_ms`, `p95_ms`, `p99_ms`, `max_ms`, `worst15_ms`,
    `ticks_per_s`, `ref_ms`, `est_phone_mean_ms`, `est_phone_p95_ms`, `alive_end`, `proj_peak`,
    `projectile_overflow`, then `stats_max=`, `phases=` and `hash=`.
  - Every statistic on the `PERF` line and every phase mean is the minimum over the three
    seed-42 runs, each taken separately, so the phase means sum to a little less than `mean_ms`
    (the checkpoint hash is in no phase). `ref_ms` is the smallest of 40 timed
    `RefLoop.run(ref_iterations)` calls: `_time_reference()` runs before and after the four
    runs, each time one untimed warm-up call and 20 timed calls (`REF_SAMPLES`).
  - The gate-file case compares every field with `PerfGateDef.new()` and a second case detects
    a missing `field = ` line; neither pins the field count, so P54r and P38pn add fields
    without touching the qa suite. The `ci_ratio_*` fields (today `ci_ratio_mean` and
    `ci_ratio_p95`) are only range-checked (exception 5: the lead changes them in the file
    alone). Details: contract row "Perf test".
  - The `game/sim` purity bans of ADR 0012 §2 are in `tests/project/test_sim_purity.gd` as
    `BANNED_THREAD_RULES`, matched in code after strings and comments are stripped: `static var`;
    the word `signal` on any line (also after an annotation, a `;` or in a one-line inner class);
    `.emit(` and `emit_signal(`; `call_deferred(`, `load(` and `preload(` as calls;
    `ResourceLoader`, `ResourceSaver`, the thread classes `Thread`, `Mutex`, `Semaphore` and
    `WorkerThreadPool`, and `await` as whole words; and the parsers
    `bytes_to_var_with_objects`, `var_to_bytes_with_objects` and `str_to_var` (ADR 0007 §9).
    `emit_signal(` and the parsers go beyond ADR 0012 §2's list; P26o's "Recorded, no code"
    follow-up is done.
  - Report-only fields that survive as mutants on today's arena: "Answers and follow-ups (P26r
    docs)".
- The test (ADR 0011 §4): the arena through `MatchLoop`, seed 42 three times and seed 43 once,
  900 ticks each, with a `TimingProbe`; prints the full `PERF` line (including `ref_ms`, the

  the three seed-42 runs match every 15 ticks and that seed 43 differs; fails only above 20 ms

### P38s core: MatchSnapshot (lead, lane B)
- Files: `game/core/MatchSnapshot.gd` (new; contract row `MatchSnapshot`, ADR 0012 §3).
  `MatchRunner` does not use it yet.
- **As merged** (#88, 2026-10-14; P26q docs; 751 changed lines, 265 of them production in
  `game/core`; row P38s; P38s2 not taken; where these bullets differ from the description
  below, they win):
  - `capture` also takes `events` and `stats` with `duplicate()`, in two explicit lines; they
    are not in `COPIED`, so `COPIED` (21) and `NOT_COPIED` (28) still cover exactly the five
    shape lists.
  - A null `previous`, or one of another match, gives every `prev_*` as a `duplicate()` of the
    current array (equal, not the same object), so `prev_proj_*` equal the current `proj_*`. A
    `SYNC` test that writes the published snapshot's `pos_*` or `proj_*` therefore leaves its
    `prev_*` alone.
  - `target_row` (`_fill_target_rows`) is one `bsearch` per row over the ascending `unit_id`,
    aliased into a typed local: O(n log n). The targets are not ascending, so it is no merge
    walk; the merge walk is `_align_prev`. The loops run over the snapshot's own copies
    (aliased into typed locals) and, in `_align_prev`, the previous snapshot's arrays as typed
    parameters; never over World arrays.
  - The capture costs about 20-24 µs at 150 rows (the arena at tick 450, `SNAPSHOT
    capture_us=…`, report only), well under the 0.1 ms expected below.
  - The PR took two fix commits after review: the prev copies on a fresh capture with chained
    prev and `target_row` cases, then the `proj_owner`/`proj_weapon` copies and "any other
    `match_serial`" pinned (tests only).
- `capture(world, previous)` takes each `COPIED` array with `duplicate()` (O(1); the copy
  happens on the next write to the World), each `ID_LISTS` list (`type_ids`, `weapon_ids`,
  `terrain_ids`) with `duplicate()` (a shallow copy of a few entries) and the fog with
  `world.fog.copy_into(fog)`. `COPIED` includes `type_armour` and the view's other type and
  weapon tables (ADR 0012's amendment). It aligns
  `prev_pos_*` by id only when `rows_version` changed (the merge walk of ADR 0011 §2); otherwise
  they are `previous.pos_*`. A null `previous`, or one whose `match_serial` differs from this
  snapshot's, gives prev = current. `prev_proj_*` are `previous.proj_*`. It fills `target_row` (the
  row of `target` in this snapshot, -1 for none or gone) with one walk over the ascending ids. World
  arrays are aliased into typed locals before every loop (ADR 0012 §2). `cell_of(x, y)` uses the
  clamped formula of `World.cell_of` with its early return (0 when `map_cols == 0`; contract row
  `World` v1, pinned in P29b). `capture` does not
  set `match_serial` or `last_hash` (the World holds neither). It reads `match_serial`, so the
  caller sets it before the capture (ADR 0012 §3): from P38j the runner does, and this PR's tests
  set it by hand on the new snapshot before every `capture` (the same value as `previous` for one
  match).
- Tests `tests/core/test_match_snapshot.gd`, on the arena (seed 42) stepped through `MatchLoop`:
  - after 30 ticks, every snapshot array equals its World array, and `unit_count()` equals the
    rows;
  - coverage: `COPIED` and `NOT_COPIED` are disjoint, their union is exactly the World's
    `ROW_ARRAYS`, `TYPE_ARRAYS`, `WEAPON_ARRAYS`, `PROJECTILE_ARRAYS` and `MAP_ARRAYS`, and every
    `COPIED` name is a snapshot property; `type_armour` and `weapon_projectile_speed` are in
    `COPIED`; `move_cost` and `terrain_cost` are in `NOT_COPIED` (the view needs only `terrain`
    and `terrain_ids`);
  - id lists: every `ID_LISTS` name (`type_ids`, `weapon_ids`, `terrain_ids`) is an
    `Array[StringName]` property equal to the World's and not the same object (`is_same`
    false);
  - `target_row`: equals `index_of(target)` for every row, -1 for target 0 and for a target
    that died in the tick;
  - `cell_of` equals `World.cell_of` on the map corners, the edges and positions outside the
    map, and is 0, like `World.cell_of`, for a world with no map (`map_cols == 0`);
  - a `previous` with another `match_serial` gives prev = current, even with an equal
    `rows_version`; with the same `match_serial` set before the capture, moving units give
    `prev_pos_* == previous.pos_*` and at least one `prev_pos_x != pos_x`;
  - immutability: after a capture, one more `MatchLoop.step` leaves every snapshot array and
    `fog` read unchanged (values compared with a deep copy taken at capture);
  - prev by id: a unit killed in the test (hp 0, then a tick) is dropped, and survivors keep
    their previous positions; a unit added with `add_unit` starts with prev = position; with
    unchanged rows, `is_same(snap.prev_pos_x, previous.pos_x)` is true (no copy);
  - projectiles: a slot whose id changed has `proj_id != prev_proj_id`; a continuing slot
    keeps its previous position in `prev_proj_*`;
  - `index_of` on ids with gaps, absent ids, and the first and last rows;
  - `capture(world, null)` gives prev = current;
  - the capture cost on the arena at tick 450 is printed (`SNAPSHOT capture_us=…`, report only;
    expected well under 0.1 ms on CI).
- Size: `MatchSnapshot.gd` about 190 (fields and comments 75, the three lists 20, capture 45,
  merge walk 25, `target_row` 10, `index_of` and `cell_of` 15), tests about 205: about 395. Split
  trigger (P38s2): the `COPIED`/`NOT_COPIED` coverage, `ID_LISTS`, `target_row` and `cell_of`
  with their cases.
- Phone check: none.

### P38j core: tick job and snapshot publishing (lead, lane B)
- (Revision 15: read with its entry in the amendment's "ADR 0013: changes to existing rows";
  where they differ, the amendment wins.)
- Files: `game/core/TickJob.gd` (new; contract row `TickJob`), `game/core/MatchRunner.gd` (the
  tick's work moves into `TickJob.run()`; `snapshot` is published before `ticked`; `prev_pos_*`
  become read-only forwards to `snapshot`; the published runner fields), and
  `tests/core/test_match_runner.gd` (own file; P26p docs, 2026-10-14: plus a new runner suite in
  `tests/core/` for the new cases, below).
- **As merged** (#91, 2026-10-14; P26r docs; 823 changed lines, 144 of them production in
  `game/core`; row P38j; P38j2 not taken; where these bullets differ from the description below,
  they win):
  - `TickJob` holds `world`, `loop`, `probe`, `commands`, `snapshot`, `previous`, and P38j adds
    `post_usec` and `end_usec` (not P38w). In `SYNC` the runner sets `job.post_usec` just before
    the inline `job.run()`, and `run()` sets `end_usec`; P38w's `serve()` keeps that.
  - `job_usec` is the run on the thread that ran it, from its start through the capture (and the
    timing copies), not the wait since the post. The runner's `last_runner_tick_usec` is the
    published snapshot's `job_usec`: it no longer includes the queue drain or the old prev copy.
  - `TickJob.probe`: `run()` reads `step_usec` only from a `StepProbe` and `phase_usec` only from
    its `TimingProbe` target, so a direct driver (P49a's `test_sim_perf.gd` on `TickJob`) passes
    a `StepProbe` whose `target` is the `TimingProbe`.
  - After `run()` the runner sets `job.world`, `job.loop`, `job.snapshot` and `job.previous` to
    null, so a restart frees the old match at once
    (`test_a_restart_frees_the_old_match_at_once`).
  - Tests: the new runner suite is `tests/core/test_match_runner_snapshot.gd` (9 cases);
    `tests/core/test_tick_job.gd` holds the guards (6 cases), with the fixtures
    `tests/core/support/holding_probe.gd` (a `SimProbe` that holds what a test puts in `held`,
    behind the job's `StepProbe`) and `tests/core/support/capturing_snapshot.gd` (a
    `MatchSnapshot` whose `capture()` takes 2 ms more and stamps the clock);
    `test_match_runner.gd` changes only the Phase 1 prev-array case.
  - The worker-file scan (`BANNED` in `test_tick_job.gd`) bans 11 names: ADR 0012 §2's ten
    (`get_tree`, `get_node`, `Engine.`, `Input.`, `OS.`, `CommandQueue`, `DetRng`, `signal `,
    `.emit(`, `call_deferred(`) plus `emit_signal`, each with one flagged and one legal fixture
    line. `get_node` is matched as a prefix (`\bget_node`), so `get_node_or_null` is caught.
    ADR 0012 §2 carries a dated status note.
- Behaviour in `SYNC` mode (the only mode until P38w): the same ticks and hashes as P38. Each
  tick captures into a new `MatchSnapshot` (with its `FogGrid`) created by the runner, with the
  current `snapshot` as `previous`, and publishes it (ADR 0012 §3, one snapshot per publish). A
  frame without a tick keeps the same `snapshot` object. The order of the header fields is fixed
  (ADR 0012 §3): the runner sets the new snapshot's `match_serial` when it creates it, before the
  job runs, because `capture` compares it with `previous.match_serial`; `TickJob.run()` sets
  `snapshot.last_hash` from `loop.last_hash` after the step. `start()` creates a new snapshot,
  sets its `match_serial` and `last_hash`, captures the tick-0 snapshot with
  `capture(world, null)` and publishes it before it returns, without emitting `ticked`. The
  runner hands the job its current `world`, `loop` and `probe` at every run (plain variables
  until P38w1b makes them private fields behind guarded properties).
- (P26o docs, 2026-10-14.) P38's decisions carry over into the job: the probe `TickJob.run()`
  passes to `loop.step` is the runner's forwarding probe, so `last_tick_usec` stays `Sim.step`
  only, and `run()` clears `commands` after the step, because `MatchLoop.step` only reads its
  `queued` (contract rows `MatchLoop` and `TickJob`).
- (P26p docs, 2026-10-14, from P38 as merged, #85.) The forwarding probe is the runner's one
  `StepProbe` (`game/core/StepProbe.gd`); the job gets it at each post with its `target` already
  set to the runner's current `probe`. The lead's decision (2026-10-14): the worker-file scan
  below includes `game/core/StepProbe.gd`, because it runs inside `TickJob.run` on the worker.
  `tests/core/test_match_runner.gd` holds 18 public tests (20 public methods with `before_test`
  and `after_test`, gdlint's limit), so the new runner cases below go into a new suite in
  `tests/core/`; only the changed Phase 1 case stays in `test_match_runner.gd`.
- Tests:
  - `tests/core/test_match_runner.gd` (P26p docs: the changed Phase 1 case; every new case
    below goes into the new suite):
    - the P38 cases stay green, except the two `is_same` lines of the Phase 1 case named in the
      first audited table, which become "a frame without a tick keeps the same `snapshot`";
    - right after `start()`, `runner.snapshot` is not null, its tick is 0, its `match_serial`
      equals the runner's, and prev equals pos (Phase 1's
      `test_start_builds_the_world_and_clears_the_queue` reads the forwards there);
    - a restart with an unchanged `rows_version` (no unit died) gives prev == pos for every row
      and `prev_proj_*` == `proj_*`, so no rig slides from the old match's positions;
    - after a publish, the previous snapshot's arrays are unchanged by the next tick (one
      snapshot per publish), and `Performance.OBJECT_COUNT` is equal before and after 20 ticks;
    - after a unit dies (hp 0 in the test), `runner.snapshot.prev_pos_*` match the new rows by
      id, survivors keep their prev, and an added unit's prev equals its position (moved here
      from P38);
    - `ticked(n)` arrives with `snapshot.tick == n`, and `snapshot.events` holds that tick's
      records;
    - `last_hash`, `last_tick_usec` and `last_runner_tick_usec` equal the snapshot's values;
      with a `TimingProbe`, `snapshot.phase_usec` equals the probe's array at publish and is a
      different object;
    - after 30 ticks, every published snapshot's `match_serial` equals the runner's and its
      `last_hash` equals `loop.last_hash` (non-zero at tick 15 and 30), and after a restart the
      next snapshot carries the new `match_serial`;
    - two consecutive ticks of one match on the arena (seed 42, units moving): for the second
      snapshot, `prev_pos_x`/`prev_pos_y` equal the first snapshot's `pos_x`/`pos_y` by value,
      and at least one row has `prev_pos_x != pos_x` (this fails if `match_serial` is set after
      the capture, because every tick would then reset prev to current);
    - Phase 1's `UnitLayer` (unchanged until P40) still draws rigs at the interpolated
      positions through the forwards (`test_unit_layer` stays green, run and named in the PR).
  - `tests/core/test_tick_job.gd` (ADR 0012 §2, guards):
    - `TickJob`'s property list holds only the allowed types (World, `MatchLoop`, `SimProbe`,
      `MatchSnapshot`, `Array[Command]`, `Semaphore`, int, bool); `run()` clears the command
      list;
    - the reachability walk from a `TickJob` on the prepared arena (World, `MatchLoop`,
      `Replay`, a `TimingProbe`, the command list, both snapshots) through script variables,
      `Array` and `Dictionary` values, with a visited set, finds no Node and no Resource; a
      fixture probe subclass holding a Node is found;
    - the source scan of `game/core/{TickJob,MatchSnapshot,TimingProbe,StepProbe}.gd`
      (`StepProbe.gd` added by the lead's decision, P26p docs, 2026-10-14) finds none of
      `get_tree`, `get_node`, `Engine.`, `Input.`, `OS.`, `CommandQueue`, `DetRng`, `signal `,
      `.emit(` and `call_deferred(`, with one flagged and one legal fixture line each (P26r
      docs, 2026-10-14: as merged it also bans `emit_signal`, 11 names, and matches `get_node`
      as a prefix; "As merged" above).
- Size: `TickJob` about 75, runner net about 65, tests about 220: about 360. Split trigger
  (P38j2, about 90): the reachability walk and the worker-file scan of
  `tests/core/test_tick_job.gd` move out; the property-list case stays. P38j2 follows P38j in
  lane B and merges before P38w, whose thread relies on these guards.
- Phone check: none (nothing visible).

### P37b sim: movement v2, part 3 (gameplay-dev)
- (Revision 15: read with its entry in the amendment's "ADR 0013: changes to existing rows";
  where they differ, the amendment wins.)
- (P26n docs, 2026-10-14, from P33 as merged, #77.) `Sim.step` marks `COMMANDS`, `INTEGRATE` and
  `REMOVE` today. P37b's `FlowCache.run` line brings the `FLOW` mark, and P37b adds `FLOW` to
  `test_sim_step.gd`'s `test_a_probe_sees_begin_then_the_marks_in_ascending_order` and raises
  the call count of its `test_hashes_are_equal_with_and_without_a_probe` by one per tick.
- (P26p docs, 2026-10-14, from P37a as merged, #86.) Build the test maps with
  `tests/sim/support/grid_world.gd`. A unit standing on a rock tile's left or top edge is inside
  that tile (`World.cell_of`), so its line starts blocked (ADR 0008 §2's status note).
- (P26o docs, 2026-10-14, from P35b as merged, #82; ADR 0012 §5 status note.) "On the ticks on
  which `MatchLoop` adds a checkpoint, using `Replay.CHECKPOINT_PERIOD` on the same tick value"
  means the tick after the step: `Sim.step` counts the tick last (`tick += 1` after every phase;
  the FLOW line P37b adds comes before it, ADR 0007 §4), and `MatchLoop` hashes after `Sim.step`
  on the new tick. So the FLOW line passes a budget of 0 when
  `(world.tick + 1) % Replay.CHECKPOINT_PERIOD == 0` (`test_match_loop.gd`'s
  `test_a_checkpoint_step_starts_one_tick_before_the_checkpoint_tick` pins that `Sim.step` sees
  ticks 14, 29 and 44 on the steps that take checkpoints 15, 30 and 45). The first test case below
  steps through `MatchLoop` and asserts `stats[FLOW_CELLS] == 0` exactly on the steps after which
  `loop.replay.checkpoint_ticks` grows.
- (P26s docs, 2026-10-14, hand-offs from P37a2 as merged, #94; P37b is in flight, and only these
  hand-offs are recorded.) `Movement.run` discards `FixedMath.line_clear`'s result today, so
  P37a2's review left three mutants alive: the call removed, the class argument replaced by 0,
  and the line started from the goal. P37b's tests kill all three. P37a2's
  `test_two_runs_give_the_same_hashes` bounds the number of `IDLE` units (more than 10, fewer
  than 40) as this phase's behaviour; if fields change that count, P37b re-pins the bounds and
  lists them as a changed assertion. Section P37a2 "As merged" gives the merged movement rules.
- Files: `game/sim/Movement.gd` (`FIELD` mode with `nav_next` at event cadence, stall, `ATTACK`
  fields and re-keying),
  `game/sim/Sim.gd` (`FlowCache.advance` with the recount; a budget of 0 cells on the ticks on
  which `MatchLoop` adds a checkpoint, using `Replay.CHECKPOINT_PERIOD` on the same tick value,
  ADR 0012 §5).
- Tests `tests/sim/test_movement.gd` (part 3):
  - With a field pending, `stats[FLOW_CELLS]` is 0 on every checkpoint tick and equals
    `flow_cells_per_tick` (or the cells left) on the others, and the field still completes.
  - A wall with a gap: 12 tanks reach slots on the far side through the gap, and no unit centre
    is ever on a blocked tile.
  - Scrub: infantry crosses it, and tanks go around.
  - A unit whose line becomes clear switches to `DIRECT` with `field = -1`; a pending field
    leaves units steering `DIRECT`.
  - `nav_next` is set at event cadence only: it equals `best_neighbour(cell)` on the tick a
    `FIELD` unit enters a new tile or its field completes, and does not change on ticks where
    the unit stays in its tile; a new order resets it to -1.
  - References are derived: after orders, `STOP`, arrival, stall and a death, each slot's
    `refs` equals the rows whose `field` points at it, and an unreferenced slot is evicted
    before any referenced one.
  - `set_terrain` mid-move: the group keeps following the old field until the new one swaps in
    (no unit steers into rock meanwhile), then re-routes.
  - Stall: 2 units ordered to slots blocked by a parked enemy go idle within `stall_ticks` + 1.
  - An `ATTACK` on a target behind a wall uses a field keyed on the target's tile and re-keys
    after the target moves more than `attack_rekey_milli`.
  - Two runs give the same hashes.
- Phone check: tanks move exactly as before (open map).

### P39 sim: separation module (gameplay-dev)
- Files: `game/sim/Separation.gd` (new; `static func run(world: World) -> void` reads
  `world.sep_grid` and the intents, writes `push_x`/`push_y` and `stats[SEP_PAIRS]`; it never
  rebuilds a grid). `Sim.gd` is not touched: the tick does not separate yet, so no existing test
  changes. Rules: ADR 0008 §4 (formula push, fixed push for near-coincident pairs).
- Tests `tests/sim/test_separation.gd` (part 1). Single-tick cases read `push_x`/`push_y` after
  one `Separation.run`; multi-tick cases use a test helper that runs, in pipeline order,
  `Movement.run`, `world.sep_grid.rebuild(...)`, `Separation.run` and `Movement.integrate`:
  - Two tanks on the same point: the pair push is `min(R / divisor, separation_push_max)` = 60
    along x, so the lower id moves -30 and the other +30, and y stays.
  - Near-coincident pairs: two idle tanks 2 milli apart (and pairs 1 and 3 milli apart, along x
    and along a diagonal) separate: after 4 ticks they are at least `R / divisor` = 200 apart,
    and after the measured tick (pinned in the PR) at least 90% of the radii sum.
  - At `d = R / divisor` the formula push (93 for two tanks) applies, not the fixed one.
  - Two units overlapping by 200 each move half the push; an idle unit takes 75% against a moving
    one (moving = non-zero intent).
  - The push is clamped to 60 per axis.
  - Only 8 neighbours and 16 scans are used in a packed 5x5 crowd (`stats[SEP_PAIRS]`).
  - No push moves a centre onto rock.
  - Units resting 1200 apart (the Phase 1 slot spacing, more than the 800 radii sum) get a zero
    push and never move.
  - Two runs of the helper give the same hashes.
- Size: `Separation.gd` about 140, 9 cases and the helper about 160: about 300.
- Phone check: none (not in the tick yet).

### P41 sim: targeting and engagement (gameplay-dev)
- (Revision 15: read with its entry in the amendment's "ADR 0013: changes to existing rows";
  where they differ, the amendment wins.)

  P33's stand-in for `ATTACK` (an inlined `ids.bsearch` per `ATTACK` row per tick). P41 keeps
  `STOP` clearing `order_target` (`_apply_stop`). `MOVE` and `ATTACK_MOVE` do not clear a stale
  `order_target`, which is harmless while only `ATTACK` reads it, and P41 keeps it that way.
- Files: `game/sim/Targeting.gd` (new; reads the 4-tile grid, never rebuilds it),
  `game/sim/Sim.gd` (the `target_grid` rebuild line inside the `GRIDS` phase that P39b created,
  then `Targeting.run` with its `TARGETING` mark), `game/sim/Movement.gd` (the chase rule's
  navigation hand-off, if needed).

  - Priority: percent, then distance, then id (rocket squad picks a tank over a closer rifle
    squad; rifle squad the reverse; equal candidates go to the lower id).
  - A unit scans only on `(tick + id) % 5 == 0`.

    acquires.

    `sight_range` (`type_sight` on a hand-built world; the pad is 0) at or above the distance
    to its target, or uses the data units (acquire never exceeds sight, P27b) with any
    `ATTACK` target inside their sight, so P46b's vision checks leave it green.

### P38w core: worker-thread tick, the hand-off (lead, lane B)
- (Revision 15: read with its entry in the amendment's "ADR 0013: changes to existing rows";
  where they differ, the amendment wins.)
- Files: `game/core/MatchRunner.gd` (`TickMode`, `tick_mode` with default `SYNC`,
  `resolve_mode`; the hand-off states `IDLE`, `RUNNING`, `FINISHED` and the pure
  `hand_off(state, event)`; the thread with the `work` and `done` semaphores; one tick ahead with
  one new snapshot per post; the refill (drain, then post); a held boundary (nothing published,
  no job started, `alpha()` 1.0, the clock stopped); `wait_idle(timeout_msec)` as a timed
  `try_wait()` loop; `job_in_flight`, `worker_active`; `_join_worker()` from `_exit_tree()` and
  `NOTIFICATION_PREDELETE`, only for a started thread; `static func live_workers()`; `start()`
  and `run_ticks()` by the table; the guarded getters of `world`, `loop`, `replay` and `probe`
  with their setters, `shared_access_allowed` and `set_probe`; `mode_after_start` and the
  fallback to `SYNC` when `Thread.start()` fails), `game/core/TickJob.gd` (`serve()`, `post_usec`,
  `end_usec`; P26r docs, 2026-10-14: superseded for the two fields, which P38j added; P38w does
  not add them: it adds `serve()` and the `work` and `done` semaphores and the `quit` flag that
  `serve()` uses, and it keeps P38j's timing, where the runner sets `job.post_usec` when it
  hands the job over and `run()` sets `end_usec`), `tests/core/gated_probe.gd` (new: a
  `SimProbe` whose `begin()` counts under a `Mutex`, flags a second job that begins before the
  first ended, and can block on a test-owned `Semaphore` or delay by a set number of ms; the block is a capped `try_wait()` loop with
  `OS.delay_msec(1)` steps that gives up after about 5 s and lets the job end, because
  `_join_worker()`'s `wait_to_finish()` has no timeout and a worker left blocked would hang CI;
  `release()` is idempotent) and the new
  `tests/core/test_match_runner_worker.gd`. Rules: ADR 0012 §1-2. No scene changes: `main.tscn`
  and `perf_arena.tscn` switch to `AUTO` in P50b, after P50w.
- At every post the runner hands the job its current `world`, `loop` and `probe` (plain
  variables until P38w1b, then the private fields behind the guarded properties; as P38j does
  for the inline run), and sets the new snapshot's `match_serial` before the
  post. That is what lets a setter's swap or `set_probe()` take effect on the next tick.
- Godot 4.7.2 facts the code relies on (verified, ADR 0012 Context):
  - `duplicate()` is lazy, and one `post()` gives exactly one token;
  - `Semaphore.try_wait()` returns false while nothing was posted;
  - a started `Thread` that is never joined only warns, but a worker still blocked at exit leaves
    an `ERROR` at process exit; `wait_to_finish()` on an unstarted `Thread` logs an `ERROR`;
  - a Node never added to the tree gets `NOTIFICATION_PREDELETE` but no `_exit_tree()`;
  - a debug build refuses a write to an in-tree Node from the worker with an `ERROR`; a release
    build does not, so the structural guards of P38j stay;
  - an assignment to a getter-only script property is silently ignored (no parse or runtime
    error), so each guarded property needs a setter, or P40's and P43a's `World.new()` swaps and
    every test that installs a probe would pass without doing anything.
- The tests run the Phase 1 way (`auto_free(MatchRunner.new())`, no tree), set
  `tick_mode = WORKER` explicitly (the exception to "headless tests run `SYNC`"), wait only
  through `wait_idle()` (never a direct `try_wait()` on the runner's semaphores, never a fixed
  sleep), and fail when it returns false. CI must stay free of the thread-leak `ERROR` at exit.
  `after_test` always releases the gated probe and then calls `wait_idle()`, so a case that
  fails or aborts while the probe holds a job still leaves the worker free; the probe's capped
  block is the backstop if gdUnit frees the runner first.
- Tests `tests/core/test_match_runner_worker.gd` (part 1):
  - **tables:** `hand_off` for every state and event of ADR 0012 §1 (`FRAME` and `FRAME_TAKEN`
    are separate events; `FRAME_TAKEN` is reached only from `RUNNING`), including `wait_idle()`
    twice, `wait_idle()` with no job ever posted, a pause with the job finished followed by
    `run_ticks(2)`, and `start()` with a finished, unpublished job; `shared_access_allowed`;
    `mode_after_start` (`WORKER` with `OK` stays `WORKER`, `WORKER` with `ERR_CANT_CREATE` gives
    `SYNC`, `SYNC` stays `SYNC`);
    `resolve_mode` over (mode, threads, web, headless): `AUTO` gives `WORKER` only for threads
    true, web false and headless false, so the scene tests stay synchronous;
  - **lockstep determinism:** a `WORKER` runner and a `SYNC` runner on the arena with seed 42,
    driven through `_process` with the same 60 fps and jittered deltas, with `wait_idle()` after
    every frame of the `WORKER` runner (said in the case: this one compares values, the overlap
    case is in P38w2). At every published tick up to 300, every `MatchSnapshot` array, both
    `prev_pos_*`, `prev_proj_*`, `events`, `stats` and the fog copy are equal by value; the
    15-tick checkpoints equal a pure `MatchLoop` run; `ticked` arrives once per tick, in
    ascending order, on the main thread (`OS.get_thread_caller_id()` equals the id read in
    `before_test`);
  - **one tick ahead:** with a gated probe holding job N+1 right after the publish of tick N,
    `probe.begin_count == N + 1`, `job_in_flight()` is true, `shared_access_allowed` is false for
    the current state (the getter itself is not called, so nothing is logged) and the published
    snapshot is still tick N; after the test releases the probe and `wait_idle()` returns,
    `world.tick == snapshot.tick + 1`;
  - **never two jobs:** over the determinism run, the gated probe never saw a second job begin
    before the first ended, and at every frame `begin_count <= published tick + 1`;
  - **life cycle:**
    - `start()` during a job discards it, and the new match's tick-15 hash equals a fresh run;
    - `run_ticks(5)` in `WORKER` mode advances the published tick by exactly 5;
    - a runner never added to the tree, freed during a job, brings `live_workers()` back to its
      value before the case; the same for a runner added to the tree and freed;
    - a `SYNC` runner that is freed starts no thread (`live_workers()` unchanged) and logs no
      error (no `wait_to_finish()` on an unstarted thread).
  - **setters:** in `SYNC`, after a tick, `runner.world = w` (a `World.new()`) makes
    `is_same(runner.world, w)` true, and after the next tick `w.tick` has advanced by one while
    the old World's tick has not (the job got `w` at that run); `runner.probe = p` installs `p`
    (`is_same`) and `p` sees the next tick's phases; in `WORKER`, after `wait_idle()`,
    `runner.world = w` gives the same result on the next post. So the `World.new()` swap cases
    of P40 and P43a still prove what they claim.
  - **what `SYNC` cannot tell apart** (lead decision 2026-10-14, P26r docs; open, to be
    restated by the lead before P38w opens): the lead asked for two `WORKER` cases that kill two
    mutants that P38j's final check left as equivalent in `SYNC`: `ticked.emit(world.tick)`
    instead of `ticked.emit(next.tick)`, and `_publish` reading `loop.last_hash` instead of the
    published snapshot's `last_hash`. As the brief words them, the cases are (a) `ticked(n)`
    arrives with `snapshot.tick == n` while the World is already one tick ahead, and (b) a
    main-thread read of `loop.last_hash` while a job is `RUNNING` goes through the guarded
    getter. Note (P26r fix, 2026-10-14): under ADR 0012 §1's accepted order (publish, emit
    `ticked`, then drain and post; "Inside a `ticked` handler no job is in flight ... so the
    World there equals the published snapshot"), `world.tick == n` holds at every emit, and
    `_publish` runs only when no job is `RUNNING` (`FINISHED` at a boundary, or after the timed
    wait of `start()` and `run_ticks()`), when `loop.last_hash` equals the finished snapshot's
    `last_hash`. So, as worded, (a) cannot happen and (b) tests the getter's guard but does not
    separate its mutant; neither case kills its mutant in `WORKER` either. P38w does not write
    them as worded, and no author breaks ADR 0012 §1's order to make one pass. Open for the
    lead: restate each case with an observation point consistent with ADR 0012 §1 or another
    kill mechanism, or accept both mutants as equivalent in every mode. No ADR text changes.
    Whatever the restated case (b) is, a case that reads through the guarded getters lands
    with P38w1b (split trigger below), because P38w's `world`, `loop`, `replay` and `probe`
    are still plain variables. **Closed** (lead decision, 2026-10-14, P26s docs): both mutants
    are equivalent in every mode under ADR 0012 §1's order (publish, emit `ticked`, then drain
    and post): inside `ticked` the World equals the published snapshot, and `_publish` never
    runs while a job is `RUNNING`. P38w and P38w1b carry no case and no special handling for
    them.
- **Open for P38w** (P26s docs, 2026-10-14, from P40 as merged, #95): no `game/view` file reads
  the runner's `prev_pos_x`/`prev_pos_y` forwards any more (only
  `tests/view/test_unit_layer.gd`'s `_world_hash`, `tests/core/test_match_runner.gd` and
  `tests/core/test_match_runner_snapshot.gd` do; the last also pins that the forwards are empty
  before the first `start()` and are the snapshot's own arrays, so a removal changes all three), and
  `MatchRunner.gd`'s comment that they serve "Phase 1's UnitLayer until P40" is stale. P38w
  decides whether to keep the forwards.
- Size: runner about 165 (150, plus about 15 for the setters and the start fallback), `TickJob`
  about 20, gated probe about 30, tests about 190: about 405. The split is the expected outcome,
  decided before the PR opens. Split trigger (P38w1b, about 115): the properties with guarded
  getters and setters, `shared_access_allowed`, `set_probe` and `live_workers()` with their cases
  (the access table, the setter case and the two free-during-job cases with the `SYNC`-free case,
  plus any restated case of the lead that reads through the guarded getters, P26r fix; P26s
  docs, 2026-10-14: none, the two mutants are closed as equivalent)
  move out; P38w keeps the thread, the table, `wait_idle`, `_join_worker`, `resolve_mode`,
  `mode_after_start`, lockstep, one tick ahead, never two jobs, `start()` during a job and
  `run_ticks(5)` (about 290). Until P38w1b, `world`, `loop`, `replay` and `probe` stay plain
  variables, the tests install the gated probe by assignment before `start()`, and the one-tick-
  ahead case gains its `shared_access_allowed` line in P38w1b. P38w1b merges
  right after P38w and before P38w2; no scene uses the worker before P50b.
- Phone check: none (no scene uses the worker until P50b).

### P38w2 core: worker-thread tick, the clock and late ticks (lead, lane B)
- Files: `game/core/MatchRunner.gd` (the `WORKER` clock: at most one publish per frame and the
  whole ticks owed after it dropped; the clock at 0 on every refill and resume, with the refill
  frame not advancing it; the clock restarting at 0 in the frame that ends a hold;
  `held_ticks`, `late_ticks` from `end_usec - post_usec` above `TICK_USEC` (`1_000_000 /
  Sim.TICK_RATE`, 66 666 µs, derived, never a literal), `latency_usec_max`;
  `start()` resets the three), `tests/core/test_match_runner_worker.gd` (part 2). Rules: ADR 0012
  §1.
- Tests (all with the gated probe of P38w installed; a "fast" job is the arena tick on CI, a few
  ms, so a "not late" case has more than 50 ms of margin, and a "late" case delays by 100 ms).
  Test frames are direct `_process(delta)` calls: each advances the runner's clock by `delta` but
  costs only microseconds of real time, while a job takes real milliseconds. So every case that
  asserts `held_ticks` 0, one publish per boundary or a hash per published tick (long frame,
  first frame, hitch, commands, replay, counters) calls `wait_idle()` after each frame, and its
  result depends on the clock rule only, never on CI speed. Only the overlap, held, late and
  forced-slow cases make the probe hold or delay a job on purpose:
  - **overlap, a published snapshot is never written:** the probe holds job N+1 while the test
    runs 30 frames of `_process` and digests every array of `runner.snapshot` (`hash()` per
    array, test code); after release and `wait_idle()` the digests are unchanged;
  - **held, not late:** the probe holds a job past its boundary and is released 10 ms after the
    hold starts: `held_ticks` 1, `late_ticks` 0, `alpha()` 1.0 and the snapshot unchanged during
    the hold; the next frame publishes, and the following publish comes one full period later
    (no catch-up burst);
  - **late:** a job delayed 100 ms counts `late_ticks` 1 and `latency_usec_max >= 100000`;
  - **forced slow, no commands:** every job delayed 80 ms for 30 published ticks: `late_ticks`
    above 0, published ticks consecutive 1..30, and the 15-tick checkpoints equal those of a
    `SYNC` run of the same scenario and seed, also without commands;
  - **forced slow, with commands:** the same delay, with a command submitted on every frame: each
    appears exactly once in `runner.replay`, with unique increasing sequences, and after
    `wait_idle()` `Replay.play(runner.replay, fresh world, final tick)` returns -1 (the recorded
    log replayed in `SYNC` is the equal-input comparison; the stamps depend on frame timing, so
    no live `SYNC` run is compared);
  - **a long frame:** `_process(1.0)` publishes exactly one tick, the next frames one tick per
    boundary with no burst, and the hash at each published tick equals `SYNC`'s at that tick;
  - **the first frame:** right after `start()`, `_process(0.2)` (the refill frame) and then
    1/60 s frames: `late_ticks` 0 and `held_ticks` 0;
  - **a hitch mid-run:** one `_process(0.15)` among 1/60 s frames: `late_ticks` 0 and
    `held_ticks` 0, and the hitch frame publishes exactly one tick (no burst);
  - **commands:** a `ticked(30)` handler that submits an `ATTACK_MOVE` gets it stamped 30 in
    both `SYNC` and `WORKER`, and the final hashes are equal; a command submitted while paused
    gets the same stamp in both modes after the resume (the refill drains);
  - **replay:** after a `WORKER` run with commands between boundaries, `wait_idle()`, then
    `Replay.play(runner.replay, fresh world, final tick)` returns -1;
  - **counters:** `start()` resets `late_ticks`, `held_ticks` and `latency_usec_max`;
  - **memory** (CI only: the monitor reads 0 on release builds): `Performance.MEMORY_STATIC`
    after tick 900 is within a pinned bound of its value at tick 100.
- Size: runner about 70, tests about 270: about 340. Split trigger (P38w3, about 110): the
  forced-slow, replay, memory and command cases move out; P38w2 keeps the clock, the counters and
  the overlap, held, late, long-frame, first-frame, hitch and counter cases (about 230).
- Phone check: none (no scene uses the worker until P50b).

### P50a ui: PerfHud, part 1: samples and verdicts (touch-ux-dev)
- (Revision 15: read with its entries in the amendment's "ADR 0013: changes to existing rows" and
  "ADR 0014: changes"; where they differ, the amendment wins.)
- Files: `game/ui/PerfHud.gd` (new: sampling, `summary()`, verdicts, golden comparison,
  `INVALID`, `restart()`; the card is a plain label list until P50a2).
- Tests `tests/ui/test_perf_hud.gd` (part 1):
  - injected frame, work, tick and drawn-rig samples give the right percentiles (through
    `PerfStats`), on-time shares, heavy-window counts and average fps;
  - verdict lines and `GATE: PASS/FAIL` follow a `PerfGateDef` built in the test, one criterion
    failing at a time;
  - checkpoint hashes against golden values give `MATCH`, or `MISMATCH` with the first tick;
  - a pause notification during the run marks it `INVALID`;
  - `summary()` holds every listed key; `restart()` resets every sample;
  - the hash text comes from `World.hash_hex`;
  - ADR 0012 §4 verdicts:
    - `work_p95_ms` is judged over all frames of the heavy window, and `work_tick_p95_ms` over
      the heavy window's publish frames only, both against 5.6 ms;
    - `sim_p95_ms` is judged against `job_p95_ms` and `job_p99_ms` against its own limit, both
      from `snapshot.job_usec`, one sample per published tick (a test feeds a tick-0 spike plus
      899 ordinary samples and the p99 passes);
    - more than `late_ticks_max` late ticks (`runner.late_ticks`, the slow-worker count) fails
      the run; `held_ticks` is shown without a verdict;
    - with `release_only`, a debug-template run shows `build: FAIL (debug template)`;
    - each fails alone in a test-built `PerfGateDef`. `tick_mode` and `build` are printed on the
      card;
  - non-gating keys: `job_mean_ms`, `job_p50_ms`, `latency_usec_max`, `held_ticks`,
    `flow_slice_p95_ms` (the `FLOW` phase over ticks whose `stats[FLOW_CELLS]` equals
    `flow_cells_per_tick`); `phases_ms` holds each phase's mean per tick over the run;
  - checkpoints come from `snapshot.last_hash` at published ticks that are multiples of 150,
    never from `runner.replay`; the HUD installs its `TimingProbe` with `runner.set_probe()`.
- Size: about 380 before ADR 0012 and about 450 with its keys, verdicts and cases, so the named
  split (golden comparison and `INVALID` into P50a2) applies before it opens.
- Split trigger: golden comparison and `INVALID` move into P50a2.
- Phone check: none (no route yet).

### P45 view: health bars (touch-ux-dev)
- Files: `game/view/HealthBarLayer.gd` (new; reads `hp` and the type tables from
  `runner.snapshot`, z 110, ADR 0012), `game/view/UnitLayer.gd` (`hit_time_ms`,
  `is_ringed`), `scenes/main.tscn`, `docs/HOW_TO_PLAY.md` (the health-bar row in Combat, and the
  "Not yet in the game" line for the under-attack indicator). The selection comes from
  `unit_layer.is_ringed(row)`, so `PlayerController` is not touched. The snapshot carries hp for
  the current tick only, so `UnitLayer` keeps its own `last_hp` and `hit_time` per row in packed
  arrays that are re-aligned by the same id merge as the rigs when `rows_version` changes, never
  plain-resized (rows compact on death, ADR 0007 §3, so a resize would shift later units' hit
  times onto their neighbours).
- Tests `tests/view/test_health_bars.gd`:
  - bars exist only for selected units (`is_ringed`, following `set_selected` and a death in
    the selection) and units hit within the recent window;
  - the bar fill equals hp / max_hp; fills are not team colours;
  - own and enemy bars use the two STYLE.md shapes, drawn as filled rects only;
  - the class comes from `type_armour` (infantry or vehicle); the bar width per class is a dp
    size at every zoom; a bar is centred on its unit with its bottom edge r x zoom + 1 dp above
    the unit's screen position (r = 26 world px for a vehicle, 14 for infantry), not placed by
    the ring;
  - each class at its own formation spacing (900 milli for squads, 1200 for tanks and mixed
    groups, which space by their largest `type_spacing`), at zoom 0.75, 1.0 and 1.5 and on every
    `UiScale` profile (1.0 to 2.37 base px per dp), pins STYLE.md's two overlap inequalities:
    side by side, bars of neighbours do not overlap; above each other, a bar's top (r x zoom +
    7 dp over its unit) stays below where the unit above starts ((spacing - half-height) x zoom
    over it), with the half-height per class that STYLE.md's sweep used: 14 world px for squads
    and 21 for tanks (the values that give its "at worst 5.6 px clear"; r = 26 would leave only
    2.0 px for tanks). art-director confirms both and records them in STYLE.md before P45 opens;
    the test holds them as test constants citing that STYLE.md line (the layer does not use
    them);
  - a new `match_serial` clears everything; frames leave no new objects alive;
  - unit 5 is hit, then unit 2 dies in a later tick: the recent-hit bar is still on unit 5 and on
    no other unit.
- Phone check: damaged tanks show bars for a moment, and selected ones always do. Own and enemy
  bars are told apart without colour (look in greyscale or squint).

  `World._map_digest_value()` recomputes the digest only when `_map_digest_version !=
  map_version` (and `prepare()` resets the cache once). So any code other than `set_terrain` that
  writes `terrain`, `move_cost` or `terrain_cost` must bump `map_version`, or the state hash keeps
  the old map.

  `MapView._bake()` indexes its tiles by `terrain[cell]` (two per entry of `terrain_ids`) with
  no range check, and the merged `World.set_terrain()` refuses only an index whose
  `terrain_cost` row is missing. P48 adds the guard in `World.set_terrain` or in the bake, and
  proves a live terrain change (a `set_terrain` during a match, drawn by `MapView`) on the phone
  or in a test.

  - The hash changes when an explored byte changes. Two runs give the same hashes.

- Phone check: none (fog is tracked but not drawn or used yet). The PR quotes the `PERF` line
  before and after, including the `fog` phase and the `FOG_CELLS` maximum that P49a will bound.
  Its `est_phone_p95_ms` is the decision point of ADR 0012 §4: above 26.6 ms, the lead starts
  drafting the GDExtension ADR in parallel with remedy steps 1 and 2. (Revision 15: dropped by ADR
  0013 §15. The lead applies ADR 0013 §1's port rule to this line, the first with the full tick;
  a phase not yet ported that meets it gets its row right after P46n.)

### P49a test: perf bounds, golden hashes and the phone estimate (qa-engineer)
- (Revision 15: read with its entry in the amendment's "ADR 0013: changes to existing rows";
  where they differ, the amendment wins.)
- Depends on P46 (the run includes fog updates, so the `FOG_CELLS` bound and the `fog` phase are
  measured, not guessed) and on P38j (`TickJob`). P46b (fog rules) merges after this PR and may
  move the golden hashes and the `alive_end` and projectile-peak bounds under exception 3. The
  cost bounds stay qa-engineer's alone. The `flow_cells_per_tick` PR of the release run re-pins
  the golden hashes under exception 3.
- The ratio it quotes (the P26s record, revision 14): `ci_ratio_mean` 4.4 and `ci_ratio_p95`
  5.9 from the release run's worker mode (11.03 / 2.51 and 19.75 / 3.36). With 5.9,
  `sim_p95_ms` (13.3 ms) equals a CI `TickJob` p95 of about 2.3 ms, and P46's GDExtension
  decision point (26.6 ms) a CI p95 of about 4.5 ms (that decision point is dropped in revision
  15; the native estimate reads `ci_ratio_native_mean` 9.4 and `ci_ratio_native_p95` 14.0).
- Why the split (ADR 0012 §4): the device-derived CI ceiling (13.3 / 4.6 = 2.9 ms with the debug
  ratio, 13.3 / 5.9 = 2.3 ms with the release worker ratio) sits below
  the ADR 0011 floor (at least 1.5 x 3.36 = 5.0 ms for a mock-sized tick), so a budget by that rule
  could never merge, and P50b, P51r and the gate would wait behind it. This PR enforces
  everything deterministic and prints the estimate; P49b enforces the wall-clock ratchet.
- Files: `tests/perf/test_sim_perf.gd` (enforcing, except the wall clock),
  `tests/perf/test_golden_hashes.gd` (new), `data/dev/perf_gate.tres` (golden seed-42 checkpoint
  hashes every 150 ticks and the final).
- `test_golden_hashes.gd` runs the seed-42 arena once through `MatchLoop` with no clock and no
  probe, and fails when a checkpoint or the final hash differs from `perf_gate.tres` (the
  message names the first differing tick and the `hash_section_names()` parts). It holds no
  timing, so P51b runs it alone by path on arm64.
- `test_sim_perf.gd` times `TickJob.run()` in `SYNC` with a fresh snapshot per tick (the same
  quantity as the phone's `job_usec`: the step, the capture and the first-write copies), with
  `Sim.step` and the phase split as sub-lines of the `PERF` line, which also prints
  `est_phone_mean_ms` and `est_phone_p95_ms` (ADR 0012 §4). The wall clock is report-only apart
  from P38p's catastrophic cap (20 ms mean). (P26r docs, 2026-10-14, from P38j as merged, #91:
  `TickJob.run()` reads `step_usec` only from a `StepProbe` and `phase_usec` only from that
  `StepProbe`'s `TimingProbe` target, so this test sets `job.probe` to a `StepProbe` whose
  `target` is its `TimingProbe`; a bare `TimingProbe` leaves `step_usec` 0 and `phase_usec`
  empty. Contract row `TickJob`.) It fails when:
  - a deterministic cost bound from `World.stats` is exceeded (flow cells and separation pairs
    from their rules; line checks; targeting candidates and fog cells stamped at the measured
    maxima plus 25%);
  - the unit count at tick 0 is not 150, `projectile_overflow > 0`, or `alive_end` or the
    projectile peak is outside its pinned bounds (each the measured value plus or minus a
    margin the PR states; exception 3 re-pins them with the same margins);
  - `MEMORY_STATIC` grows by more than the pinned bound between tick 100 and tick 900 (CI only:
    debug builds);
  - the seed-42 runs differ from each other, or seed 43 equals seed 42 (the golden comparison
    is in `test_golden_hashes.gd`).
- Size: about 300 (the golden file about 60 of it).
- Phone check: none.

### P49b test: CI wall-clock budget, a regression ratchet (qa-engineer)
- (Revision 15: read with its entry in the amendment's "ADR 0013: changes to existing rows";
  where they differ, the amendment wins.)
- Depends on P49a and P46b, so the ratchet is calibrated on the final Phase 2 tick and no sim PR
  of this phase has to move it.
- Before opening: run the P49a harness at least 10 times on the CI runner (workflow_dispatch or a
  temporary loop) and record the min-of-3 mean and p95 of `TickJob.run()`.
- Files: `tests/perf/budget.cfg` (`[sim] mean_ms`, `p95_ms`, `ticks=900`, `units=150`),
  `tests/perf/test_sim_perf.gd` (reads it and enforces it), `docs/ROADMAP.md` (box 6,
  exception 4).
- The rule (ADR 0012 §4, replacing ADR 0011 §4's ceiling): `mean_ms` and `p95_ms` are 1.5 times
  the worst min-of-3 seen in the calibration runs. A sim PR never raises them (exception 3); a
  rise that a new tick phase causes (Phase 3's production and economy) is a qa-engineer PR with
  before and after calibration numbers and its reason recorded in the PR. ADR 0011 §4's
  4.0 ms starting value of `mean_ms` (and the qa-engineer role's "start at 4 ms per tick on CI")
  is withdrawn: a calibrated mean above 4.0 ms is not a finding and does not block this PR (the
  mock tick alone is 2.51 ms mean on CI, the real tick an estimated 6 ms). The PR shows the
  calibration numbers, `ref_ms`, and `est_phone_p95_ms` against 13.3 ms. The estimate is never an
  assertion.
- Size: about 80.
- Phone check: none. Ticks box 6.
