## HOW_TO_PLAY rows
Each row describes only what the game does when its PR merges. P43b may merge before P41 and
P42a, so it writes the Attack row without chasing or firing, and P42a extends it. If P42a merges
first, the tap still attack-moves until P43b, and P43b writes the extended text at once. P43a
always precedes P42a (a dependency) while nothing can die, so the two death rows ship with P42a.

| PR | Section | Row (action → result) | Teach |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P43a | Selection | Any selection: the panel names come from the unit's display name (row reworded) | |
| P43b | Orders | With units selected, tap an enemy → Attack: the units drive at that enemy; a diamond marks it (replaces "Attack-move to the enemy") | yes |
| P43b | Not yet in the game | Moving without fighting (retreat): every ground tap is an attack-move | |
| P42a | Combat (new) | Enemies in range → units fire on their own | |
| P42a | Combat | Attack-moving units meet enemies → they stop to fight, then continue | yes |
| P42a | Orders | The Attack row extended: the units chase that enemy and fire until it dies; a diamond marks it | yes |
| P42a | Selection | A selected unit is destroyed → it leaves the selection and the panel count drops | |
| P42a | Army bar | A grouped unit is destroyed → it leaves the group; the badge shows the new count, an empty group shows only its number | |
| P45 | Combat | A unit is damaged or selected → a health bar shows; own and enemy bars differ in shape | |
| P45 | Not yet in the game | An "under attack" indicator at the screen edge | |
| P47 | Fog (new) | Black: never seen. Dimmed: seen before, enemies there are not shown | yes |
| P47p | Fog | Tap near a hidden enemy → it counts as a tap on the ground | |
| P46b | Fog | Units only attack enemies your side can see; an attacked enemy that leaves sight → the units move to where it was last seen | |
| P48 | Terrain (new) | Rock and water block all units, and scrub (the patterned ground) blocks vehicles; units drive around them. (The sentence that infantry crosses scrub waits until infantry can be built, Phase 3.) | yes |

## ROADMAP Phase 2 box -> proof
| Box | Proof | Ticked in |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 ADR for World, Command format and state hash | ADRs 0007-0011 accepted by Michael | P26 |
| 2 15 Hz tick, FixedMath, DetRandom, Command queue, state hash, replay test | `test_det_random`, `test_fixed_math`, `test_command_queue` (API v1 = v0, unchanged), `test_world_hash`, `test_sim_step`, `test_match_loop`, `test_replay` (with `test_replay_bytes`), `test_replay_play` (the plan's `test_replay` part 2, P35b as merged), `test_determinism`, `test_match_runner` (runner records, replays, frame-rate independence). The worker-thread tick (P38w, `test_match_runner_worker`: the same checkpoints as `SYNC`) comes later and does not hold this box back | P38 (ticked, #85, 2026-10-14) |
| 3 Movement with flow fields and separation; combat with projectiles, armour classes and death | `test_flow_field`, `test_flow_cache`, `test_movement`, `test_separation`, `test_targeting`, `test_combat`, `test_counter_triangle`, `test_scenarios` (arena scale), `test_projectile_layer`, `test_effects_layer`, and the P48 phone check on the preview (terrain on the main map) | P48 (or the later of P44a/P44b) |
| 4 Fog of war and its rendering | `test_fog_grid`, `test_fog`, `test_fog_layer`, preview | P46b |
| 5 View interpolation at the display frame rate | `test_match_snapshot` (prev by id, projectile prevs), `test_match_runner` (30/60/120 fps), `test_match_runner_worker` (one tick ahead, held and late ticks, lockstep with `SYNC`, a published snapshot unchanged under real overlap), `test_worker_scenes` (the main scene and the arena on the worker, headless), `test_unit_layer`, `test_projectile_layer`, the perf arena on the phone with the sim on the worker | P50b |
| 6 Performance test: 150 units for 900 ticks, ms per tick reported, budget enforced | `tests/perf/test_sim_perf.gd` timing `TickJob.run()` with the phone estimate in the `PERF` line (P49a), cost bounds (including `FOG_CELLS`: P49a runs after P46, so the measured tick includes fog updates), golden hashes, the `PERF` line in CI, and `budget.cfg` as a regression ratchet calibrated after P46b (P49b, ADR 0012 §4). P46b's changes to the golden hashes and outcome bounds go through exception 3; the release run's ratios go through exception 5 | P49b |
| Gate | Michael, see "Gate procedure": the release-template APK (P51r) with the sim on the worker and the C++ kernels (`impl: native`), ADR 0012 §4 thresholds as ADR 0014 changes the pacing rows and the main-thread measure (revision 15) | Michael |

## Step list
1. Lead opens P26 (this plan and the ADRs) as a docs PR. Michael answers the open questions
   (done 2026-10-12). The lead writes the "Answers" section and ADR 0011's acceptance note (the
   thresholds), sets ADRs 0007-0011 to "accepted", adds the status lines to ADRs 0003 and 0004,
   applies the `rts-sim-conventions` edits and ticks box 1 in P26. The lead merges P26 on
   Michael's "merge P26" (2026-10-12).
   Then the lead opens P26s as a draft, Michael runs it on his phone, and the lead posts its
   outputs in the draft PR (see P26s): the ratios for the phone estimate, `flow_cells_per_tick` as a
   data value, and a new ADR before P34 only if a draw contract fails. Done 2026-10-12: the probe
   contradicted ADRs 0008 and 0011, so the lead proposes ADR 0012 with this plan's amendment
   (revision 8) as a docs PR. Michael accepts it (P34 waits for that). Michael runs the release
   build of the probe once, in both modes (the lead first adds the worker mode to the probe branch).
   The lead posts its card in PR #35 and opens the ratio PR (exception 5), and gameplay-dev opens
   the `flow_cells_per_tick` PR if the value moves. No lane waits for it. Done 2026-10-12:
   Michael accepted ADR 0012 ("1 ja, 2 ja, 3 ja, 4 ja"), the lead applied the "On acceptance"
   list, and the in-frame mode of the release run gave the ratios 3.6 and 3.8 and
   `flow_cells_per_tick` 320. The worker-mode run then gave 4.4 and 5.9 (shipped by P38t and P38p)
   and 64 for the flow cap, with P27f held (revision 14). The lead then proposed ADRs 0013 and
   0014 in P26b with the plan's amendment. Done 2026-10-12: Michael accepted both ("1 ja, 2 A, 3
   ja, 4 ja"), and the lead applied both ADRs' docs lists in P26b (revision 15: CLAUDE.md, the
   agent files, the skill, the status lines, the ROADMAP licences line, these rows). P27f is
   withdrawn.
   Then the lead builds P26n2 on the spike branch, Michael runs it on his phone (about 5
   minutes), and the lead records C1-C5 and the outcome in ADR 0014's acceptance note before lane
   B reaches P50a. Done 2026-10-13 (P26g): the per-frame method is the formula, with no repeat
   and no load-pinned fallback (P50lp not taken).
2. Lanes A and B as in the PR table, at most two threads at a time (P26q docs, 2026-10-14: this
   limit is superseded; how many lanes run at once is Michael's call through his start commands,
   three at a time in practice since P33, "Answers and follow-ups (P26q docs)"). The first pair
   is P27a and P28 (two gameplay-dev threads on disjoint files), then P27q in lane B. After each
   merge, the next PR in a lane branches from the new `main`. From revision 15 the lanes carry
   the native rows in the order under "Work packages and PRs"; after P53 merges, Michael
   re-pastes `scripts/cloud/setup.sh` into the cloud environment's settings (ADR 0013 §8).
3. The lead reviews each sim PR's contract against the tables before review, as in Phase 1:
   names, signatures, ADR rules, the column table's ranges, aliasing and no allocation in hot
   loops, the probe phase ids, and the `PERF` line before and after (from P38p on; a sim PR that
   merges earlier says so). Accepted deviations go in the lead's memory and, if they change a
   decision, into a new ADR. From revision 15 the lead also applies ADR 0013 §1's port rule to
   the `PERF` line of P37b, P41, P42b and P46 (a conditional row that fires takes the slot right
   after that PR), checks every row that edits a ported module against the twin rule, and reviews
   every C++ PR with the skill's C++ checklist, mutants and a 20,000-tick lockstep.
4. Each PR that meets a ROADMAP box ticks it in the same PR (named exception 4; the "ROADMAP
   box ticked" column names the PR). The lead checks that line in review, as in Phase 1, and
   never ticks a box in a separate commit on `main`.
5. After P50b, P51r and P52: run `bash scripts/verify.sh` on `main` in a cloud session, and post
   the VERIFY SUMMARY and the `PERF` line in the gate issue.
6. Gate (below). The lead ticks the gate only after Michael writes that it passed.

## Gate procedure
- The lead opens the issue "Phase 2 gate: 150 units at 60 fps, same seed same match". It holds:
  - the commit SHA on `main`;
  - CI's `PERF` line for that commit (with the seed-42 final hash from `hash_hex`) and a note
    that the golden hashes passed on x86_64 (and arm64, if P51b exists);
  - the `android-perf-release` APK link (P51r: the release template with the dev menu; its
    release notes name the commit), and the reminder to uninstall the `android-dev-latest`
    build first (a different signing key) and to reinstall it afterwards;
  - the preview link of the last merged PR.
- Michael, on the gate phone: his own phone, permanently (answer 1, ADR 0011 §5). No other phone
  takes part, and no later phone changes the thresholds; a slower test phone comes only through
  a new ADR.
  1. Install the release APK, then Dev → Perf arena. Keep the phone on the table, plugged in or
     not, and do not switch apps. When the card shows, tap "Copy report" and paste it in the
     issue. Tap "Run again" twice more, copying each report (three runs in a row, about 3
     minutes, so a warm phone is measured too). Each run starts with its own reference burst, so
     every report carries its own `ref_peak_us` (revision 15, ADR 0014 §3). ADR 0014's
     calibration (2026-10-13) chose the per-frame method, not the load-pinned fallback, so there
     is no load-pinned run (P50lp not taken).
  2. Open the web preview, then Dev → Perf arena, and paste its report once. The web build runs
     the sim in the frame (`SYNC`, threads off) on the GDScript twins (ADR 0013 §7, option A),
     so its frame numbers show hitches and do not gate; only its hashes count, and they compare
     the GDScript twins with the APK's C++ kernels (a cross-implementation check).
  3. Play the main scene for 5 minutes in the same release APK (the sim on the worker): select
     tanks, attack red tanks, watch the fog lift. Note anything odd, including whether taps feel
     slow (one tick more latency, ADR 0012 §1), a diamond on an enemy at the edge of sight with
     no attack following (P43b), and hitches while order markers are on screen (ADR 0012 §6).
- **Pass**, read from the reports (each card also shows its own verdict lines):
  - all three APK runs show `GATE: PASS`, `build: release`, `tick mode: worker` and `impl:
    native` with `C++ matches GDScript: yes (60 ticks)`, none `INVALID`: heavy window at least
    600 frames; dropped frames at most 1 % and cadence at least 99 % over the run and the heavy
    window, and no hitch in the heavy window (ADR 0014 §1; revision 15, in place of "at least 95 %
    of frames on time"); main-thread frame work p95 over the heavy window and over its publish
    frames (5.6 ms each) and fog update (0.4 ms), read on the clock-normalised values (ADR 0014
    §3, with the per-frame method its acceptance note names, calibration 2026-10-13); worker
    job p95 (13.3 ms) and p99 (26.7 ms), raw; no late tick (no job longer than one period from
    post to done); draw calls (60); all within the `perf_gate.tres` thresholds (ADR 0012 §4 as
    ADR 0014 changes it);
  - every APK and web checkpoint shows `MATCH` against the golden hashes;
  - the play session found no blocker.
- A miss becomes an issue, with the ADR 0011 §6 order of remedies (step 4, the worker, is already
  in), chosen from the per-phase and per-layer numbers in the report (the layer-mode selector
  narrows a render miss in one more run). Tuning goes through `data/rules.tres` only, one value
  per PR, with before and after numbers. For the sim (revision 15), the lead reads ADR 0013
  §15's summary keys (`phases_p95_noncp_ms`, `job_p95_noncp_ms`, `job_minus_step_p95_cp_ms`,
  `job_minus_step_p95_noncp_ms`): a GDScript-only phase above 25 % of the non-checkpoint job p95
  gets a port row under ADR 0013, and a capture or hash cost above 3.3 ms goes to its own ADR
  (fewer copied arrays, fewer hashed bytes or a longer checkpoint period), never a port row.
  Neither is a verdict. If pacing fails, ADR 0014 §6 lists the remedies, each through its own
  ADR. The same holds for the first release perf-arena card after P50b and P51r, before the
  gate, which also re-derives the native ratios by the 10 % rule (exception 5).
- The gate passes when Michael writes that it passed. The lead then ticks it.

## Test plan (what is proven headless)
- **Numbers and data:** every contract number pinned literally, data validation over all unit
  files, `RulesDef` defaults equal the data (`test_defs`, `test_defs_data`).
- **Purity:** no float, time, Node, unseeded randomness, `hash(` or allocation inside loops in
  `game/sim` (`test_sim_purity`, with fixtures).
- **DetRandom:** the published reference vector, the transcribed seeding, `mul32` against the
  true product, stream independence, bounds, unbiased `next_int`, shuffle, state round-trip with
  high-bit words.
- **World:** tables, map grid, jitter and its draw count, hand-built worlds (type tables padded,
  the Phase 1-shaped world steps through every phase), removal without
  reordering over `ROW_ARRAYS`, events (stride 8), hash coverage of World and helper objects,
  every hashed scalar, `hash_hex`, `hash_parts`, pinned tick-0 hash.
- **Commands:** every drop rule per type, `MOVE`/`STOP`/`ATTACK`, order independence,
  encode/decode, probe neutrality.
- **Determinism and replays:** same seed → same hashes every 15 ticks (sequential and
  interleaved); different seed → different hashes; replay reproduces the final hash, finds a
  changed command at the next checkpoint and detects changed data; the runner at 30/60/120 fps
  and jittered frames equals the pure loop; three arena runs and golden hashes at 150 units over
  900 ticks; the same suites on arm64 (P51b).
- **Movement:** Phase 1 exactness on open maps, detours through gaps, per-class passability, no
  centre ever on a blocked tile, staggered line checks, line switching, derived references,
  double-buffered re-routing after a map change, stall, attack fields, time slicing equal to
  one-shot integration, cache sharing and eviction.
- **Separation:** the formula push, the fixed push for near-coincident pairs (units 1-3 milli
  apart separate), shares, clamps, neighbour caps, no push into rock, resting slots unmoved, the
  Phase 1 orders end without overlap, and every Phase 1 test that separation would change is
  listed (audited table) and changed in a named PR.
- **Probe:** fixed phase ids, marks in ascending id order, unmarked phases read 0, hashes
  unchanged by a probe.
- **Snapshot and worker thread (ADR 0012):**
  - the snapshot equals the World after capture and never changes afterwards;
  - prev positions by id across deaths and spawns, with no copy when the rows are unchanged;
  - projectile prevs by slot id;
  - in `WORKER` mode, every published snapshot equals `SYNC`'s by value (lockstep), and the
    15-tick checkpoints equal a pure `MatchLoop` run; a `WORKER` replay plays back in `SYNC`;
  - a command submitted in `ticked(N)` or while paused gets the same stamp in both modes, and no
    command is lost, also with every job slower than the period;
  - one tick ahead and never two jobs (a gated probe), held ticks without catch-up bursts, late
    ticks counted only for a slow worker (first frame, a hitch and a long frame give none), a
    published snapshot unchanged while the next job runs, `ticked` only on the main thread;
  - the hand-off states as a table (no wait can block on a taken token), timed waits, the join
    on free with and without the tree, `start()` and `run_ticks` in `WORKER` mode, flat memory;
  - `AUTO` resolves to `SYNC` headless and on the web;
  - nothing reachable from `TickJob` is a Node or a Resource; the worker files in `game/core`
    use no Node API, autoload or signal;
  - `game/sim` has no statics, signals, `call_deferred`, loads or threading (`test_sim_purity`);
  - no production view, input or UI file reads `runner.world`, `.loop`, `.replay` or `.probe`
    (`test_view_reads_snapshot`), and the main scene and the arena run on the worker headless,
    equal to `SYNC`, with no Node touched from the worker (the debug build reports it);
  - draw z per batch group (`test_unit_layer`).
- **Combat:** acquisition priority, staggering and stickiness, reach by order, `ATTACK` lock and
  fallback, chase then hold, no chase through rock; fire timing, hitscan with both positions,
  flight time, homing, misses on dead targets, all 4 armour classes, firing in the tick of
  death, overflow, the counter triangle.
- **Fog:** masks against brute force, overlaps, explored persistence, block indexing, staggered
  cadence, re-stamping only on tile change, dirty lists, initial stamp; visibility gates
  targeting and `ATTACK`.
- **View and input:** interpolation of units and projectiles, rig mapping by id across deaths
  and spawns with sync at every entry point, restart resets in every layer, turret aim, health
  bar density and shapes, the effects ring with a hitscan kill, fog texture states, dirty
  patching and update count, hidden and unpickable enemies; object-leak checks (no new Objects
  per frame; per-frame cost is judged by the card, not these tests). Every Phase 1 view, input,
  UI and scene test that these PRs could break is listed in the second audited table, with the
  PR that changes it or the contract rule that keeps it green.
- **Perf HUD:** statistics, verdicts, golden comparison, invalid runs, copy report (through an
  injected sink), 48 dp and safe-area layout at every profile. Headless tests never read the
  clipboard, keep-on state or an `ImageTexture` after `update()`.
- **End to end:** tap to attack, death, selection and badge pruning, fog reveal and picking on
  `main.tscn` (`test_combat_flow`).
- **Performance:** deterministic cost bounds per tick, and min-of-3 mean and p95 of
  `TickJob.run()` against `budget.cfg` (a regression ratchet, ADR 0012 §4), on every CI run; the
  phone estimate is printed, not asserted.
- **Native kernels** (revision 15, ADR 0013 §4):
  - one lockstep test per kernel comparing the full state after every call (900 arena ticks, and
    once on non-default rules), with crafted tie, cap, division and opposite-corner cases;
  - the module tests under both implementations through `sim_impls.gd` (with its non-vacuity
    assert), and the dispatch test with a GDScript spy;
  - golden hashes under both implementations on x86_64 (g++ `-O2` and clang `-O3`) and arm64
    Linux (clang `-O3`);
  - the boundary tests: `check_world` and the names in P54 over the five phases' lists; the
    in-place write, fork, scalar and no-op cases in P30n through the FLOW kernel;
  - the on-device self-check's match and mutated-kernel cases (P54r), and the `impl` verdict and
    ADR 0013 §15's summary keys on injected samples (P50a2);
  - the C++ scan (P56), the sanitizer job (P57), `check-native-tests.sh`'s discovery and floor on
    fixture trees and `results.xml` files (P55), and the fresh-clone import canary (P53);
  - every `core/` function runs in a bound kernel's tests in the PR that adds it; there are no
    test-only bound entries; all of these rerun on any Godot or godot-cpp pin change.
- **Gate measures** (revision 15, ADR 0014): the normalisation formula (the per-frame method),
  the 24 ms cadence case, the hitch case, the one-sided median rule, the refresh tolerance and the
  preflight order and refusal are proven headless in P50a, P50a2, P50a3 and P50b; the
  calibration run (P26n2, recorded 2026-10-13) was the device check that picked the per-frame
  method.
- **Not provable headless** (measured by P26s and its release run, and judged by Michael in the
  gate):
  - fps, main-thread frame work, render CPU and draw calls on the phone (a 2021 phone is never
    measured: the divided limits stand in for it, ADR 0012 §4);
  - GDScript speed on ARM, on the release template, on a worker thread (and which core Android
    runs it on);
  - the absence of data races in a release build (no Node guard there): the worker tests compare
    snapshots with `SYNC` and the guards narrow the shared surface, and the matching golden
    hashes on the phone are the final check;
  - cross-thread contention and the core the worker runs on (the release run's worker mode
    measures them on the gate phone);
  - how the extra tick of input latency feels;
  - the hash on wasm and Android (and arm64 if P51b cannot run), and the C++ kernels built by NDK
    clang at `-O3` on the phone (the APK's 60-tick self-check and the gate's checkpoint match
    cover them);
  - whether the main thread's work scales with the clock like the reference loop (P26n2's C3 and
    C4 checked it on the gate phone on 2026-10-13: they hold on the per-frame method);
  - the look of effects, health bars and fog;
  - whether combat reads clearly at zoom 0.75.

## Risks
1. **GDScript is too slow, even on the worker** (the main risk of this phase). The probe
   measured the gate phone at about 4.4 times slower than CI, and on the debug build the mock
   tick alone took 15.3 ms p95. ADR 0012 moves the tick off the main thread and allows a worker
   job p95 of 13.3 ms (33.3 ms on a 2021 phone, half the period) and a p99 of 26.7 ms. The worker
   removes the frame-pacing failure; it does not make the job budget certain. Debug-to-release
   speed-ups measured on CI put the release mock tick on the worker at about 10.4-13.3 ms p95,
   and the real tick does more than the mock (the ADR 0011 estimate understated even the mock by
   a factor of 3.3), so the limit may be missed even on the release template. The release run's
   in-frame mode (2026-10-12) measured the mock tick at 12.63 ms p95, about 5 % inside the
   limit, and the worker cost 4-5 % more than the frame on CI: the real tick is expected to be
   marginal. (Superseded, revision 14, by the finding below.)
   - **Early-warning finding (revision 14).** The worker-mode run measured the mock's worker job
     at 19.75 ms p95 (PR #35 release run 2) and 20.10 ms (PR #41): it misses `sim_p95_ms`
     (13.3 ms) by 48-51 % before any real-tick work is added. The GDScript sim is not marginal;
     it fails the limit. The mitigation bullets below on P46's decision point and ADR 0012 §7's
     trigger are overtaken: the miss is already measured, and the response proposed is ADR 0013
     (amendment, question 1). If ADR 0013 is rejected, ADR 0012 §7's trigger stays and the gate
     is expected to fail on sim time ("If an ADR is rejected").
   - **Revision 15:** Michael accepted ADR 0013, so the sim part of this risk (the sim's CPU time
     on the worker) is covered by the C++ kernels: the measured C++ mock leaves the worker job
     about 15 times below `sim_p95_ms`, and ADR 0013 §1's rule bounds what any phase left in
     GDScript can add. The main-thread part stays, and ADR 0014 changes how it is measured.
   - Mitigation:
     - the release run of the probe in both modes, which measures the worker on the gate phone;
     - every sim PR from P38p on prints its gate-phone estimate (`est_phone_p95_ms`), so a miss
       shows weeks before the gate, and P46's line is the decision point for starting the
       GDExtension ADR draft in parallel (above 26.6 ms);
     - cadences, slices and staggered line checks are in data;
     - the ADR 0011 §6 order, steps 1 and 2;
     - then a GDExtension ADR, on the trigger fixed in ADR 0012 §7 (not adopted now; overtaken
       in revision 15 by ADR 0013, accepted).
2. **Render cost of 150 rigs plus effects.** The probe passed this: 13 draw calls and 1.94 ms
   render CPU p95 with sprite rings. Two gaps remain:
   - mixed unit types interleave textures, which costs one call per sprite (verified with Xvfb;
     the probe drew tanks only);
   - interleaved team tints cost one call per sprite too (161 calls on the phone).

   Mitigation: draw z per batch group (ADR 0012 §6), proved by a headless z test and by the card's
   draw-call line, plus the existing draw rules and the 60-call ceiling. MultiMesh stays the
   prepared fallback, with a trigger: a forecast above 40 calls (types x owners grows). Markers
   in the main scene can add about 40 calls for a moment; the gate's play session watches for
   hitches there.
3. **Silent int32 truncation and int64 wrap-around** (verified behaviour) would break
   determinism or game logic without an error.
   - Mitigation: the ADR 0007 §1 rules, the column table's ranges, contract review, and tests
     with values near map limits and high-bit generator words.
4. **Cross-platform determinism** (wasm, arm64, x86_64) is assumed from integer-only maths and
   little-endian SHA-256 input.
   - Mitigation: golden hashes in CI on x86_64 and, if available, arm64 Linux on every PR; the
     gate compares the APK and web runs with the same golden values.
   - Revision 15: the C++ compilers join the list (g++ `-O2` in verify, clang `-O3` in
     `native-clang`, NDK clang on the phone, Apple clang later); the golden hashes run under both
     implementations on x86_64 and arm64, and the APK runs a 60-tick lockstep self-check.
5. **Phase 1 behaviour drifts.** Mitigation: an open map takes the Phase 1 code path exactly
   (line clear), hand-built worlds stay valid, the controls spawns stay, the test area stays
   open, and the only intended changes to Phase 1 tests are the ones in the two audited tables
   under "File ownership", each named with its reason and PR:
   - sim side: two pair bounds and one idle tick in P39b (exception 2 for the two pair bounds in
     other owners' files), two re-aimed `test_unit_layer` cases in P40;
   - view, input and art side: the `test_feedback` ring case extended for the ring sprite in P40
     (with the STYLE.md ring row changed in P34); the enemy taps of `test_player_controller`
     (after P43b's own rewrite) and `test_feedback` revealed with `reveal_enemies` in P47p;
     `test_picker`'s fill case writing the snapshot instead of the World in P40 and extended for
     `pickable` in P47p; `test_army_bar`'s "All army" case gaining one `run_ticks(1)` in P43a;
     `test_view_scale` and `test_main_scene` moved to `MapView`, and exception 1, in P36b (P36's
     split, P26l docs).
6. **CI timing noise fails the perf test.**
   - Mitigation: deterministic cost bounds carry the strict checks; wall clock uses min-of-3 as a
     fixed method, a budget calibrated over at least 10 CI runs, and `ref_ms` in the line so a
     relative budget can follow through an ADR. No retries.
7. **The sim spine is serial** (`Sim.step` and `World` are shared).
   - Mitigation: World v1 declares every array up front, helpers are wired once in P31a, phase
     modules live in their own files, standalone modules sit in lane B, and only one-line
     conflicts in `Sim.step` remain.
8. **Balance starting values break the counter triangle** after truncation or range effects.
   - Mitigation: the triangle test is the judge, values may change in P42b with the table
     updated, and real balance is Phase 4.
9. **The gate phone does not represent a 2021 phone** (Michael's phone is faster; answer 1 keeps
   it as the gate device for good).
   - Mitigation: every CPU-time threshold is divided by k = 2.5 (ADR 0011 §5, ADR 0012 §4).
     What the division does not model is covered as follows:
     - GPU fill rate, memory bandwidth and heat: the Compatibility renderer, the draw-call
       ceiling and three runs in a row;
     - the worker landing on an efficiency core, and heat: the p95 limit, which keeps the p95
       tick at half the period on a 2021 phone; the tail above the p95 is gated by the p99 limit
       (one period on a 2021 phone). They replace the debug-APK margin, now that the gate uses
       the release template. The gate phone itself can put the worker on one of its four little
       cores; the release run's worker mode measures that.

     The card records model, refresh rate and screen, so later phases compare on the same
     model.
   - If k is wrong for real slow phones, the gate passes too easily. Field data (Phase 4 and 5
     playtests, Android vitals after release) can add a slower test phone or change k through a
     new ADR.
10. **Flow cache churn** if many distinct blocked orders happen at once, and slower fields:
    `flow_cells_per_tick` is 512 (P27b), and it stays: ADR 0013 is accepted, so ADR 0012 §5's
    rule gives 512 with the C++ slice and P27f is withdrawn (revision 15; it would be 64 in
    GDScript, revision 14). Until the §1 ports have merged, 512 runs on partly GDScript phases,
    which no gate judges. At 512 a field over the arena's
    open cells (3942 for tracked and 3974 for foot units in the P27c map) takes 8 ticks, and the
    fourth of the arena opening's four fields, served one at a time, completes after about 2.3 s
    (no slice on checkpoint ticks); at 320 that would be 13 ticks per field (about 0.9 s) and
    about 3.7 s; at 64 about 18 s. Flow-active ticks are about 6 % of the arena run, so they sit
    inside the job p95.
    - Mitigation: lazy fields only when a line is blocked, derived references (no leaks), a
      graceful fallback to `DIRECT` with sliding while a field integrates, fields reused from the
      cache, the size and slice in data, the `flow_slice_p95_ms` line on the card, and no slice
      on hash ticks so the two costs never share a job.
11. **Players cannot disengage** (every ground tap attacks). Mitigation: recorded in HOW_TO_PLAY
    and accepted by Michael (answer 2); a gesture is on the Phase 3 plan's agenda.
12. **Scope creep:** no artillery, heavy tank, buildings, minimap, under-attack indicator, sound,
    info card, `PlayerView`, rig pooling or smooth fog shader. Each needs a plan change first.
13. **Long PR list (80 rows from revision 15: P26, the never-merged probes P26s and P26n2, revision
    14's licence fix P26l, the withdrawn P27f, and 75 implementation PRs, 4 of them conditional
    (P37n2, P41g, P42n, P50lp; P50lp is not taken, calibration 2026-10-13); revision 15 adds 19
    rows for ADRs 0013 and 0014, and moves the gate from slot 30 to 35 (38-39 with every named
    split, up to 44 if every conditional row fires with its splits; the amendment's "the slip").
    Before revision 15: 61 rows, after the revision 3 and 4 splits, P27b's split into P27c, ADR
    0012's P27f, P38s, P38j, P38w and P51r, its revision 2's P38w2, P49b and P50w, and the named
    splits P29c, P34b and P34c; a named split can add a few more).** Lane B gains two lead PRs
    before P40 (P38s and P38j; one more each if P38s2 or P38j2 split) and two before P50a (P38w
    and P38w2; three with the expected P38w1b), so P40, and with it P42a, starts about two PRs
    later (up to four with both splits).
    - Mitigation: every PR is small and single-concern, batch merge approvals (Phase 1 answer
      9), and two lanes from day one.
14. **A data race between the worker and the main thread** would show as rare, unrepeatable
    crashes or a hash mismatch on the phone only. Debug builds refuse and report a write to an
    in-tree Node from a worker; release builds do not, and an unjoined thread only warns
    (verified, ADR 0012 Context).
    - Mitigation:
      - one ownership rule with exactly two semaphores and three named hand-off states (ADR 0012
        §1), with every wait timed;
      - one immutable snapshot per publish;
      - getters on `world`, `loop`, `replay` and `probe` that fail a main-thread read during a
        job;
      - purity bans in `game/sim`, the reachability walk from `TickJob`, the worker-file scan in
        `game/core`, and the scan for `.world`, `.loop`, `.replay` and `.probe` in production
        view code (P50w, before any scene runs the worker);
      - a headless run of the main scene and the arena in `WORKER` mode, where the debug build
        reports any Node touched from the worker;
      - worker tests that compare every published snapshot with `SYNC`, including under real
        overlap and with a slow worker;
      - golden hashes compared on the phone at every gate run.
15. **One tick more input latency on Android** (66.7 ms) could make taps feel slow.
    - Mitigation: move and target markers show at once (view only); the gate's play session
      asks for it explicitly; Michael accepted it for Phase 2 (answer 2 in "Answers (Michael,
      ADR 0012)").
16. **Cross-thread property access is serialized by Godot** (measured: both threads 2-4 times
    slower when each reads `obj.prop` in a loop). A per-row `world.pos_x[row]` on the worker
    while the main thread sets rig positions would slow both, and CI cannot see it.
    - Mitigation: the alias rule of ADR 0012 §2 for both threads (P37a converts
      `Formation.assign`; P38s, P40 and the layers follow it), reviewed through the
      `rts-sim-conventions` Threading section, and measured by the release run's worker mode.
17. **Dual maintenance of the hot phases** (revision 15, ADR 0013 §5): every later rule change in a
    ported phase edits two versions.
    - Mitigation: one owner per rule (gameplay-dev writes both), the twin rule in every row's
      review, and the lockstep test in the same PR.
18. **The toolchain in every session and job** (SCons 4.9.1, g++, binutils, the pinned
    `clang-format`; the NDK in CI).
    - Mitigation: `verify.sh` exits RED naming a missing tool, never a skip; the session hook
      installs the tools (P53); Michael re-pastes `setup.sh` after P53.
19. **Reliance on engine internals:** in-place writes use the internal-pointer getter and the
    Variant's Packed array layout; the import workaround uses `.godot/extension_list.cfg`.
    - Mitigation: the boundary tests and the fresh-clone canary rerun on every pin change; the
      `--recovery-mode` fallback (P53 measures it).
20. **Undefined behaviour in C++ written by a Sonnet agent.**
    - Mitigation: the sanitizer job (P57), the lead's mutants and review, `-fwrapv`, and the
      widening rule (ADR 0013 §4).
21. **The Android library:** 16 KB page alignment, and the libc++ ABI against the template's
    `libc++_shared.so`.
    - Mitigation: P55a's ELF, alignment and libc++ symbol checks (in P55a2, its split, since
      2026-10-13), rerun on every Godot or godot-cpp bump.
22. **The gate's own measures** (revision 15, ADR 0014 Consequences): frame intervals are a proxy
    for presents; a 120 Hz panel can hide half-period judder; normalisation assumes the main
    thread's work scales like the reference loop (optimistic for memory- and driver-bound work
    such as the fog upload) and cancels core placement by design; a per-run `ref_peak` hides
    heat from the main-thread verdicts.
    - Mitigation: the calibration run (P26n2, recorded 2026-10-13: C3 and C4 hold on the
      per-frame method, which is the formula; the load-pinned fallback P50lp is not taken), the
      raw values kept on the card, and pacing judged on dropped frames. Risk 9 is unchanged,
      because the worker stays raw.

## Open questions for Michael
Answered on 2026-10-12 (see "Answers (Michael)"). Questions 2 and 3 are kept as they were asked.
Question 1 is shortened to the option Michael chose, because the plan no longer follows its
recommendation.

1. **Gate phone.** The ROADMAP asks for 60 fps on a mid-range phone from 2021. Your phone
   (2424x1080, probably 2023-2024) runs game logic about 2.5 times faster than such a phone, by
   our estimate. Measured on this computer, the game's logic alone would fill 8-14 ms of a
   16.7 ms frame on a 2021 phone. That is too close to judge from a faster phone without a
   correction. The plan recommended buying a used 2021 mid-range phone for the gate (not taken).
   Michael's choice: the gate runs on his own phone for good, with the limits divided by the
   assumed slowdown of 2.5.
2. **Phase 2 content.** **Recommendation:** yes to all of these:
   - Three unit types: light tank, rifle squad, rocket squad. The heavy tank, artillery and
     harvester come with production in Phase 3.
   - The infantry is shown only in the perf arena (dev menu) until Phase 3 lets you build it.
   - On the normal test map the red tanks now fire back, are hidden by fog until your tanks see
     them, and rock and water appear.
   - Until Phase 3 there is no way to pull units out of a fight: every tap on the ground means
     "go there and fight".

   This is the smallest set that proves armour classes, projectiles and pathfinding, and it keeps
   the Phase 1 controls unchanged.
3. **Telling your units from the enemy's without colour** (an open point in the art style
   guide). Today blue and red units differ only in colour, which colour-blind players cannot
   see. What the plan builds: health bars have two shapes, and an enemy bar has a notch, so
   whenever an enemy's bar shows (it was just hit) you can tell it apart without colour. An
   enemy that has not been hit shows no bar, so in a calm moment colour is still the only cue.
   **Recommendation:** accept the notched enemy bar for Phase 2. Options:
   - an always-on small marker under every visible enemy (a notched rect, drawn in the same
     pass as the bars, so almost free on the phone). It costs one more PR (P45b, about 200
     lines, after P45). Not built (Michael, 2026-10-12); revisit in Phase 5;
   - a different team stripe shape per side, which needs a second set of team drawings for
     every unit (art work, not recommended now).

## Answers (Michael, 2026-10-12)
This section records the answers as given. The values in answer 1 that ADR 0012 changes
(`work_p95_ms` measured on all frames, `sim_p95_ms` 13.3 ms on the worker, the slice of about
2 ms) are listed in "Amendment (ADR 0012)" below. Answer 1's rule (Michael's phone, k = 2.5) stands.

Michael answered in chat ("1 nej, 2 ja, 3 ja") and approved the merge of P26. ADRs 0007-0011 are
accepted.
1. **No: no 2021 phone is bought.** The gate runs on Michael's own phone (2424x1080, 420 dpi,
   Android) permanently, not as a stand-in until another phone arrives. Consequences:
   - The thresholds are the 2021 limits divided by k = 2.5 (ADR 0011 §5 and its acceptance
     note): tick-frame work p95 5.6 ms, device sim p95 3.2 ms, fog update p95 0.4 ms. The
     frame-pacing rules (17.5 ms, 95% on time, a 600-frame heavy window) and the draw-call
     ceiling (60) are not divided.
   - CLAUDE.md rule 6 stays as it is: the divided limits stand in for the "mid-range 2021
     phone" budget, and ADR 0011 §5 states what they do not model (GPU, memory bandwidth, heat).
   - No later data-only correction from another phone. A slower test phone, or another k, comes
     only through a new ADR if field data demands it.
   - The P26s probe had not run when Michael approved P26. It runs on his phone after the merge
     and before P34, and changes no threshold. It gives the phone-to-CI ratio for P49's
     `budget.cfg`, the value of `flow_cells_per_tick` (a slice of about 0.2 ms on his phone; a
     one-value data change if it is not 512) and a check of the draw contracts (a failed
     contract goes through a new ADR before P34).
   - The limits are tight, and the sim p95 is the likely first miss (risk 1); ADR 0011 §6 orders
     the remedies.
2. **Yes to all of the Phase 2 content.** Three unit types (light tank, rifle squad, rocket
   squad); infantry only in the perf arena until Phase 3 lets players build it; on the normal
   test map the red tanks fire back, hide in fog until seen, and rock and water appear; no way to
   pull units out of a fight until Phase 3. Consequences: the scope, the data tables and the PR

3. **Yes: the notched enemy health bar is the colour-blind cue for Phase 2.** Consequences:
   - no always-on enemy marker: the optional PR P45b is not built (Michael, 2026-10-12) and has
     no row in the PR table;
   - P34 writes the decision into STYLE.md's open point about team colours (P26 does not edit
     STYLE.md);
   - Phase 5 (polish) revisits the cue if playtesters have trouble telling the sides apart, for
     example with the always-on marker or a second team-stripe shape. This note lives in this
     plan; `docs/ROADMAP.md` does not change for it.

## Amendment (ADR 0012), 2026-10-12
**Why.** The P26s probe ran on the gate phone (Pixel 9a, debug build) on 2026-10-12. Its reports
are in PR #35 and its numbers in ADR 0012's Context:
- the render side passed;
- the 15 Hz tick inside the frame made every tick frame miss (20.6 ms p95, 83.5 % of frames on
  time against 95 %);
- the mock tick alone took about five times `sim_p95_ms`;
- a 0.2 ms flow slice would hold about 28 cells;
- alternating team tints cost 161 draw calls.

That contradicts ADR 0011 (budget, gate, team-tint batching) and ADR 0008 §3 (flow slice). The
P26s rule ("a contract the probe contradicts changes through a new ADR before P34 starts") applies,
and Michael approved writing ADR 0012 ("ok ADR 0012"). Revision 8 put the ADR into the rows,
sections and tables above, revision 9 follows the ADR's revision 2 and revision 10 its revision
3. The docs PR that carries it is exempt from the ~400-line rule, as P26 was.

**Decisions** (ADR 0012, accepted 2026-10-12; revision 2 after a review in four lenses and
revision 3 after the completeness check, which this plan's revisions 9 and 10 follow):
1. **Worker-thread tick** (§1-2): on Android and desktop, `MatchRunner` runs `TickJob` on one
   dedicated `Thread`, handing off through two semaphores, one tick ahead, with three named
   hand-off states and timed waits. A job not finished at its boundary is held, never run twice,
   and no command is dropped. A tick counts as late only when the worker itself was slow (post to
   done above the period), so a main-thread hitch never counts. The thread is joined when the
   runner is freed, in the tree or not. `world`, `loop`, `replay` and `probe` fail a main-thread
   read during a job. `SYNC` mode serves every headless test (except the worker suites),
   `run_ticks`, replays, `tests/perf` and the web build. `AUTO` is set in `main.tscn` and
   `perf_arena.tscn` in P50b, after P50w. Sim code gains bans on statics, signals,
   `call_deferred`, loads and threading; both threads alias object properties before per-row
   loops; determinism is unchanged.
2. **`MatchSnapshot`** (§3): the view reads an immutable copy of each tick, a new object per
   publish, never `runner.world`. Prev positions are aligned by id in the copy, projectile prevs
   are kept by slot id, and the copy carries `target_row` and `cell_of`.
3. **Gate and budget** (§4). Changed fields only; the others are unchanged:

   | Field | Before (ADR 0011 acceptance note) | Now (ADR 0012 §4) |
   | --- | --- | --- |
   | `work_p95_ms` | 5.6 ms, tick frames of the heavy window, sim in the frame | 5.6 ms, all frames of the heavy window, main thread only (sim on the worker) |
   | `work_tick_p95_ms` | - | 5.6 ms, the heavy window's publish frames only |
   | `sim_p95_ms` | 3.2 ms, `Sim.step` in the frame, debug APK | 13.3 ms, the whole worker job, release template (33.3 ms, half the period, on a 2021 phone / 2.5) |
   | `job_p99_ms` | - | 26.7 ms (one period on a 2021 phone / 2.5) |
   | `late_ticks_max` | - | 0 (no job longer than one period from post to done) |
   | `release_only` | - (the gate used the debug APK) | true |
   | `ci_ratio_mean`, `ci_ratio_p95` | - (the P26s ratio was to be quoted in P49) | 4.4 and 4.6, provisional (debug run), not thresholds; 3.6 and 3.8 from the release run (answers below; superseded, revision 14: 4.4 and 5.9 from its worker mode) |

   A run whose draw-call or render CPU p95 reads 0 is `INVALID`. The CI wall-clock budget is a
   regression ratchet (P49b), not a device ceiling: the ceiling 13.3 / 4.6 = 2.9 ms sits below
   ADR 0011's own floor rule. CI prints the phone estimate instead, and P46's estimate is the
   decision point for drafting the GDExtension ADR. The release run (both modes) sets the ratios
   (the lead, exception 5) and `flow_cells_per_tick` (gameplay-dev); no gate threshold and no
   lane waits for it.
4. **Flow slice** (§5): `flow_cells_per_tick` 256 from the debug run at proposal, 320 after the
   release run (about 3.7 s for the arena opening's four fields; 4.6 s at 256); no slice on
   checkpoint ticks, and units steer `DIRECT` meanwhile. (Superseded, revision 14: the
   headroom rule gives 64, and P27f is held.)
5. **Draw z per batch group** (§6): hull and turret per type, team per type and owner. That
   covers interleaved tints (161 calls on the phone) and interleaved type textures (150 calls
   for 150 sprites, verified with Xvfb). There is no second art set, the rig node contract is
   unchanged, and the ceiling stays at 60, with a MultiMesh trigger above a 40-call forecast.
6. **Stays** (§7): answer 1 (Michael's phone, k = 2.5) and CLAUDE.md rule 6. GDExtension is not
   adopted. Its ADR is proposed only if a release perf-arena card on the worker misses
   `sim_p95_ms` or `job_p99_ms`, or shows late ticks, after remedy steps 1 and 2; the lead
   starts drafting it earlier if P46's `est_phone_p95_ms` is above 26.6 ms.

**New PRs** (P27c, P27b's named split from the same day, is recorded too):

| PR | Lane | Owner | ~Lines | Depends on | What |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P27f | A | gameplay-dev | 15 | P27b, acceptance | `flow_cells_per_tick` 320 (ADR 0012 §5's 2 ms rule on the release slice mean of 3.13 ms); the worker-mode run checks the headroom and may lower it in a one-value follow-up (superseded, revision 14: 320 is withdrawn and P27f is held, see the P27f row) |
| P38s | B | lead | 380, split trigger (P38s2) | P38, P32 (and P32f) | `MatchSnapshot` (pure), capture by `duplicate()`, prev by id, `target_row`, `cell_of`, copied-column coverage |
| P38j | B | lead | 360, split trigger (P38j2) | P38s, P38t | `TickJob`; the runner publishes one new snapshot per tick (`SYNC`) and the tick-0 snapshot at `start()`; the runner sets `match_serial` on the new snapshot before the capture, the job sets `last_hash` after the step, with a two-tick prev case that fails on the wrong order; runner prevs become forwards; reachability and worker-file guards |
| P38w | B | lead | 405: the named split (P38w1b) is the expected outcome | P38j (and P38j2) | worker `Thread`, hand-off states and table, timed `wait_idle`, join on free, guarded getters with setters, `resolve_mode`, the `Thread.start()` fallback, lockstep and life-cycle tests |
| P38w2 | B | lead | 340, split trigger (P38w3) | P38w (and P38w1b) | the `WORKER` clock (`TICK_USEC` from `Sim.TICK_RATE`), held and late ticks, overlap, slow-worker, hitch and command cases |
| P49a | A | qa-engineer | 300 | P46, P38p, P38j | P49's bounds, golden hashes and `TickJob` timing with the printed phone estimate; wall clock report-only |
| P49b | A | qa-engineer | 80 | P49a, P46b | the wall-clock budget as a regression ratchet; ticks box 6 |
| P50w | B | qa-engineer | 240 | P47p, P38w2 | the snapshot-only scan and the main scene on the worker, headless |
| P51r | A | release-engineer | 130 | P51b (lane order) | release-template perf APK with the dev menu and the not-debuggable check, for the gate |

**Changed PRs:**
- **Sim and data:**
  - P27q: the purity bans (statics, signals, `call_deferred`, loads, threading); otherwise P38p
    carries them;
  - P27b: none; it opened before ADR 0012 and keeps 512, and the new value comes in P27f;
  - P29b: `World.cell_of` clamps x and y into the map (ADR 0012 §3), with its case on corners,
    edges and positions outside the map (about 385, split trigger);
  - P32: the added method `FogGrid.copy_into(dst)`, with the P32f split expected;
  - P33: every Command factory duplicates its arrays, with one test per factory;
  - P37a: `Formation.assign` aliases the World arrays before its loops;
  - P37b: no flow slice on checkpoint ticks.
- **Art:** P34 waits for acceptance; it also rewords STYLE.md's two per-rig draw-order lines.
- **Core:**
  - P38: prev re-alignment moves to P38s, so about 270 lines and no split;
  - P38t: the new `PerfGateDef` fields and values;
  - P38p: the new `perf_gate.tres` values and the printed phone estimate.
- **View:**
  - P36: reads `runner.world` until P40;
  - P40: snapshot reads (with `MapView`) without a cached snapshot member, aliased loops, draw z
    per group, `MarkerLayer` z 150, and `test_picker`'s fill case writing the snapshot;
  - P43a: snapshot reads in `PlayerController` and `SelectionPanel`, and `test_army_bar`'s "All
    army" case gaining `run_ticks(1)`;
  - P43b: a phone-check note on targets at the edge of sight;
  - P44a: turret aim from `target_row`;
  - P45: hit times re-aligned by id, with a death case;
  - P44b, P47: snapshot reads (P47 with `snapshot.cell_of`) and z 110-140.
- **Perf and gate:**
  - P49 becomes P49a and P49b (above);
  - P50a: new summary keys and verdicts (`job_p99_ms`, `work_tick_p95_ms`, late against held
    ticks), checkpoints from the snapshot, `set_probe`, with its named split now expected;
  - P50a2: about 380 with split trigger P50a3, and the zero-monitor `INVALID` rule;
  - P50b: `AUTO` in both scenes after P50w; latency and judder judged on the APK only;
  - P52: the perf arena on the worker headless, and cross-file facts for the new table (the
    snapshot-only scan moved to P50w; the ceiling fact is dropped);
  - Gate: the release APK of P51r, with the new verdicts.

The two-lane rule holds, with the new lead PRs and P50w in lane B and P51r, P49a and P49b in
lane A. Every PR estimated at 330 or more names its split. P38w (about 405) is expected to split
into P38w and P38w1b before it opens, as P32 and P50a are. P38s (380; 395 from revision 12), P38j (360) and P38w2 (340)
split at their named lines (P38s2, P38j2, P38w3) if their first commits pass 400; with Phase 1's
review growth of up to 30 % they may still end near 450 after review, as every Phase 1 PR did.

**Unchanged:**
- the sim contracts (`Sim.step`, `MatchLoop`, World v1, the probe ids), apart from four rules
  that change no signature: Command factories duplicate their arrays (P33), `Formation.assign`
  aliases its arrays (P37a), no flow slice on checkpoint ticks (P37b), and `World.cell_of`
  clamps into the map (P29b). The one addition is a new method, `FogGrid.copy_into` (P32); no
  existing signature changes;
- the ROADMAP lines (box 6 now ticks in P49b);
- the HOW_TO_PLAY rows (no control changes; the tick of latency is not a player-facing rule);
- file ownership, apart from exception 5 and qa's new `tests/scenes/test_worker_scenes.gd`;
- the Phase 1 audited tables, apart from the `test_match_runner` row (P38j), the `MarkerLayer`
  z, `test_picker`'s fill case (P40) and `test_army_bar`'s "All army" case (P43a).

**Release run (Michael, once, about 4 minutes).** Status 2026-10-12: the in-frame mode ran on a
release build without the worker mode (outputs in P26s and in ADR 0012's acceptance note). The
worker mode is still open: steps 1, 2 and 4 with a new build that adds it. (Closed, history, P26n
docs: both modes ran on 2026-10-12, the worker mode in revision 14, and PR #35 was closed
unmerged on 2026-10-14 ("luk #35 og #41", P26m). The steps below are no longer instructions;
there is no probe APK to install.)
1. The lead adds the worker mode to the probe branch first (about 40 lines).
2. Uninstall rts-mobile and install the release-template probe APK from PR #35.
3. Dev → Perf probe in the in-frame mode, wait 60 s, then "Copy report" and paste it.
4. Switch to the worker mode, wait 60 s, then "Copy report" and paste it.

The lead then posts both in PR #35 and opens the ratio PR. gameplay-dev opens the
`flow_cells_per_tick` PR if the headroom rule gives a value other than 320 (P27f's value; superseded,
revision 14: it gave 64, and P27f is held). If draw calls or
render CPU read 0 on release, the lead records it as a finding for P50a's render line (they read
above 0: 13 calls, 1.65 ms mean; no finding).

**On acceptance** (lead, docs only; done 2026-10-12 after Michael's "1 ja, 2 ja, 3 ja, 4 ja"):
- set ADR 0012 to accepted (done, with an acceptance note on the release run);
- add one status line each to ADRs 0003 (§7, the `WORKER` clock only; `SYNC` unchanged), 0007
  (§6, §10), 0008 (§3), 0010 (§1-2) and 0011 (§1, §2, §3, §4 including the withdrawn 4.0 ms start,
  §5, §6 step 4, and the sim cost estimate) (done);
- only after Michael's yes to open question 4 (the file has no owner row, and agent definitions
  change only with his approval; his yes to question 1 does not cover it):
  `.claude/agents/qa-engineer.md` line 22 (continued on line 23), "start at 4 ms per tick on CI,
  tighten as the game grows", becomes "a regression ratchet at 1.5 x the calibrated min-of-3
  (ADR 0012 §4); a sim PR never raises it, and a rise that a new tick phase causes is a
  qa-engineer PR with before and after numbers and its reason". The lead makes this one change
  before P49b opens. P49b depends on it: with a no, the lead brings the conflict back to Michael
  before P49b opens, because the old line would make qa-engineer start `budget.cfg` at 4 ms
  (done: Michael said yes to question 4);
- apply the `rts-sim-conventions` edits: a "Threading" section with the rules of ADR 0012 §2
  (including the alias rule for the worker and the main thread, and World reads only inside
  `ticked` or after `wait_idle()`), the flow-slice line ("no slice on checkpoint ticks;
  `flow_cells_per_tick` from the release run's headroom"), and "the view reads
  `MatchSnapshot`, never the World" (done; the flow line names 320, superseded by revision 14:
  the lead's docs PR changes it, see "Recorded now");
- unblock P34 and P27f (done).

**Open questions for Michael (ADR 0012):** answered on 2026-10-12 ("1 ja, 2 ja, 3 ja, 4 ja"; see
"Answers (Michael, ADR 0012)" below). The questions are kept as they were asked.
1. **Accept ADR 0012?** The game logic moves to a second thread on the phone. The gate then
   uses the release build of the app, with the limits on your phone re-derived:
   - screen work 5.6 ms per frame, also on the frames that receive a new game state;
   - game logic 13.3 ms per tick for 95 % of ticks (half of the 66.7 ms tick period on a 2021
     phone, divided by 2.5) and 26.7 ms for 99 % of ticks (a full period, divided by 2.5);
   - no tick whose logic took longer than a full period.

   **Recommendation:** yes. It is the only option found that takes the game logic out of the
   frame without C++, so the picture stops stuttering on every tick. It does not by itself make
   the game logic fast enough: our estimate for the release build is 10.4-13.3 ms against the
   13.3 ms limit for the test logic alone, and the real logic does more. That shows in the
   release run, then in a check after the fog PR (P46), and at the latest at the gate. If it is
   not enough, the next step is a separate ADR for C++ (GDExtension) for the slowest parts, and
   its draft starts early if P46's estimate is far off.
2. **One tick of extra tap delay** (66.7 ms, about 0.17 s from tap to movement instead of about
   0.1 s), on the phone and desktop only. The move and target markers still appear at once.
   **Recommendation:** accept it for Phase 2. The gate's play session checks how it feels.
3. **A second rolling test download, `android-perf-release`** (the release build with the dev
   menu, for the gate). Switching between it and `android-dev-latest` needs an uninstall,
   because the signing keys differ. **Recommendation:** yes. It updates on every merge to
   `main`, like `android-dev-latest`. If no: the release-engineer publishes it only for the gate
   commit, on request, and the lead deletes it after the gate.
4. **Change one rule in the QA assistant's instructions?** Its instructions say that the
   automatic speed test on the build server starts at a limit of 4 ms per game tick. The phone
   test showed that the game logic alone already needs more than that on the build server (an
   estimated 6 ms for the full game), so with that rule the speed test (P49b) could never pass,
   even when the game is fast enough on your phone. The new rule: the build server's limit is
   1.5 times what it measures on the finished Phase 2 game, and it only catches the game getting
   slower than before. A feature change may never raise it. Only the QA assistant may raise it,
   in its own change with the numbers before and after and the reason (for example when Phase 3
   adds buildings and economy). Whether the game is fast enough is still decided on your phone.
   **Recommendation:** yes. The 4 ms figure was a guess that the phone test proved wrong, and the
   assistants' instructions change only with your approval, so a yes to question 1 does not
   cover it.

## Answers (Michael, ADR 0012)
Michael answered in chat on 2026-10-12: "1 ja, 2 ja, 3 ja, 4 ja". ADR 0012 is accepted; its
acceptance note records the answers and the release run.
1. **Yes: ADR 0012 is accepted.** Consequences:
   - the gate thresholds of ADR 0012 §4 are final (work 5.6 ms on all heavy-window frames and on
     publish frames, worker job p95 13.3 ms and p99 26.7 ms, no late tick, release template);
   - the status lines are in ADRs 0003 (§7, the `WORKER` clock only), 0007 (§6, §10), 0008 (§3),
     0010 (§1-2) and 0011 (§1-§6 as listed, including the withdrawn 4.0 ms start and the sim cost
     estimate), and `rts-sim-conventions` has the Threading section, the flow-slice line and the
     snapshot rule;
   - P34 and P27f are unblocked. Lane B continues with P34; lane A slots P27f in between any two
     of its PRs.
2. **Yes: one tick of extra tap delay** (66.7 ms) on Android and desktop is accepted for Phase 2.
   Consequences: no change to the PRs; the move and target markers still appear at once; the
   gate's play session (step 3 of the gate procedure) checks how it feels; if taps feel slow
   there, the lead brings it back to Michael with the options. HOW_TO_PLAY does not change (the
   latency is not a player-facing rule).
3. **Yes: `android-perf-release` is a rolling pre-release**, updated on every merge to `main` like
   `android-dev-latest`. Consequences: P51r publishes it from the CI job on push to `main`; the
   gate-commit-only fallback is not built. Switching between the two downloads still needs an
   uninstall (different signing keys), as the gate procedure says.
4. **Yes: the qa-engineer role's budget rule changes.** `.claude/agents/qa-engineer.md` now
   says: a regression ratchet at 1.5 x the calibrated min-of-3 (ADR 0012 §4); a sim PR never
   raises it, and a rise that a new tick phase causes is a qa-engineer PR with before and after
   numbers and its reason. Consequences: P49b's dependency on this change is met. qa-engineer
   calibrates `budget.cfg` after P46b and does not start it at 4 ms. A calibrated mean above
   4.0 ms is not a finding.

**Release run so far** (in-frame mode, recorded in P26s and ADR 0012's acceptance note; the
worker mode in revision 14):
- the in-frame ratios were 3.6 (mean) and 3.8 (p95). The worker-mode run, on which ADR 0012 §4
  defines them, gives 4.4 and 5.9: the values the CI side uses for the printed phone estimate.
  P38t (`PerfGateDef` defaults) and P38p (`perf_gate.tres`) ship them, and `budget.cfg` does not
  move with them;
- `flow_cells_per_tick` was 320 by ADR 0012 §5's 2 ms rule; the worker-mode headroom gives 64,
  and P27f is held (revision 14);
- the mock tick's release p95 of 12.63 ms is inside the 13.3 ms worker limit with about 5 %
  margin, so the real sim is expected to be marginal. The GDExtension trigger stays as ADR 0012
  §7 wrote it. (Superseded, revision 14: the worker-mode job p95 of 19.75-20.10 ms misses
  13.3 ms by 48-51 %, risk 1's early-warning finding; the response proposed is ADR 0013.)

**Done (revision 14):** the worker-mode run. Its job p95 (19.75 ms) is above 11.6 ms, so the §5
headroom gives 64 and the early-warning finding of risk 1; the ratios are 4.4 and 5.9.

**Why.**
- **ADR 0013, the sim's CPU time.** On the gate phone, release template, the GDScript mock tick on
  the worker took 18.88-19.16 ms p95, against the 13.3 ms `sim_p95_ms` limit (job p95 19.75 and
  20.10 ms; PR #35 release run 2, PR #41). The C++ port of the same tick took 0.43 ms p95 (job
  mean 0.44 ms, p95 0.88 ms), and it was bit-identical on arm64.
- **ADR 0014, the gate's own measures.** Two measurements showed that the gate misjudges:
  - the on-time share (≤ 17.5 ms) swings with no change in the work: mode 4 (the GDScript worker)
    measured 95.0 % in release run 2 and 98.2 % in the spike session, and mode 5, with less work
    (an almost idle C++ worker), 93.6 %;
  - main-thread work in raw ms rose when the main thread got lighter (6.04-6.93 ms with the tick
    off the frame, against 4.18-4.37 ms with it in the frame), which is consistent with the
    governor lowering the clock for a lighter thread (ADR 0014 Context, problem 2).

already accepted in ADR 0012 to the worker-mode run; apart from the P27f hold that the third one
names, they are not new decisions. The last two, the ROADMAP licences line and P26l, are a
licence fix that no ADR decides.

  - spike session, mode 4: job mean 11.87 ms, p95 20.10 ms.

**What holds until acceptance** (history; revision 15 ends it: the lanes take the order under

- **Lanes.** The lanes continue as above:

  outcome: as the sim if ADR 0013 is rejected, and as the GDScript twin (the web path and the
  lockstep reference) if it is accepted.
- **A recommendation, not a contract change.** Keep the state a hot loop changes in Packed arrays
  and int members of the World or of a helper object. A Dictionary there would block a port; an
  Array of helper objects costs a checked fetch per element (ADR 0013 §2), which `FlowCache`'s
  slots can afford because they are few. For P30b: size every slot's `cost`, `next` and buckets
  once in `configure()`, so `begin` never resizes (also fewer allocations under ADR 0007 §1).
  For P32 (lane B runs it before acceptance is likely): each player's fog dirty list is a fixed
  block of one flat `PackedInt32Array` (`player_count` blocks of `cells / 4`, ADR 0010 §1's
  capacity), sized in `configure()`, with a `PackedInt32Array` of counts and one of "full" flags;
  `begin_update` resets the player's count and flag and never resizes, and no `Array` of Packed
  arrays holds the lists. `dirty_cells(player)` keeps its contract signature and returns a copy
  of the used part (only the view calls it, on the snapshot's copy). On acceptance both become
  the rows' rules (ADR 0013 §2).
- **No `native/` yet.** No PR adds `native/`, `ClassDB`, `World.kernels` or a C++ build step
  before ADR 0013 is accepted.
- **P27f is held** (PR #41 finding 4, revision 14) until Michael answers question 1 below. `main`
  keeps P27b's 512, and nothing waits for P27f.
- **The probe branches stay unmerged.** `claude/p26s-perf-probe` and `claude/p26n-cpp-spike` stay
  unmerged. ADR 0014's calibration build goes on the spike branch.
- **Gate fields.** If P38t opens before ADR 0014 is accepted, it ships ADR 0012 §4's fields, and
  the lead changes them in one follow-up after acceptance. Its position after P38 makes this
  unlikely.

### ADR 0013: changes to existing rows (applied in revision 15)
**Twin rule for every GDScript row that merges after a port** (ADR 0013 §5): a row whose file list edits a module whose phase has a merged port (P41 for `Movement.gd`, P46b for `Targeting.gd`, and every later row) edits the C++ kernel in the same PR and passes that kernel's lockstep test there. Its line estimate grows by the kernel edit and its lockstep cases; the reviewer checks the Files line against the ports merged by then.

**Rule for every ported module.** The GDScript PR stays as written. The module's port PR (next
table) adds the dispatch as the first line of the phase's entry (ADR 0013 §3's table:
`FlowCache.run(world, budget)` for FLOW, the module's `run(world)` for the others, and
`SpatialGrid._rebuild_one(world, which)` for GRIDS only if its rule fires):
`if ((world.native_phases >> SimProbe.Phase.X) & 1) == 1: world.kernels.x(world[, int]); return`.
It also adds the C++ kernel, its line in `bind/kernel_list.h`, its bit in `sim_impls.gd`'s
`EXPECTED_PHASES`, its case in P54's dispatch test, and the lockstep test.

### ADR 0013: new PRs (applied in revision 15)
**Estimating basis for the ports:** about 1.2-1.5 times the GDScript code lines of the module (the
spike's 2.2 included bind boilerplate, which P54 shares), plus a 900-tick lockstep test and the
crafted cases. (Superseded for the rows P37n, P39n, P41n, P42n and P46n by **the port-size rule**,
Michael's decision in chat on 2026-10-14, verbatim **"ja til port-reglen"**, recorded in P26m: a
native port row is estimated at its real size, about 1,000 changed lines (about 450 of C++ and
about 600 of module tests under both implementations, which ADR 0013 §2 requires to land with
the kernel; P30n came to 1,089 with about 460 of C++), and it splits at the lockstep: part 2 is
the lockstep test, as P30n and P30n2 did. This basis undercounted P30n, 340 against 1,089, because
it left out the module tests under both implementations.) Every port names a split trigger: kernel \| lockstep and crafted cases, unless
its row names another. The first part of a split always holds the module's tests under both
implementations, so no part merges C++ that no test runs; a `core/` function lands only in the
PR of the kernel that calls it (ADR 0013 §2). **Conditional rows** (P37n2, P41g, P42n) exist only if ADR 0013 §1's rule
fires for their phase: (a) the phase's mean on the `PERF` line of the named PR (or of P46) times
`ci_ratio_mean` is above 0.67 ms (5 % of `sim_p95_ms`), or (b) the phase runs on only some ticks
and its per-active-tick cost decides a headroom value of the ADR 0012 §5 kind (FLOW's clause; a
run mean cannot show it). The lead applies it in that PR's review
and records the numbers in the PR; a row that fires takes the slot right after that PR.
