The lead brings the choice (keep r28, or pin r29 to match the template) to Michael before P55a starts; this plan does not decide it. **The NDK choice is made** (P26i, 2026-10-13): Michael answered in chat on 2026-10-13, verbatim: **"NDK r29"**. The kernels are built with NDK **29.0.14206865**, the NDK of Godot 4.7.2's Android template, whose `libc++_shared.so` is LLVM 21 / Android clang r563880c (ADR 0013 §8's status note). P55a applies it: `NDK_VERSION` in `scripts/build-native.sh` (with the comments there that name 28.1.13356709 or the open choice), `scripts/ci/install-ndk.sh` (which reads that line) and the stub NDK of `scripts/ci/test-native-scripts.sh` (the pin's comment names it). The libc++ symbol check above stays. godot-cpp's own default at the pin stays r28 (`tools/android.py`, 28.1.13356709); `build-native.sh` passes `ndk_version=${NDK_VERSION}` to SCons, and building with r29 is P55a's to prove in CI. **The split is taken** (lead, 2026-10-13, P26j): **P55a** is the NDK install (`scripts/ci/install-ndk.sh`) with its offline test, NDK r29 applied (the three places named above), the Android arm64 `template_debug` and `template_release` builds in CI after the host's Linux library (below), and the fix of `native/sim_kernels/SConstruct`'s comment that says godot-cpp fills `CPPEXTPATH` (ADR 0013 §12's status note: godot-cpp leaves it empty; the next PR that edits `SConstruct` corrects the comment); **P55a2** (release-engineer, right after P55a in lane B, Depends P55a) is the export checks and notices: `export-android.sh`'s checks of the export mode's entry, `libc++_shared.so`, the ELF facts and the libc++ symbol check, the logged `.note.android.ident` and alignment facts, and the godot-cpp notice in the APK's and the web build's notices with the offline pins test. **The host library comes first** (P26i, P53 review): the generated `native/sim_kernels.gdextension` names all six libraries (`linux` x86_64 and arm64, `android` arm64, each debug and release), so a job that builds the Android library must also build the Linux library of its host first, or the host editor logs `Can't open dynamic library`; a real `--export-debug` Android export is where Godot checks that the library of the export mode exists. **Compiler and optimisation** (P26i, P53 review): `build-native.sh` needs a compiler and optimisation pass-through (it has none at `ebb3b91`); P55 (in flight) adds `--compiler` and `--optimize`. **Merged** (P26k, 2026-10-13): P55a as #67 and P55a2 as #68. As merged, the Android job builds with NDK r29 and `SConstruct` links Android with `-Wl,-z,max-page-size=16384` and `-Wl,-z,common-page-size=16384` (the second ends `PT_GNU_RELRO` on a 16 KB boundary; a real build without it ends RELRO at 0x6d000 and fails the check); the template's `.note.android.ident` strings read `Android r29 14206865` (`libgodot_android.so`) and `Android r28 13004108` (`libc++_shared.so`, expected: NDK r29 ships that file). The measured times and the rest are in rows P55a and P55a2 of the PR table and in ADR 0013's status notes (Context, §8, §10) |
| P56 | B | qa-engineer | 300 | P53 | The C++ scan over `native/sim_kernels/src/core/**` (ADR 0013 §2, §4 and §6: floats, `<cmath>`, time, randomness, threads and atomics, mutable statics and `thread_local`, allocation without `// alloc-ok`, unordered containers and `std::hash`, Godot headers, SIMD intrinsics, `char`, `short`, `int` and `long` as types, integer literals other than 0, 1 and -1 outside `constexpr` mirrors of a GDScript `const`, raw `int32_t`/`uint8_t` pointers outside `WorldView`); the `bind/` scan (no parameter of a `Packed*Array` type, by value or by reference, anywhere in `bind/`; the float, time, randomness and thread bans; Godot headers and the casts allowed); the `ClassDB` and `SimKernels` bans in `game/sim`; one flagged and one legal fixture per pattern. **Known limits, left to review** (P26j, 2026-10-13, P56 final check, #64; `rts-sim-conventions`' "Native kernels" checklist lists them for reviewers): `tests/project/test_native_purity.gd` misses a mutable static with a named or expression direct-init (`static int64_t calls(K);`); a `constexpr` lambda without a parameter list exempts its body from the literal rule; only the first declarator of a view pointer list is collected (`const int32_t *pos_x, *pos_y;`), and a view method that returns the raw pointer is not caught; rarer float helpers are not in its word lists (`UtilityFunctions` `asinh`, `acosh`, `atanh`, `is_nan`, `is_inf`, `ease`, `remap` and similar; `std::` `nextafter`, `erf`, `tgamma` and similar); a namespace-scope `static const StringName n(NAME);`; a function-local `static const auto x = make();`; `std::array<const T, N>` (a `const` among the template arguments reads as a const variable); it reports false positives for static members inside `extern "C" { }` and `struct alignas(8) S { }` (a head that is not read as a scope); and the blind spot its header names, a global without `static` or `inline`. **Lead decision** (2026-10-13): these are review items; a hardening PR comes only if a port PR's review finds one of them in real code. **P56b** (#78, merged 2026-10-14; P26n docs): the P30n review (#74) found that `raw_pointer` matched only `int8`/`int32` pointers, so P56b adds the rule `view_pointer` (a raw pointer member of any element type, `T *`, `int64_t *` or a template parameter, declared public or protected in a `core/` `*View`, templates included; a struct is public until a `private:`, a class private until a `public:`), and the cross-file use check (reported as `raw_pointer`) reads every view pointer member, of any element type and section. It collects every declarator of a member list (`_declared_pointers` ends a member at a top-level `,`), so the limit "only the first declarator of a view pointer list is collected" no longer holds. The scan header now names, as left to review, a global without `static` or `inline` and, for views, a method that returns the pointer, a `using` alias of it, a pointer in a struct nested in a view, a member defined outside the view body and a view that `VIEW` does not name; P56b's PR text also names an `alignas(...)` specifier, a macro without a `;`, a friend view reading another view's pointer and a contrived `operator*=`. Its qa follow-ups (among them the header's blind spots) are P56c's (merged, #83, 2026-10-14; P26o docs: row P56c) |
| P30n | B | gameplay-dev | 340, split trigger (P30n2); split taken at the lockstep (P26m, 2026-10-14): P30n 1,089 at merge (#74), P30n2 280 (#75) | P30b, P54, P56 | The FLOW kernel `flow(world, budget)`: `FlowField.advance` (Dial buckets, corner rule, padded grid, the swap) and `FlowCache.recount` and `advance`. **From the merged P30b** (P26i, 2026-10-13, P30b review): `FlowField.begin()`, which `FlowCache.advance()` calls on a `WAITING` slot (then sets it `ACTIVE`), and `reserve()`'s clear of `complete` when it resizes join the behaviour P30n ports. The names FLOW's checked list draws from (row P54) are `FlowCache`'s `cols`, `rows`, `classes`, `requests`, its `STATE_ARRAYS` (the seven Packed arrays `key_goal`, `key_class`, `key_version`, `last_used`, `ref_count`, `requested`, `status`) and `fields` (the typed `Array[FlowField]`), and per slot `FlowField.STATE_ARRAYS` plus the ints `goal_cell`, `move_class`, `grid_cols`, `cell_count`, `stride`, `level`, `pending` and the bool `complete`; the list holds those the kernel gets or sets. `FlowCache.run` does not call `invalidate()`: `World.set_terrain()` does (a decision of the lead, "Lead decisions and answers (P26i)"). **The GDScript order it mirrors** (P26h, 2026-10-13, from the merged P30a): the buckets are LIFO (a cell is linked at the head of its bucket); a decrease-key unlinks the cell from its old bucket (the head, or `link_next` of its `link_prev`) before linking it at the head of the new one; a pop moves only the bucket's head to `link_next[cell]` and leaves a stale `link_prev` on the new head, which is never read (a cell of the bucket being drained is never relaxed), so the kernel must never "clean it up" (the lockstep compares the full state, `link_prev` included); neighbours in `DX`/`DY` order with the contract's step rule; the completion swap exchanges the two Variants (`cost` and `next`) and sets `complete`, so FLOW's checked list needs the bool kind (ADR 0013 §2's status note of 2026-10-13). `best_neighbour` (ported in P37n) needs no terrain table: it reads only `cost`; the `FlowCache.run` entry with the dispatch (P30n owns the entry; if P37b added it first without the dispatch, P30n adds the dispatch, and if P37b merged before acceptance with the `FlowCache.advance` line, P30n changes that line); `tests/sim/native/test_flow_lockstep.gd` (900 arena ticks, 12 fields with odd budgets, the full state after every call, one run on non-default rules); `test_flow_field` and `test_flow_cache` under both implementations; ADR 0013 §2's write cases in `test_kernel_abi.gd`, through the FLOW kernel and each also on a worker `Thread` (about 40 lines): an in-place write visible on the slot member and through an alias taken before the call, a held `duplicate()` keeping its old values, the `FlowCache` and slot scalars set on exit arriving, and a budget-0 call after an ordinary FLOW call (a non-zero budget, through `FlowCache.run`) leaving `state_hash` unchanged; P30n needs no `Sim.step` tick for it. They stay in the first part if P30n splits, and so do the `FlowCache.run` entry and its dispatch. `best_neighbour` and `acquire` are not here: no FLOW code calls them, so they land with INTEGRATE (P37n), whose lockstep runs them (ADR 0013 §2). Its module tests under both implementations assert that `SimImpls.use(world, SimImpls.Impl.NATIVE)` returns an empty list and that `SimImpls.is_native(world, phase)` holds for its phase (P26l docs, 2026-10-13, P54 as merged, #70: `use()` returns its problems; with a wrong mask or kernels of another class it still attaches them, so an unchecked result would run the native pass on the GDScript twin, and with no library it attaches nothing and returns its problem list; P26m, 2026-10-14). `WorldView` (with its `int64_t` accessors) and the internal-pointer fetch of `world_access.h` (`ptr`/`ptrw`, ADR 0013 §2 steps 1-3) land here (P26l docs, 2026-10-13: moved from P54, #70, because a function lands with the kernel that calls it). P30n also settles which check its kernel runs on entry: ADR 0013 §2's accepted text says "The kernel calls it on entry", and `check_world` checks the phase's whole list on each call (FLOW about 8.7 µs on the arena, P54, #70); the question is open (ADR 0013 §2's status note of 2026-10-13). **As merged** (P26m, 2026-10-14; P30n #74, P30n2 #75): the split was taken at the lockstep, not at the named line above (`FlowCache.recount` and `advance` with the `FlowCache` cases), which could not be built: `SimKernels.flow` is `FlowCache.run`, so the recount and the slot pick are in C++ for any bound call, and ADR 0013 §2 forbids test-only entries. **Part 1** (#74, 1,089 changed lines: 986+, 103−, about 460 of C++ and about 600 of module tests; accepted by Michael by merging it on 2026-10-14) holds the whole kernel (`FlowCache.run` as `SimKernels.flow` in `bind/kernel_flow.cpp`, the rules in `core/flow.h` and `core/flow.cpp`, `core/array_view.h`), the dispatch in `FlowCache.run` (its first line tests the bit `FlowCache.PHASE_FLOW`, a stand-in for `SimProbe.Phase.FLOW` until P33; P33 (#77) removed it, and the line now tests `SimProbe.Phase.FLOW`, P26n docs), the write cases in `test_kernel_abi.gd` (each also on a worker `Thread`), the dispatch case in `test_kernel_dispatch.gd`, and `test_flow_field` and `test_flow_cache` under both implementations (with `tests/sim/support/flow_rig.gd` and `state_diff.gd`). **Part 2** (P30n2, #75, 280 changed lines, tests only) holds `tests/sim/native/test_flow_lockstep.gd`: 900 arena ticks (12 keys over 8 slots, terrain changes at ticks 200, 450 and 700, odd budgets), the full state compared after every call, one run on changed rules, maps wider and taller than square, and crafted cases (keys the costs cannot integrate, a served slot re-keyed by hand, a diagonal and an orthogonal neighbour queued in scan order). The kernel runs `check_world(FLOW)` on every call (`access_.check(world, PHASE_FLOW)` in `kernel_flow.cpp`), and on a non-zero status raises one `push_error` naming the property and writes nothing. `WorldView` is realised as `ArrayView<T>` per array with `flow::Slot` as the aggregate (the contract row "Native kernels"). FLOW's checked list now holds the 26 names the kernel gets or sets (`max_rows`, `map_cols`, `map_rows` and `flow_cache_size` lost FLOW's bit). Measured on the arena (seed 42, 900 FLOW calls, g++ `-O2` debug build, shared VM; PR #74): the kernel is about 10x faster than the twin per FLOW call (0.025-0.027 against 0.24-0.26 ms) and about 15x on busy calls (339 of 900: 0.042-0.046 against 0.63-0.68 ms); an idle call costs more natively than in GDScript, because `check_world` runs on every call (row P38pn). Mutants: 44 of the C++, 42 killed and 2 equivalent; two survive the module tests alone and die only in the full-state lockstep (a cleaned-up `link_prev`, and `through > old`). The review facts recorded with it (no decision) are under "Answers and follow-ups (P26m)" |

- (P26m, 2026-10-14, the port-size rule: P30n and P30n2 merged at 1,089 and 280 lines, and P37n,

- about 1,800 lines of ports now (P30n 340, P37n 430, P39n 260, P41n 380, P46n 410), about 900

  on the merged plan (`main` at `038392d` with P34c merged: lane A's next row is P31a, which waits
  for lane B's P30b and P32 with P32f; lane B's next is P30a). The model is `sched15.py` in the

  | The same, all three conditional rows fired, every split | 12 | 19 | 20 | 37 | 43 |

  So ADR 0013 moves the gate by **5 slots** if ADR 0014 is accepted too (30 to 35) and by
  **6** if it is accepted alone (29 to 35, P50a3 then charged to it), **8-9** with every named
  split (38; 9-10 if P41b is expected too), and by up to **5 more** if the three conditional rows fire with their splits (43;
  1 more without splits, because lane B bounds the gate until the splits lengthen lane A). If
  P41's twin edit applies (its row in the changes table), P41b is expected too and the two
  "every split" rows become 39 and 44 (9-10 slots). These are the numbers question 1 and ADR 0013's Consequences quote. Revision 7 counted 35/39 against
  30 on the older base `5311132`; the deltas hardly moved (+5/+6 and +9 then).

  The slip is not only lane A's: P40 moves by 2 (4 with splits), and lane A waits for it at P39b
  (P42a needs it too), so lane B is on the critical path up to P40 and lane A's ports after it.
  ADR 0013's Consequences explain why the conditional rows are likely to fire.

**Lanes on acceptance** (at most two at a time, as before; applied in revision 15; P26q docs,
2026-10-14: this limit is superseded; how many lanes run at once is Michael's call through his
start commands, three at a time in practice since P33 (lead decision, "Answers and follow-ups
(P26q docs)")):
- **Lane A:** (P29a2, P29b and P29c merged) P31a → P31b → P54 → P33 → P35a → P35b → P37a →
  P37a2 → P37b → P37n → (P37n2) → P39 → P39b → P39n → P41 → (P41g) → P41n → P42a → P42b →
  (P42n) → P48 → P46 → P46n → P49a → P51 → P51b → P51r → P46b → P49b → P52. A row in brackets is
  conditional (ADR 0013 §1); if P46's line fires a rule for a phase not yet ported, its row goes
  right after P46n. By the port-size rule (P26m, 2026-10-14), P37n3, P39n2, P41n2, (P42n2) and
  P46n2 are expected, and each takes the slot right after its port. If both FIRE and PROJECTILES
  fire, P42n's parts are P42n → P42n2 → P42n3 → P42n4 (P26n docs, "Split triggers").
- **Lane B:** (P34 and P34b merged, P34c about to merge) P30a → P30b → P32 → P32f → P53 → P55 →
  P55a → P55a2 (P55a's split, taken 2026-10-13, P26j) → P55b (P26k, 2026-10-13) → P56 → P36 → P36b (P36's split, taken 2026-10-13, P26l docs) → P38 → P38t → P38p → P30n → P30n2 (P30n's split, taken, P26m) → P38s → P38j → P40 → P43a → P43b → P54r → P38pn →
  P57 → P38w → P38w2 → P50a → P50a2 → P50a3 → (P50lp) → P45 → P44a → P44b → P47 → P47p → P50w →
  P50b. Only P30n sits before P38s, because P37n needs it (and P38j's skip of
  `NON_STATE_MEMBERS` needs P54, which P30n already waits for). P54r comes after P43b: its self-check tests need P37n (FLOW and INTEGRATE in the
  tick), and the first rows that need P54r are P38pn, P38w and P50a. P38pn and P57 follow it,
  because nothing waits for them before P50b (P57 guards later PRs, and P38pn only adds a
  report line).
- **Outside the lane order:** P26e (folded into P26b, revision 15), P26l (now), and ADR 0014's calibration build (P26n2). Named splits
  of rows already in the lanes (P36b, P30n2, P38s2, P38j2, P38w1b, P38w3) keep their slot right after
  their PR, as before.
- **If lane B runs behind.** P54 (lane A, after P31b) needs P53 (lane B, after P32f). If P53 has
  not merged when lane A reaches P54, lane A goes on with P33 and the rows after it and takes P54
  as soon as P53 merges: nothing in lane A needs P54 before P37n. P30n and P54r in lane B wait
  for P54 either way.
- **The split rule stays:** a named split takes the slot right after its PR, and "Px" in a
  Depends cell means all of its parts.
- **Why this order works.** Lane B has slack before P38, because P38 waits for P35b; P53-P56 go
  there, and they use most of it (P38 moves by 1-2 slots in the model above). P54 touches
  `World.gd`, so it sits in lane A. P38p keeps no native dependency, so P37a2 (which waits for
  P38p) does not wait for native infrastructure. Between P38p and P40 lane B carries only what
  lane A needs (P30n for P37n); everything else in lane B, P54r included, waits until after
  P43b, because P39b and P42a wait for P40.
- **Two gameplay-dev threads.** Lane B's one port, P30n, merges before lane A's first, P37n, which
  needs it, so two port PRs are open together only when a conditional row or a later phase's port
  overlaps another. Their files are then disjoint (one `bind/kernel_<phase>.cpp` each) except the shared lists:
  one line each in `bind/kernel_list.h` and in `sim_impls.gd`'s `EXPECTED_PHASES`, the names
  their modules add to `bind/world_access.h`'s get and set lists (ADR 0013 §2), and their
  dispatch cases in `test_kernel_abi.gd`. Those are the second expected rebase conflict, next to
  the `Sim.step` call line: the second PR rebases onto `main`. `FlowCache.gd` joins them only if
  P37b opens before P30n has merged (the P37b row of the changes table).

**Other sections on acceptance** (applied in revision 15):
- **Test plan:**
  - a "Native kernels" bullet: one lockstep test per kernel comparing the full state after every
    call (and once on non-default rules), crafted tie, cap, division and opposite-corner cases,
    the module tests under both implementations through `sim_impls.gd` (with the non-vacuity
    assert), the dispatch test with a spy, golden hashes under both on x86_64 (g++ and clang) and
    arm64 (clang), the boundary tests (`check_world` and names in P54, over the five phases'
    lists; the in-place write, fork, scalar and no-op cases in P30n through the FLOW kernel), the
    on-device self-check's match and mutated-kernel cases (P54r), the `impl` verdict and §15's
    summary keys on injected samples (P50a), the C++ scan (P56), the sanitizer job (P57),
    `check-native-tests.sh`'s discovery and floor on fixture trees and `results.xml` files (P55)
    and the fresh-clone import canary (P53);
  - every `core/` function is run by a bound kernel's tests in the PR that adds it (ADR 0013 §2);
    there are no test-only bound entries;
  - every one of them reruns on any pin change.
- **Risks:**
  - risk 1: its sim part (the sim's CPU time on the worker) is covered by ADR 0013. Its
    main-thread part stays (and ADR 0014 changes how it is measured, if accepted);
  - risk 4 gains the C++ compilers (g++, clang, NDK clang; Apple clang later);
  - new risks:
    - dual maintenance of the hot phases;
    - the toolchain in every session (SCons 4.9.1, g++, binutils, the pinned `clang-format`);
    - reliance on engine internals (the in-place getter, `extension_list.cfg`);
    - undefined behaviour in C++ written by a Sonnet agent (mitigations: sanitizer, mutants,
      lead review, `-fwrapv`, the widening rule);
    - the Android library: page size (16 KB), the libc++ ABI against the template's
      `libc++_shared.so` (P55a2's checks, rerun on every bump).
- **Out of scope:**
  - "GDExtension (C++) for hot sim phases" becomes "C++ outside `native/sim_kernels/` or outside
    the phases of ADR 0013 §1";
  - also out of scope: web on C++ (dlink, ADR 0013 §7 option B) and iOS builds.
- **Gate procedure:**
  - the APK and web hash comparison becomes a cross-implementation check;
  - the "GDExtension ADR" sentence in the miss paragraph is replaced by ADR 0013 §15's port
    trigger and its engine-side watch line.

### P50a's parts on acceptance (either ADR)
The two ADRs add about 180 lines to the PerfHud PRs (15 of them in P50a3), more than the room left in P50a and P50a2
(base: P50a about 350 after its expected split, P50a2 about 380 with what that split moves).
So on acceptance of either ADR, P50a2's named split P50a3 (copy report and "Run again") is the
expected outcome too, and the lines go here:

| Part | Base | ADR 0013 | ADR 0014 | Both accepted |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P50a (samples, stats, verdicts) | 350 | - | +40: pacing verdicts, burst and ref samples, normalised verdicts | about 390 |
| P50a2 (card, golden, `INVALID`; Depends + P54r with ADR 0013) | 380, minus about 115 to P50a3 | +70 (`impl` verdict, self-check call, §15's keys) and +15 (card rows) | +20 (§2's `INVALID` rules) and +20 (card rows) | about 390 |
| P50a3 (copy report, "Run again", every copy-report key) | about 115 | - | +15: "Run again" re-runs the burst | about 130 |

With only ADR 0013, P50a2 is about 350; with only ADR 0014, about 305; P50a3 is needed either
way, because without it P50a2 would be 465 or 420. "P50a" in the ADRs means these parts
together, like any "Px" in a Depends cell. P50a3 takes one lane-B slot before P50b: in the slot
model ("the slip" above) the gate is ready at 35 with ADR 0013 (38 with every split, lane A
bounding it), at 30 with ADR 0014 alone, and at 29 with neither. So question 3 quotes about 1
slot for ADR 0014 and question 1 about 5 for ADR 0013 (6 if ADR 0014 is rejected, because P50a3
is then charged to ADR 0013 alone).

### P27f under each outcome (outcome: ADR 0013 accepted, P27f withdrawn)
| Outcome | P27f |
| --- | --- |
| Until Michael decides on ADR 0013 | Held. `main` keeps P27b's 512, and nothing waits |
| ADR 0013 accepted | Withdrawn. 512 stays, by ADR 0012 §5 with the C++ slice and the C++ job. It holds once the §1 ports have merged (P49a's Depends), before any gate judges it; with only FLOW ported the GDScript phases would still miss the headroom half |
| ADR 0013 rejected | Ships 64 by ADR 0012 §5's headroom rule (worker job p95 19.75-20.10 ms, above 11.6 ms), with the early-warning finding of risk 1. The arena opening's four fields take about 18 s, and units drive straight meanwhile |

### If an ADR is rejected (not taken: both were accepted)
- **ADR 0013 no:**
  - the plan above stays as it is (with revision 14), and P27f ships 64;
  - ADR 0012 §7's trigger stays;
  - P26l still lands;
  - the lead tells Michael now that the measured mock alone misses `sim_p95_ms` (by 42-44 % on
    its tick p95, 48-51 % on the gated job p95), so the gate is expected to fail on sim time.
- **ADR 0014 no:**
  - ADR 0012 §4's on-time and raw-ms verdicts stay;
  - the lead tells Michael now that the measured runs already trip them: raw other-frame work
    was 6.04-6.93 ms against 5.6 with the tick on the worker; the GDScript worker (mode 4)
    passed the on-time rule in both sessions with the same work (95.0 % and 98.2 % against 95,
    the first exactly on the line), while the C++ worker (mode 5), whose worker is almost idle,
    missed it at 93.6 %. So the verdict may flip between runs, and less sim work can fail where
    more passed. (Mode 1's 90.5-91.6 % had the tick in the frame, which is different work.)

### Open questions for Michael (ADR 0013/0014)
Answered on 2026-10-12 ("1 ja, 2 A, 3 ja, 4 ja"; see "Answers (Michael, ADR 0013/0014)" below).
The questions are kept as they were asked. The lead asked them in Danish; each one was answered
with yes or no, or with a short choice:
1. Accept ADR 0013 (C++ for the measured hot phases, GDScript twin kept, with its CLAUDE.md
   lines and session tools)? Cost: about 5 PR slots before the gate (up to 10 with every split
   and 5 more if the conditional rows fire; "the slip"), about 4,000 lines, and every later
   rule change in a hot phase edits both versions. Until you answer, P27f is held (`main` keeps
   512; ADR 0012 gives 64). Yes keeps 512; no ships 64 (the arena's opening fields take about
   18 s instead of 2.3 s). **Recommended: yes.**
2. Web preview: keep it on the GDScript twins (A), or move it to C++ through the dlink template
   after a spike (B)? A costs no PR slot; the preview hitches at 150 units (an estimate), and the
   gate uses the APK. B costs a spike (P26w) and a new ADR first, then a third ABI in CI and
   about 1.3 MB more download. **Recommended: A.**
3. Accept ADR 0014 (pacing by dropped frames, main-thread CPU on clock-normalised time, same
   limits) plus a 5-minute calibration run? It costs the gate about 1 PR slot (P50a3, "P50a's
   parts on acceptance"), plus one conditional row (P50lp) and a second run per gate only if
   the calibration picks the load-pinned fallback. P50a waits for the run's recorded outcome.
   **Recommended: yes, and the run.**
4. The agent-file changes of ADR 0013 §13 (gameplay-dev, release-engineer, qa-engineer)?
   **Recommended: yes**, if question 1 is yes: a no blocks P53 and every later C++ PR, because no
   agent may then write `native/sim_kernels/src/`.

## Answers (Michael, ADR 0013/0014)
Michael answered in chat on 2026-10-12, verbatim: "1 ja, 2 A, 3 ja, 4 ja". ADRs 0013 and 0014
are accepted; each acceptance note records the answers. Plan revision 15 (P26b) applies them.
1. **Yes: ADR 0013 is accepted** (C++ kernels for the measured hot phases, the GDScript twin
   kept, with its CLAUDE.md lines and the session tools). Consequences:
   - the amendment's rows are live: P53, P55, P55a, P56 and P30n in lane B before P38s, P54 in
     lane A after P31b, the ports P37n, P39n, P41n and P46n in lane A, P54r, P38pn and P57 in
     lane B after P43b, and the conditional P37n2, P41g and P42n if ADR 0013 §1's rule fires;
   - P27f is withdrawn, and P27b's 512 stays;
   - the GDScript twin rule binds every row that edits a ported module (P41, P46b and later);
   - `CLAUDE.md` changed in P26b (Stack, Tests and Format/lint, Layout, Commands, rule 1); the
     lead adds the `clang-format` version number after P53 in a one-line follow-up (done in
     P26i, 2026-10-13: 20.1.8);
   - after P53 merges, Michael re-pastes `scripts/cloud/setup.sh` into the cloud environment's
     settings (ADR 0013 §8);
   - `-fwrapv` stays in every production build until Michael decides otherwise; P53 measures its
     cost, and above 5 % the lead brings the number to him with a recommendation;
   - the gate becomes ready at slot 35 instead of 30 (38-39 with every named split, up to 44 if
     every conditional row fires with its splits).
2. **A: the web preview stays on the GDScript twins.** Consequences: the plain `web_nothreads`
   template and no emsdk; the Web preset excludes `native/*` and `export-web.sh` fails on a
   packed `.gdextension` (P53); the preview runs the tick in the frame at GDScript speed and may
   hitch at 150 units (an estimate), while the gate uses the APK; its hashes check the twins
   against the C++ APK. Option B (dlink) stays recorded in ADR 0013 §7; it needs the P26w spike
   and a new ADR after the Phase 2 gate, and no PR works on it before then.
3. **Yes: ADR 0014 is accepted, and Michael does the calibration run.** Consequences:
   - the lead builds P26n2 on the spike branch (about 100 GDScript lines, never merged); Michael
     runs modes 1, 2, 4 and 5 for 60 s each on his Pixel 9a (about 5 minutes, battery saver off,
     not charging) and pastes the reports; the lead records C1-C5 and the outcome in ADR 0014's
     acceptance note before lane B reaches P50a, whose Depends cell waits for it. Done
     2026-10-13 (P26g): C1 and C2 hold, C3 and C4 hold on the per-frame method only, so the
     per-frame method is the formula P50a implements;
   - P50a3 is a row (the expected split of P50a2); P50lp and the second gate run exist only if
     the calibration picks the load-pinned fallback. Not taken (calibration 2026-10-13): no
     P50lp, no `pin_busy_pct`, no second gate run;
   - the citations of ADR 0014's "Source facts not re-run" are recorded in its acceptance note
     (Godot 4.7.2's Android frame loop, schedutil, Perfetto, the Android docs); Context needed no
     correction.
4. **Yes: the agent-file changes of ADR 0013 §13.** `gameplay-dev.md`, `release-engineer.md` and
   `qa-engineer.md` changed in P26b. Consequences: P53 and every later C++ PR are unblocked;
   release-engineer writes P53's skeleton under named exception 7, and gameplay-dev owns it from

## Lead decisions and answers (P26i)
Recorded 2026-10-13 in P26i, a docs PR after the reviews of P30b (#58), P53 (#59) and P53b (#60).
No plan revision number: the rows named below carry dated P26i notes.

**Michael's answer (NDK).** Michael answered in chat on 2026-10-13, verbatim: **"NDK r29"**. The
kernels are built with NDK 29.0.14206865, the NDK Godot 4.7.2's Android template is built with
(its `libc++_shared.so` is LLVM 21 / Android clang r563880c, `docs/licenses/llvm-libc++/README.md`),
not 28.1.13356709. ADR 0013 Context and §8 carry dated status notes. P55a applies it
(`NDK_VERSION` in `build-native.sh`, `install-ndk.sh`), its libc++ symbol check stays, and
building with r29 is P55a's to prove in CI; godot-cpp's own default at the pin stays r28 (row
P55a).

**Decisions of the lead** (2026-10-13, after the P30b review; ADR 0008 §3 carries status notes):
1. **Flow invalidation.** `World.set_terrain()` calls `flow.invalidate(map_version)` right after it
   bumps `map_version`, once the World is prepared. `FlowCache.run` (P30n) does not call
   `invalidate()`. Reason: `COMMANDS` run before `FLOW`, so an `acquire()` under the new version
   can happen before any `FLOW` phase; invalidating at the change keeps one slot per key. P31a
   implements the call (rows: the contract's `World` and `FlowField`, `FlowCache` rows, P31a,
   P30n, P37b).
2. **Provisional references (ADR 0008 §3).** `acquire()` counts the slot it hands out as
   referenced until the next `recount()`, so two new keys in one tick never share a slot; P30b
   merged it. ADR 0008 §3's "integrating allocates nothing after the first field" became
   "`configure()` allocates every slot's arrays; nothing allocates after it", already the
   revision-15 rule (ADR 0008's status note of 2026-10-12, ADR 0013 §2).

**Follow-ups applied** (no new decision):
- P30b review: the P30b row's Files line (`FlowField.gd`, `test_flow_field.gd`); the checked names
  of `FlowCache` and its slots in rows P30n, P37n and P54, and `FlowField.begin()` with
  `reserve()`'s clear of `complete` in the behaviour P30n ports; `FlowCache`'s `HASHED` and
  `UNHASHED` lists in row P31b.
- P53 and P53b reviews: ADR 0013's status notes (the `-isystem` flags in `CCFLAGS` because godot-cpp
  keeps its include directories in `CPPPATH` and leaves `CPPEXTPATH` empty, which SCons 4.9.1
  would not expand anyway; `-DNDEBUG` on both targets; `OS` and `RefCounted` in the build
  profile and the 23-34 s cold build; `-Wsign-conversion` and the plain `-I` counts; the
  recovery-mode and fresh-clone measurements; the `-fwrapv` cost; `clang-format` 20.1.8); the
  compiler and optimisation pass-through in rows P55a, P51b and P57; the six-library
  `.gdextension` in row P55a.
- `CLAUDE.md`: the `clang-format` version (20.1.8, pinned in `scripts/ci/check-cpp-format.sh`),
  the one-line follow-up ADR 0013 §14 promised after P53.

  unchanged.
- #35 and #41 closed: section P26s and rows P26s and P26n2 (both tables); ADR 0013's status note
  in Context.

**Facts from the P30n reviews** (recorded, no decision):
- **`FlowCache.run` writes `stats[FLOW_CELLS] += settled`.** The twin adds what
  `world.flow.advance(world.move_cost, budget)` returns to `world.stats[World.Stat.FLOW_CELLS]`;
  the kernel adds its `settled` once after its loop (`STAT_FLOW_CELLS`, a `constexpr` mirror of
  `World.Stat.FLOW_CELLS`, in `bind/kernel_flow.cpp`).
- **A deliberate exception to ADR 0013 §2's "each touched slot's arrays and scalars once".** The
  kernel re-reads `pending` of every `ACTIVE` slot on each pick: `flow::oldest_request` calls
  `pending_of(slot)`, which reads the slot's `pending` through `Object::get`
  (`access.int_in(WorldAccess::slot_in(fields, slot), ID_SLOT_pending)`), so the reads per pick
  are bounded by `flow_cache_size`, never per row or cell. Reading each `pending` once would need
  a fixed buffer of slots in C++, so a capacity literal, which `CLAUDE.md` rule 1 forbids
  (kernels read every rule value from the World, never from a C++ literal; ADR 0013 §6 also
  forbids heap allocation in a kernel). It stays as it is, recorded as a deliberate exception;
  ADR 0013 §2's status note and the skill's FLOW line point here.
- **`test_flow_cache`'s "a key the costs cannot integrate" case stays GDScript-only.**
  `test_a_key_the_costs_cannot_integrate_completes_at_once_and_does_not_stall_advance` calls a
  `FlowCache` with `move_cost` of two classes (`_costs().slice(0, 2 * CELLS)`), and `check_world`
  refuses that World (the `move_cost` length rule is three classes of `map_cols` x `map_rows`).
  The kernel's side of the key rule is `test_flow_lockstep.gd`'s
  `test_keys_the_costs_cannot_integrate_match` (a goal off the map, a class of 3 and of -1, a
  goal of -1).
- **`world_access.h`'s `check()` still reads four names outside FLOW's list.** Since P30n,
  `max_rows`, `map_cols`, `map_rows` and `flow_cache_size` no longer carry FLOW's bit (FLOW's list
  holds the 26 names the kernel gets or sets), but `check_world(FLOW)` still reads them through
  FLOW's length rules: `unit_id`'s `ROWS_CAP` (`max_rows`), `move_cost`'s `COSTS` (`map_cols`,
  `map_rows`) and the `SLOTS` rule of `fields`, the cache arrays and the slot loop
  (`flow_cache_size`).
- **"`FlowCache.run` never calls `invalidate()`" is pinned by a test.** `test_flow_cache.gd`'s
  `test_run_never_invalidates_a_slot_of_an_older_map_version` (added in #74, under both
  implementations; its doc comment names the rule and P26i) moves `map_version` on by hand, runs
  `FlowCache.run` through `flow_rig.gd`, and asserts that both slots stay `ACTIVE` with
  `key_version` 0, `ref_count`, `requests` and the served field unchanged; `invalidate(3)` on the
  same state gives `FREE` and `WAITING`, so a `run` that called `invalidate()` with the World's
  version fails it. (The P26m brief called the rule "only pinned incidentally" and asked for a
  follow-up test; the P26m review found this test, so no follow-up test is planned.)

## Answers and follow-ups (P26o docs)
Recorded 2026-10-14 in "P26o docs", a docs PR after the merges of P35a (#81), P35b (#82), P26n
docs (#80) and P56c (#83), checked against `main` at `12c66db`. No plan revision number: the rows
named below carry dated notes. P38 and P37a are in flight in parallel; this PR records nothing of
their outcome. Every size below is from `git diff --numstat` of the merged commit, not from the PR
text.

**The lead's decisions** (given in the P26o brief; no other decision is made here):
1. **P38's timing, list and sequences** (given to P38, which is in flight): `last_tick_usec` is
   `Sim.step` only, measured by a `game/core` forwarding probe (from its `begin()` to the last
   mark) that the runner always passes to `loop.step`; the runner clears its queued list after
   every step; sequences start at 0, so four runner assertions change. Recorded in the contract
   row `MatchRunner` v1, rows and sections P38 and P38j.
2. **P37b's checkpoint tick:** inside `Sim.step` the checkpoint tick is `world.tick + 1`
   (`Sim.step` counts the tick last, after every phase including the FLOW line P37b adds;
   `MatchLoop` hashes after it on the new tick), so "no
   flow slice on checkpoint ticks" is a budget of 0 when
   `(world.tick + 1) % Replay.CHECKPOINT_PERIOD == 0`. P37b's test steps through `MatchLoop` and
   asserts `stats[FLOW_CELLS] == 0` exactly on the steps after which
   `loop.replay.checkpoint_ticks` grows. Recorded in the contract row `Sim`, row and section P37b,
   and ADR 0012 §5's status note.
3. **P56c's rule:** an inner class extends only an engine class (`ClassDB` knows it) or nothing.
   It is structural, because two review rounds of scanning the body found 16 leaking
   self-reference shapes (the lead's count), and every one of them needed a script base.
   **Open item** (a lead decision): a top-level file whose own base is a script and that names
   itself at run time leaks the same way, and the rule does not cover it. Until a second case
   appears, a file with a script base must not name itself at run time; P38's forwarding probe
   and P38t's `TimingProbe` (both `extends SimProbe`) are the first such files. The backstop is
   `verify.sh`'s scan of the log (`^ERROR:` catches the leak's "resources still in use at exit"
   lines) in the suite order `run-tests.sh` now fixes. No list of test-harness rules exists in the
   rts-sim-conventions skill or the docs next to `CLAUDE.md`, so the rule and this item are
   recorded here and in row P56c only (qa-engineer's memory holds the rule since P56c);
   `CLAUDE.md` is unchanged. Row P38t, the `TimingProbe` contract row and sections P38 and P38t
   point here.

**Open question for the lead** (no decision taken here; answered by the lead on 2026-10-14, P26p
docs: the scan includes `game/core/StepProbe.gd`): from P38j on, the forwarding probe runs
on the worker inside `TickJob.run()`, but P38j's worker-file scan names only `TickJob.gd`,
`MatchSnapshot.gd` and `TimingProbe.gd` (section P38j, ADR 0012 §2). Whether that scan also
covers the forwarding probe's file is open.

**Follow-ups applied** (no new decision):
- P35a (#81) as merged: the contract row `Command` v1 (`INT32_MIN`, `INT32_MAX`,
  `ENCODED_HEADER` 7, `ENCODED_MIN` 9; the int32 saturation; `decode`'s malformed list, the

- P35b (#82) as merged: the contract rows `Replay` (`KINDS`, the wire-format walk, the null

## Answers and follow-ups (P26r docs)
Recorded 2026-10-14 in "P26r docs", a docs PR after the merges of P38p (#92, `2354038`), P38j
(#91, `e84096f`) and P26q docs (#90, `844b0bc`), checked against `main` at `844b0bc`. No plan
revision number: the rows named below carry dated notes. P37a2, P40 and P57 are in flight in
parallel; this PR records nothing of their outcome. Every size below is from `git diff --numstat`
of the merged commit.

**The lead's decision** (given in the P26r brief; no other decision is made here):
1. **P38w's `WORKER`-mode tests** (lead decision 2026-10-14; recorded, open as worded): the lead
   asked for two cases that kill two mutants P38j's final check left as equivalent in `SYNC`
   (`ticked.emit(world.tick)`, and `_publish` reading `loop.last_hash`): `ticked(n)` arriving
   with `snapshot.tick == n` while the World is already one tick ahead, and a main-thread read
   of `loop.last_hash` while a job is `RUNNING` going through the guarded getter. Note (P26r
   fix, 2026-10-14): under ADR 0012 §1's accepted order (publish, emit, then drain and post),
   the World inside `ticked` equals the published snapshot, and `_publish` never runs while a
   job is `RUNNING`, so as worded the first case cannot happen and neither case separates its
   mutant from the correct code. Open for the lead before P38w opens: restate the cases with an
   observation point consistent with ADR 0012 §1 or another kill mechanism, or accept both
   mutants as equivalent. No ADR text changes. Recorded in section P38w's tests; a restated
   case that reads through the guarded getters lands with P38w1b (its split trigger). (P26s
   docs, 2026-10-14: closed by the lead, who accepts both mutants as equivalent in every mode;
   P38w carries no special case for them; "Answers and follow-ups (P26s docs)", decision 1.)

**Follow-ups recorded** (non-blocking, by the review-rounds rule of "Answers and follow-ups (P26q
docs)"; no row yet):
- A later qa-engineer PR pins `tests/perf/test_sim_perf.gd`'s `_make_report` mapping. On today's
  arena `units`, `alive_end`, `projectile_overflow` and `proj_peak` are 150, 150, 0 and 0 and the
  `World.stats` maxima are zeros in every run, so a report that takes `alive_end`, `units`,
  `projectile_overflow` or `proj_peak` from the wrong place, or `stats_max` from the seed-43 run,
  survives as a mutant. The same PR renames the loop variable `_sample` in `_time_reference()`,
  which shadows the `_sample` method.
- P42 or P49a kills the `proj_peak` and overflow paths on the real arena once projectiles exist.

**Follow-ups applied** (no new decision):
- P38j (#91) as merged: row P38j (823 at merge, 144 of them production; P38j2 not taken, also in
  the split list), section P38j ("As merged"), the contract rows `TickJob` and `MatchRunner` v1:
  P38j adds `post_usec` and `end_usec` (section P38w's Files line now says so; in `SYNC` the
  runner sets `job.post_usec` just before the inline run and `run()` sets `end_usec`, and P38w's
  `serve()` keeps that); `job_usec` is the run on the thread that ran it, from its start through

## Answers and follow-ups (P26s docs)
Recorded 2026-10-14 in "P26s docs", a docs PR after the merges of P37a2 (#94, `499ff4e`), P40
(#95, `7c2543f`), P57 (#96, `65428aa`) and P26r docs (#93, `c59d596`), checked against `main` at
`c59d596`. No plan revision number: the rows named below carry dated notes. P37b, P43a and P43b
are in flight in parallel; this PR records nothing of their outcome (for P37b only the hand-offs
given to it). Every size below is from `git diff --numstat` of the merged commit.

**The lead's decisions** (given in the P26s brief; no other decision is made here):
1. **P38j's two surviving mutants are equivalent** (`ticked.emit(world.tick)`, and `_publish`
   reading `loop.last_hash`), in every mode, under ADR 0012 §1's order (publish, emit `ticked`,
   then drain and post): inside `ticked` the World equals the published snapshot, and `_publish`
   never runs while a job is `RUNNING`. P38w carries no special case for them. This closes the
   open item of "Answers and follow-ups (P26r docs)" and section P38w's tests bullet (dated
   notes in both, and in row P38w and P38w's split trigger). No ADR text changes, and ADR 0012,
   which does not name the mutants, needs no pointer.
2. **P57b** (a new row; release-engineer, its dump-step part qa-engineer; about 40 lines;
   depends on P57 and P37b): (a) widen the sanitizer job's path filter (`PATTERN` of the
   `sanitizer-changes` job, today `^(native/|game/sim/|data/|tests/sim/native/)`) to the files
   that drive it: `scripts/build-native.sh`, `scripts/ci/run-sanitizer-harness.sh`,
   `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `tests/sim/support/**`. `ci.yml`'s comment on that job names
   this gap ("P26r decides whether to widen PATTERN"; P26r did not, so the lead decides here). (b) Remove `dump_states.gd`'s FLOW stand-in (it drives the
   flow cache itself the way `test_flow_lockstep.gd` does, because `Sim.step` does not step FLOW
   yet) once `Sim.step` steps FLOW (P37b). The row stands beside P57 in lane B, outside the
   lane order's arrow list (the Lane B paragraph): no row waits for it, so P38w does not wait for
   P57b.
3. **For P37n** (gameplay-dev's request, accepted by the lead): `bind/kernel_flow.cpp`'s
   per-call loop moves into `core/flow.h` as `flow::run`, so the bind layer and the sanitizer
   harness share it (today `native/sim_kernels/sanitizer/flow_cases.cpp`'s `call()` mirrors
   `SimKernels::flow` loop for loop). Each later kernel (P37n, P39n, P41n, P42n, P46n and any
   conditional port) lands its `core/` entry point the same way and adds to the harness one cases
   file, one pointer in `KERNELS` (`native/sim_kernels/sanitizer/main.cpp`), its `extern` line
   in `cases.h` and its records in the dump step. Recorded here once; row P37n points here.

**Follow-ups for a later qa-engineer PR** (non-blocking, by the review-rounds rule of "Answers and
follow-ups (P26q docs)"; no row yet):
- From P38p: pin `tests/perf/test_sim_perf.gd`'s `_make_report` mapping and rename the loop
  variable `_sample` in `_time_reference()`, as already recorded in "Answers and follow-ups (P26r
  docs)".
- From P37a2: a speed-0 type with a hand-set order standing on its goal gets intent 0 and logs
  no engine error (no case today); `stats[LINE_CHECKS]` is added with `+=` (`Movement.run` adds
  its count after the loop), which no case shows when something else counts in the same tick.
- From P40: the half-step turn threshold `4 * moved >= speed * speed` in `UnitLayer._update_rigs`
  (the survivor of P40's final mutation run, a Phase 1 line); an optional cross-file pin of

- From P57: the runner's refusal check (the harness's exit code 2 and its "does not know" text

  the plain build's `-fwrapv`, which no test checks (the ELF checks prove only that the sanitized

  read like counts.
